# 后端子系统地图（12 个并行 reader 的结构化输出）
原始数据：[raw-maps.json](raw-maps.json)。以下按子系统汇总，只保留实施时要用的部分。

---

## SQLite schema and migrations (src-tauri/src/db.rs + its only consumer library_commands.rs)
**数据流**：Location and lifecycle. One DB file: <app_data_dir>/authoring_hub.db (db.rs:24,27-29; app_data_dir at lib.rs:358). There is no pool and no long-lived Connection anywhere — every operation calls db::open_connection(root), which (1) attempts the legacy library.db -> authoring_hub.db rename, (2) Connection::open (creating the file if absent), (3) execute_batch of journal_mode=WAL + foreign_keys=ON + busy_timeout=5000, (4) execute_batch(SCHEMA_SQL). So schema convergence is lazy and unconditional: every single DB touch re-runs all 8 CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS + 10 CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS statements. There is NO PRAGMA user_version (grep over src-tauri/src returns zero hits), NO schema_version table (the identically-named thing at db.rs:134 is a *column* on library_item_revisions holding "ReadingAuthoringIRV1"/"WritingExamSourceV1"), and NO migration-history table. The only versioning primitive is the library_meta(key,value) table, which in production holds exactly one key: migration_done_v1.

Write path (reading). A Tauri command mutates an ImportJob -> job_store::update_job -> job_store::save_job (:33) writes jobs/<id>/job.json, then best-effort calls library_commands::upsert_reading_job (:37); errors only go to stderr. upsert_reading_job reads jobs/<id>/authoring-ir.json (falling back to the serialized job when the file is absent, and ABORTING the DB write entirely when the file exists but won't parse, library_commands.rs:95-101), builds an ExamRecord, calls db::upsert_exam (connection #1, writes `exams`), then upsert_library_item_from_exam (connection #2, writes library_items + a brand-new library_item_revisions row). Writing is the same shape via writing_store.rs:129. Both hooks run outside any shared transaction, so a crash between them forks the two tables.

Write path (V2 workspace) — the gap. The converged editor saves through apply_authoring_v2_patches. authoring_v2_commands.rs:123-168 loads the current authoring, applies patches, and persists via artifact_store::append_revision (file tree under jobs/<id>/authoring/revisions/). That file contains no `db::`, `job_store::`, `library_commands::` or `workflow_state::` reference at all, so a V2 edit never reaches SQLite. library_item_revisions.payload_json therefore only ever holds V1 authoring-ir (or raw job.json) content.

Metadata write path runs the other direction: update_library_exam_meta_core (library_commands.rs:227) reads the summary, calls db::update_exam_meta (UPDATE exams first — bails with Ok(None) if zero rows matched, db.rs:689 — then UPDATE library_items), then best-effort write_back_meta_to_source (:293) which mutates job.json/writing-job.json and re-triggers save_job, which re-triggers the double-write hook, which appends yet another revision row.

Delete path. delete_library_exam_core (:254) is soft: ensure_library_item_for_exam then soft_delete_library_item, leaving the exams row intact. But delete_job (job_commands.rs:142-156) and delete_writing_job (writing_store.rs:183-195) rm -rf the job dir and then HARD `DELETE FROM exams` — library_items and its revisions survive with deleted_at still NULL.

Read path. list/get/search/stats and the post-update read-back all funnel through the two generated SQL strings at db.rs:325-399, whose FROM clause is `exams e LEFT JOIN library_items li ON li.id = e.id WHERE li.deleted_at IS NULL`. Because `exams` drives the join, a library_items row with no matching exams row is invisible to every production query except list_trashed_items. list_trashed_items (db.rs:1013) is the sole library_items-only query. Reads reach the frontend as LibraryExamSummary/Detail (lib.rs:215-243) through the 8 commands registered at lib.rs:1467-1474, each a spawn_blocking shell around a *_core.

Bootstrap. lib.rs .setup() (:1408-1414) calls ensure_app_dirs then `let _ = library_commands::migrate_existing_into_library(&root);` — return value AND error both discarded. That function's own open_connection is what first creates the DB and schema on a fresh install.

Separate DB, do not conflate: publish/library.db (export_nas_library.rs:24) is a publish artifact with its own library_meta + reading_assets schema (:693-726), deleted and rebuilt from scratch on each publish (:733-737). It never migrates and shares only the table name.

### 关键签名
- `db_path` — `pub(crate) fn db_path(root: &Path) -> PathBuf`
  - root.join("authoring_hub.db"). DB_NAME const at :24, LEGACY_DB_NAME="library.db" at :22. root is always app.path().app_data_dir() (lib.rs:357-359).
- `migrate_db_file` — `pub(crate) fn migrate_db_file(root: &Path) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - The ONLY file-level migration: std::fs::rename(library.db -> authoring_hub.db) when the new name is absent. Idempotent; new file wins if both exist. Called from open_connection:53, so it runs on every open.
- `open_connection` — `pub(crate) fn open_connection(root: &Path) -> CommandResult<Connection>`
  - THE only way in. No pool, no AppState-held connection — a fresh transient rusqlite Connection per call. Does: migrate_db_file -> Connection::open -> execute_batch("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL; PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON; PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000;") (:60-65) -> ensure_schema (:66). Errors are redacted to the literal "open_db_failed" (:58) with the real path only on stderr. 15 call sites, all in library_commands.rs (:177,215,223,232,255,266,275,370,375,387) + db.rs:278,773.
- `ensure_schema` — `pub(crate) fn ensure_schema(conn: &Connection) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - Body is exactly `conn.execute_batch(SCHEMA_SQL)`. This IS the whole migration mechanism. Runs unconditionally on every open_connection.
- `SCHEMA_SQL` — `const SCHEMA_SQL: &str = r#" ... "#;`
  - Single raw-string DDL blob, entirely CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS / CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS. 8 tables: library_meta(:79), exams(:83), library_items(:108), library_item_revisions(:129), ingest_jobs(:143), source_assets(:158), publish_records(:170), job_artifact_index(:184). 10 indexes: :100,:101,:102,:103,:124,:125,:126,:140,:155,:181. One UNIQUE(library_item_id, revision_no) at :138. No views, no triggers, no AUTOINCREMENT. Real FK clauses exist only at :131, :172, :173.
- `set_meta` — `pub(crate) fn set_meta(conn: &Connection, key: &str, value: &str) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - UPSERT into library_meta. The entire versioning primitive available in this codebase.
- `get_meta` — `pub(crate) fn get_meta(conn: &Connection, key: &str) -> CommandResult<Option<String>>`
  - Exactly ONE production key exists: "migration_done_v1" (read library_commands.rs:388, written :487).
- `ExamRecord` — `pub(crate) struct ExamRecord { pub id: String, pub exam_id: Option<String>, pub title: String, pub subject: String, pub category: Option<String>, pub frequency: Option<String>, pub status: String, pub task_type: Option<String>, pub tags: Vec<String>, pub payload_json: String, pub source_hash: Option<String>, pub issue_errors: u32, pub issue_warnings: u32, pub created_at: String, pub updated_at: String }`
  - Flat row for legacy `exams`. status is the public enum draft|needs_review|ready|exported.
- `upsert_exam_conn` — `pub(crate) fn upsert_exam_conn(conn: &Connection, record: &ExamRecord) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - INSERT ... ON CONFLICT(id) DO UPDATE, created_at deliberately not overwritten. Accepts &Transaction via Deref — that is how migrate_existing_into_library reuses it (library_commands.rs:479).
- `upsert_exam` — `pub(crate) fn upsert_exam(root: &Path, record: &ExamRecord) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - open_connection + upsert_exam_conn. The root-taking convenience wrapper pattern that lets save hooks avoid holding a Connection.
- `upsert_library_item` — `pub(crate) fn upsert_library_item(conn: &Connection, record: &LibraryItemRecord) -> CommandResult<String>`
  - Returns the new revision id. Uses `conn.unchecked_transaction()` (:846) so it only needs &Connection — but that means it issues BEGIN DEFERRED and will FAIL if called while an outer transaction is open. Appends a NEW library_item_revisions row on every call (revision_no = MAX+1, :864) and points current_revision_id at it. record.task_type is silently dropped — see surprises.
- `LibraryItemRecord` — `pub(crate) struct LibraryItemRecord { pub id: String, pub subject: String, pub content_type: String, pub title: String, pub category: Option<String>, pub difficulty: Option<String>, pub status: String, pub task_type: Option<String>, pub tags: Vec<String>, pub source_asset_id: Option<String>, pub linked_ingest_job_id: Option<String>, pub created_at: String, pub updated_at: String, pub revision_payload_json: String, pub schema_version: String, pub created_from_job_id: Option<String>, pub change_reason: Option<String> }`
  - #[derive(Clone)]. status is the internal enum draft|review_required|ready|published|archived.

| 判定 | 计划说 | 代码实际 | 证据 |

| partly-wrong | Plan 11.2-M1: add library_items_v2, processing_jobs_v2, actionable_issues_v1, library_item_recovery_v1 while old exams/library_items/jobs stay readable (plan:2001-2004). | Additive is the ONLY thing this codebase can do, so M1 is nearly free — append to SCHEMA_SQL and existing installs get the tables at the next open_connection. But the plan's own DDL is not copy-pasteable: plan 4.3 (:385,:398) and 4.4 (:428,:435) all use bare `CREATE TABLE`, which throws on the second ensure_schema call. Every statement must be rewritten as CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS. Also the M1 list omits editor_journal_v1 (only in 4.4's SQL), and actionable_issues_v1 has NO DDL anywhere in the plan — only the Rust struct ActionableIssueV1 at plan:1481-1495 and bare mentions at plan:2003/:3173. The implementer must author that table themselves. | `src-tauri/src/db.rs:71-75 (ensure_schema runs SCHEMA_SQL every open), db.rs:77-191 (all DDL uses IF NOT EXISTS) vs plan lines 385, 398, 428, 435, 2003` |
| partly-wrong | Plan 11.2-M2: "迁移必须幂等，以 migration_version 标记" (plan:2024). | There is no migration_version anywhere. The existing marker is the boolean-ish library_meta row migration_done_v1 (read library_commands.rs:388, written :487). There is no PRAGMA user_version and no migrations table — grep for user_version across src-tauri/src returns nothing. A monotonic integer version does not exist and would have to be introduced. | `src-tauri/src/library_commands.rs:388 `if matches!(db::get_meta(&conn, "migration_done_v1")?, Some(_)) { return Ok(0); }` and :487 `db::set_meta(&tx, "migration_done_v1", "1")?;`` |
| confirmed | Plan 17.12: "删除 production query 对 legacy exams / new library_items 的 COALESCE 混合". | Confirmed and worse than a single query: two generated SQL strings, active_summary_source_sql (db.rs:325) and active_detail_source_sql (db.rs:362), are consumed at 6 call sites (db.rs:516, 555, 586, 606, 616, 700) covering list_exams, get_exam, search_exams, get_stats (x4 executions), and the post-update read-back. `exams` is the DRIVING table of the join, not the fallback, so library_items rows without an exams row are invisible. Removing the mixing is therefore not a cleanup — it changes which rows exist. source_hash, issue_errors and issue_warnings come from `exams` ONLY (db.rs:347-349) and have no column at all in library_items, so those three fields are lost the moment exams is dropped from the join. | `src-tauri/src/db.rs:356-358 `FROM exams e LEFT JOIN library_items li ON li.id = e.id WHERE li.deleted_at IS NULL`; consumed at db.rs:516, 555, 586, 606, 616, 700` |
| partly-wrong | Plan 11.1: the five parallel sources of truth include "jobs/<id>/authoring/revisions/* V2 内容" and "library_items + revisions 新 DB 内容", forking only transiently. | The fork is permanent, not transient. apply_authoring_v2_patches_core persists exclusively through artifact_store::append_revision; authoring_v2_commands.rs contains zero `db::`, `job_store::`, `library_commands::` or `workflow_state::` references. So the DB's library_item_revisions.payload_json holds V1 authoring-ir (or raw job.json) forever, and every V2 edit made by the already-shipped Phase 1-3 workspace is invisible to the DB. The frontend's claim that the workspace "saves through get_authoring_v2 / apply_authoring_v2_patches" is true, and that path is entirely file-based. | `src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs:149-156 (append_revision is the only persistence); grep of that file for db::/job_store::/library_commands::/workflow_state:: returns nothing; src-tauri/src/job_store.rs:37 is the only DB write trigger for reading content` |
| partly-wrong | Plan 11.2-M3/M4 and 17.13: the new UI stops calling legacy library CRUD, then the double-writes and legacy exams queries get deleted. | The already-shipped Phase 1-3 Library route still calls all of it. src/features/library/libraryStore.ts:55-66 fans out to listJobs() (a raw file scan of jobs/*/job.json, job_store.rs:58-84), listLibraryExams() (the COALESCE query), and listTrashedExams(), then merges by id. moveToTrash/restore (:105,:110) call deleteLibraryExam/restoreLibraryExam. So M4 is blocked by live frontend code, and there are currently THREE read sources on the converged route, not one. | `src/features/library/libraryStore.ts:55-66, :105, :110; commands registered at src-tauri/src/lib.rs:1467-1474` |

No library/ directory exists; lib.rs:44-87 declares flat modules, with only docx_ingest, ielts_grammar, pdf_ingest and schema as mod.rs-style directory submodules. There are zero .sql files and zero migrations/ directories in the repo. | ``ls -la src-tauri/src/db.rs` = 53620 bytes; src-tauri/src/lib.rs:44-87; `find . -name '*.sql'` and `find . -type d -name migrations` both empty` |

No assets/, no source-evidence/, no tmp/. Also cleanup.rs contains no DB references whatsoever, so plan 11.5's cleanup_on_startup has no existing DB-aware hook to extend. | `src-tauri/src/util.rs:63-78; src-tauri/src/db.rs:27-29; grep of src-tauri/src/cleanup.rs for db::/Connection/library_items/exams returns nothing` |
| confirmed | Plan 4.3: library_items_v2.modality CHECK (modality IN ('reading','listening','writing')). | Nothing blocks adding that table, but during the M1-M3 coexistence window a listening item can never be mirrored into the legacy tables: exams.subject CHECK is IN ('reading','writing') (db.rs:87) and library_items.subject CHECK is the same (db.rs:110). Since the production list query is exams-driven, a listening item created in v2 tables will be BOTH uninsertable into exams AND invisible in the Library list until the COALESCE query is replaced. There is no DELETE FROM library_item_revisions and no DELETE FROM library_items anywhere in db.rs; the only DELETEs are on `exams` (:766, :801). Revision rows and soft-deleted items grow without bound. | `src-tauri/src/db.rs:864, :887-896; full inventory of write statements in db.rs shows DELETE only at :766 and :801` |
| confirmed | Stated as verified by the orchestrator: library_items.id == job.job_id. | Confirmed at every construction site. Reading: ExamRecord.id = job.job_id (library_commands.rs:50); writing: id = job.job_id (:120); LibraryItemRecord.id = exam.id (:187). db.rs's own backfill path does the same (:460). Additionally linked_ingest_job_id and created_from_job_id are also set to that same id (library_commands.rs:197, :202; db.rs:474, :479), so those two columns carry no extra information today. | `src-tauri/src/library_commands.rs:50, :120, :187, :197, :202; src-tauri/src/db.rs:460, :474, :479` |
| already-done | Plan 11.2-M1 implies the Phase-1 six-table "formal schema" is a working foundation to build v2 on. | Four of those six tables are dead schema: ingest_jobs, source_assets, publish_records and job_artifact_index are created and indexed but never INSERTed, SELECTed or referenced anywhere in the codebase — grepping each name across db.rs finds only the CREATE statement, its index, and the module doc comment at db.rs:10. So processing_jobs_v2 is not replacing a working ingest_jobs; it is the first real job table. | `src-tauri/src/db.rs:143-190 create them; per-name grep across all of src-tauri/src finds no INSERT/SELECT for any of the four` |

### 改动点

| 风险 | 文件::符号 | 改什么 | 前置 |
|---|---|---|---|
| low | `src-tauri/src/db.rs::SCHEMA_SQL (const, lines 77-191)` | THE recipe for adding a table in this codebase, and it is one step: append `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS <name> (...);` plus `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ...;` to this raw string, before the closing "#. Nothing else is required — ensure_schema (:71) runs the whole blob from open_connection (:66) on every connection, so existing installs materialize the new tables the next time any DB call happens. Add all five (library_items_v2, processing_jobs_v2, actionable_issues_v1, library_item_recovery_v1, editor_journal_v1) here. Rewrite the plan's DDL: plan 4.3/4.4 use bare CREATE TABLE, which errors on the second ensure_schema. Author actionable_issues_v1 yourself from ActionableIssueV1 (plan:1481-1495) — the plan has no DDL for it. Follow the file's existing conventions: TEXT ISO8601 timestamps (never INTEGER epoch), TEXT PRIMARY KEY ids, *_json TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}' or '[]' for blobs, soft delete via a nullable deleted_at. | — |
| medium | `src-tauri/src/db.rs::open_connection / ensure_schema (lines 51-75)` | If you need anything CREATE-TABLE-IF-NOT-EXISTS cannot express — adding a column to an already-shipped v2 table, backfilling a NOT NULL, renaming — you must introduce the mechanism, because none exists: zero ALTER TABLE statements in the entire repo (rust, sql and ts), zero PRAGMA user_version, no migrations table. Add a `fn run_migrations(conn: &Connection) -> CommandResult<()>` invoked from db.rs:66 immediately after ensure_schema, gated on a library_meta key (e.g. schema_version_v2 read via get_meta/set_meta, which already accept &Connection and work inside a Transaction). Do this in the same commit that adds the v2 tables — retrofitting a version baseline after installs exist in the field is what forces guesswork later. Do NOT add PRAGMAs here without deciding about foreign_keys: it is currently ON (:62) in production and OFF in the unit-test harness. | — |
| low | `src-tauri/src/db.rs::module scope; new src-tauri/src/library/{mod.rs,schema.rs,repository.rs,migration.rs}` | Plan 17.12's split. Convention to copy: a directory submodule with mod.rs re-exporting `pub mod x;` (src-tauri/src/schema/mod.rs:1-5), declared as a bare `mod library;` in the alphabetical list at lib.rs:44-87 — it belongs immediately before `mod library_commands;` at lib.rs:66. Move SCHEMA_SQL to library/schema.rs and keep db.rs::ensure_schema delegating to it so the ~15 existing open_connection call sites keep working unchanged. Everything in db.rs is pub(crate), so cross-module moves need no visibility changes. New repository fns should keep the file's two-signature idiom: `fn foo(conn: &Connection, ...)` for composability inside transactions plus an optional `fn foo_at(root: &Path, ...)` wrapper that opens its own connection (pattern at db.rs:277-280 and :772-776). | — |
| medium | `src-tauri/src/db.rs::upsert_library_item (line 841, specifically `conn.unchecked_transaction()` at :846)` | Trap for any v2 repository write you call from inside a migration. This fn takes &Connection but issues its own BEGIN DEFERRED via unchecked_transaction, so calling it (or anything copying the idiom) while an outer transaction is open fails with "cannot start a transaction within a transaction". The existing migration sidesteps this by calling only upsert_exam_conn inside its tx (library_commands.rs:479) — note it deliberately does NOT write library_items there. New v2 repository writers must expose a tx-safe form (plain execute, no unchecked_transaction) if they are to be reused by migration.rs. Also note that upsert_library_item INSERTs library_items with current_revision_id pointing at a revision row that does not exist yet (:867-884, revision inserted at :887) — this only works because current_revision_id has NO FK clause (db.rs:117 is a comment, not a REFERENCES). Do not add a real FK there, and do not copy that ordering into v2 tables that do have FKs. | — |
| medium | `src-tauri/src/library_commands.rs::migrate_existing_into_library (line 386)` | Copy this as the M2 template into library/migration.rs, guarded by a NEW library_meta key (do not reuse migration_done_v1). The shape that matters: `let mut conn` because conn.transaction() (:470) needs &mut, unlike the unchecked_transaction used elsewhere; all filesystem IO done BEFORE opening the tx (:392-463, explicitly commented at :392-393); set_meta inside the tx (:487) so a rollback also un-sets the done flag and the next launch retries; failures collected into a `skipped` vec and logged rather than aborting (:465-467). Two hazards for M2 specifically: (a) this existing function calls prune_orphan_exams (:483), which DELETEs any exams row whose id is not backed by a jobs/*/job.json or writing-jobs/*/writing-job.json file — once v2-native items exist without job dirs, re-running anything with that pruning would delete their legacy mirror rows; (b) plan 11.2's M2 pseudocode wants prefer_in_order(current_v2_revision, authoring_v2_shadow, convert_v1_authoring), and only the third of those is reachable from the DB — V2 content lives solely in the file tree via artifact_store, so migration.rs must read jobs/<id>/authoring/revisions/* off disk, not from library_item_revisions. | — |
| low | `src-tauri/src/lib.rs::run() .setup() closure (lines 1408-1414)` | Register the new migration next to the existing `let _ = library_commands::migrate_existing_into_library(&root);` at :1412. Note that line discards both the count AND any error, so a failing migration is completely silent today — at minimum log the Err for the new one. Order matters: ensure_app_dirs (:1410) must precede any open_connection because Connection::open cannot create the parent directory. | — |
| high | `src-tauri/src/db.rs::active_summary_source_sql (line 325) and active_detail_source_sql (line 362)` | Plan 17.12's deletion target. When you repoint reads at library_items_v2, replacing these two functions is a behavioural change, not a refactor, for three reasons: exams is the join's DRIVING table so the row set shrinks/changes; source_hash, issue_errors and issue_warnings are read from `e` only (:347-349) and have no library_items counterpart, so LibraryExamSummary (lib.rs:215-237) loses three fields unless library_items_v2 gains columns for them; and the status CASE at :334-341 plus normalize_public_status (:309) are what keep the frontend's draft\|needs_review\|ready\|exported contract alive, which is incompatible with plan 11.3's LibraryItemStatusV2 (Processing/ActionRequired/Ready/Publishing/Published/Failed/Archived). Add a v2 read path alongside these rather than editing them in place until the frontend contract moves. | src/features/library/libraryStore.ts:60 still calls listLibraryExams, and src/pages/LibraryPage.tsx / LibraryExamDetail.tsx / Dashboard.tsx call list/search/get/stats. Plan 11.2-M4 cannot start until those move to the v2 commands. |
| high | `src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs::apply_authoring_v2_patches_core (line 123) and get_authoring_v2_core (line 54)` | The single highest-value convergence edit and the one the plan understates. These are the commands the shipped Phase 1-3 workspace saves through, and they persist ONLY to the file tree via artifact_store::append_revision (:149-156); the file has no db:: import at all. To make library_items_v2.canonical_ds_json authoritative, this is where the write must be added (or moved). The optimistic-concurrency contract already in place — base_revision compared against the loaded current_revision, erroring `revision_conflict:current={}:base={}` (:135-140) — maps directly onto the plan's current_edit_version, so reuse that error string rather than inventing one. Batch cap is 200 patches (:130-132). | — |
| high | `src-tauri/src/library_commands.rs::upsert_reading_job (line 92), upsert_writing_job (line 139), upsert_library_item_from_exam (line 172)` | Plan 11.2-M4's "删除 save_job -> upsert_reading_job". Call sites to cut: job_store.rs:37 and writing_store.rs:129. Before cutting, know what each hook silently absorbs: upsert_reading_job SKIPS the entire DB write when authoring-ir.json exists but fails to parse (:95-101) — deliberately, to avoid clobbering a good DB payload with job.json; and each hook opens TWO separate transient connections (upsert_exam at :104/:141 and upsert_library_item_from_exam at :177), so exams and library_items are never written in one transaction. upsert_library_item_from_exam also encodes two mislabelled mappings you should not carry into v2: source_asset_id := exam.source_hash (:196, a bare SHA-256 hex, not a source_assets.id) and linked_ingest_job_id := exam.id (:197). | src/features/library/libraryStore.ts merges listJobs() file-scan output into the Library rows, so job.json must keep being written until that merge is removed. |
| medium | `src-tauri/src/db.rs::update_exam_meta (line 644, guard at :689-691)` | Fix or bypass before any v2-only item can be edited. The function UPDATEs `exams` first and returns Ok(None) when zero rows matched, so update_library_item_meta (:692) is never reached and the Tauri command reports "not found" for any item that exists only in library_items(_v2). Every metadata edit today therefore requires a legacy exams row. Either invert the order or route v2 items through a new command. | — |
| medium | `src-tauri/src/db.rs::delete_exam (line 764) / delete_exam_by_id (line 772)` | Hard `DELETE FROM exams` that leaves library_items and all its revisions behind with deleted_at still NULL — invisible to every production read (exams drives the join) and absent from list_trashed_items (which filters deleted_at IS NOT NULL). Call sites: job_commands.rs:149 (delete_job) and writing_store.rs:189 (delete_writing_job). The codebase already acknowledges the resulting mess with restore_exam_from_library_item (:1001) and the test restore_rehydrates_legacy_exam_row_deleted_by_old_flow (library_commands.rs:689). When v2 tables land, add the corresponding v2 delete here or these two entry points will orphan v2 rows the same way. | — |
| low | `src-tauri/src/db.rs::mem_conn test helper (lines 1041-1045)` | Add `conn.execute_batch("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON;")` before ensure_schema, or every FK you write in the new v2 DDL is unenforced in tests and enforced in production. SQLite defaults foreign_keys OFF; production turns it ON at db.rs:62 but this helper does not. This matters immediately for the plan's `processing_jobs_v2.library_item_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES library_items_v2(id)` (plan:400): inserting a job before its item passes every unit test and fails on a user's machine. | — |
| low | `src-tauri/src/db.rs::LibraryItemRecord.task_type (line 825) + upsert_library_item INSERT column list (lines 868-882)` | Dead field: library_items has no task_type column (only exams does, db.rs:91), and the INSERT never binds record.task_type — it is silently dropped on every write even though three call sites populate it (library_commands.rs:194, db.rs:471, and the test at :1222). That silent drop is exactly why the read queries have to reconstruct task_type with `COALESCE(e.task_type, CASE WHEN subject='writing' THEN li.category END)` (db.rs:342-345) and why list_trashed_items fabricates it from category (:1016). Either add the column or delete the field; do not reproduce the workaround in v2. | — |
| medium | `src-tauri/src/db.rs::exams CHECK constraints (lines 87, 90) and library_items CHECK constraints (lines 110, 115)` | Hard blocker for incremental listening support. subject is CHECKed IN ('reading','writing') in both tables, so nothing with plan 4.3's modality='listening' can be double-written to the legacy tables. Since the production list query is exams-driven, a listening item in v2 tables is both uninsertable into exams and invisible in the Library. Sequence listening AFTER the read path moves off active_summary_source_sql, or ship it with the reads. Note SQLite cannot relax a CHECK via ALTER TABLE — widening the legacy constraint would need a full table rebuild, which is a mechanism this codebase does not have. | — |
| medium | `src-tauri/src/db.rs::library_item_revisions growth (INSERT at :887-896, revision_no at :864)` | Plan 4.4's bounded-recovery goal needs a pruning path that does not exist: db.rs contains no DELETE against library_item_revisions or library_items — the only DELETEs are on exams (:766, :801). Every save_job appends a revision, including the extra save_job that write_back_meta_to_source triggers after a metadata edit (library_commands.rs:339). If library_item_recovery_v1 (single row per item, PRIMARY KEY library_item_id) and editor_journal_v1 (AUTOINCREMENT, needs a keep-last-100 delete) are to replace this, write the pruning in the same change — and give editor_journal_v1 an index on (library_item_id, id) since the plan's DDL declares none. | — |
| low | `src-tauri/src/db.rs::to_library_item_status (:317) / normalize_public_status (:309) and the duplicate at library_commands.rs:160-168` | Three copies of the status mapping exist: two in db.rs and a third `fn to_library_item_status(exam_status: &str) -> &'static str` in library_commands.rs:160-168 which differs by having a `_ => "draft"` catch-all. A fourth mapping is inlined as SQL CASE in both source-SQL builders (db.rs:336-338, :373-375), and a fifth direction lives in library_status_to_reading/library_status_to_writing (library_commands.rs:346-364). Adding plan 11.3's LibraryItemStatusV2 without consolidating these first will produce a sixth. Consolidate into library/schema.rs as part of the split. | — |

### 现有测试方式
All tests are inline `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` — there is no src-tauri/tests/ directory and no integration-test crate. Package `ielts-author-studio`, lib `ielts_author_studio_lib` (src-tauri/Cargo.toml:1-10), rusqlite 0.31 with the `bundled` feature (:34), so tests need no system SQLite.

db.rs tests (module at :1037-1349, 12 tests). Two fixtures to copy verbatim:

    fn mem_conn() -> Connection {
        let conn = Connection::open_in_memory().unwrap();
        ensure_schema(&conn).unwrap();
        conn
    }

(db.rs:1041-1045 — note it omits PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON, so FKs are unenforced in every one of these tests) and `fn sample_record(id: &str, subject: &str, status: &str) -> ExamRecord` (:1047-1069) / `fn sample_library_item(id: &str, subject: &str, status: &str) -> LibraryItemRecord` (:1208-1237). Test names, which are the exact patterns to clone for v2: schema_is_idempotent (:1072 — calls ensure_schema twice; the direct guard against a bare CREATE TABLE slipping into SCHEMA_SQL, so add nothing to that constant without this passing), upsert_inserts_then_updates (:1079), list_filters_by_subject_and_status (:1099), search_matches_title_and_tags (:1131), stats_group_counts (:1143), update_meta_partial_and_delete (:1157), meta_get_set (:1180 — the template for a schema-version-key test), check_constraint_rejects_invalid_status_and_subject (:1190 — asserts CHECK violations surface as Err), library_item_upsert_creates_revisions (:1240 — asserts revision_no increments and current_revision_id follows), soft_delete_and_restore (:1278), active_queries_follow_soft_delete_and_normalize_statuses (:1296 — the one that pins the exams-driven join's visibility semantics; it will fail if you repoint reads at v2 tables, which is the signal that the change is real), migrate_db_file_renames_legacy (:1325 — uses std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("ielts-migtest-{}", uuid::Uuid::new_v4().simple()))).

library_commands.rs tests (module at :492-802, 6 tests) cover the full file+DB round trip and are where a new migration belongs. Fixtures: make_reading_appdata() (:501-543) builds a temp AppData root at std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("ielts-lib-test-{}", uuid::Uuid::new_v4().simple())) containing jobs/import-test-1/{job.json,authoring-ir.json} with a minimal ReadingAuthoringIRV1 payload (:526-536); make_writing_appdata() (:545-567); fn cleanup(root: &PathBuf) (:569-571) does fs::remove_dir_all. Tests: migrate_imports_reading_and_writing (:574 — asserts the return count is 2 AND that a second call returns 0, i.e. the migration_done_v1 idempotence assertion to duplicate for a new key), update_meta_writes_back_to_job_json_and_survives_resave (:598), delete_library_exam_moves_to_trash_and_restore_brings_it_back (:638), restore_rehydrates_legacy_exam_row_deleted_by_old_flow (:689 — deliberately simulates the hard-deleted-exams-row-with-surviving-item state), update_meta_keeps_cleaned_reading_jobs_cleaned (:713), migrate_prunes_orphan_db_rows (:754).

Notable coverage holes an implementer should not assume are covered: no test opens a real on-disk authoring_hub.db and asserts the schema (only in-memory), so a WAL/pragma-ordering regression would not be caught; no test exercises foreign_keys=ON at all; no test covers concurrent connections despite busy_timeout=5000 being the entire concurrency strategy; and nothing tests the double-write hooks' partial-failure path (exams written, library_items not).

Run with `cargo test -p ielts-author-studio db::` / `library_commands::` — but per the assignment I did not execute cargo, so I have not verified these pass on the current tree.

### 意外发现
- The migration mechanism is not a mechanism. There is no user_version, no schema_version table, no migrations directory, no .sql file, and zero ALTER TABLE statements anywhere in the repo. "Migration" = ensure_schema (db.rs:71) re-running one CREATE-TABLE-IF-NOT-EXISTS blob on every single connection open. Adding a TABLE is therefore trivially safe; adding a COLUMN to a table that has already shipped has no supported path at all.
- exams is the DRIVING table of the production join, not the fallback: `FROM exams e LEFT JOIN library_items li ON li.id = e.id` (db.rs:356-357). Every reading of the plan that treats library_items as the new primary and exams as legacy compatibility has it backwards — a library_items row with no exams row is invisible to list/get/search/stats. list_trashed_items (db.rs:1013) is the only production query that reads library_items alone.
- source_hash, issue_errors and issue_warnings have NO column in library_items. They are read from `e` only (db.rs:347-349) and are hardcoded to NULL/0 in both the library_items-only paths (:985-987, :1017). Dropping exams from the join silently zeroes three LibraryExamSummary fields the frontend already renders.
- LibraryItemRecord.task_type (db.rs:825) is set by three call sites and persisted by none — library_items has no such column, and the INSERT at :868-882 never binds it. The read queries then reconstruct it with COALESCE(e.task_type, CASE WHEN subject='writing' THEN li.category END) (:342-345). A silent data loss that the query layer papers over.
- Four of the six Phase-1 "formal schema" tables are dead: ingest_jobs, source_assets, publish_records, job_artifact_index are created and indexed but never read or written anywhere in the codebase. So processing_jobs_v2 is not replacing a working ingest_jobs — it is the first real job table. Correspondingly, library_items.source_asset_id is populated with a bare SHA-256 hex (library_commands.rs:196) rather than a source_assets.id, and linked_ingest_job_id / created_from_job_id are both just the item's own id.
- The already-shipped V2 workspace never touches SQLite. apply_authoring_v2_patches_core (authoring_v2_commands.rs:123-168) persists only via artifact_store::append_revision; that file contains no db::, job_store::, library_commands:: or workflow_state:: reference. library_item_revisions.payload_json holds V1 authoring-ir content forever. The plan's "short-time fork" framing understates this: it is a permanent fork for every V2 edit.
- foreign_keys is ON in production (db.rs:62) and OFF in the db.rs test harness (mem_conn at :1041-1045 omits the pragma). Any FK in the new v2 DDL — e.g. the plan's processing_jobs_v2 -> library_items_v2 reference — is unenforced in every unit test and enforced on users' machines.
- upsert_library_item deliberately INSERTs library_items with current_revision_id pointing at a revision row that does not exist yet (:867-884 before :887-896). This only works because current_revision_id has no REFERENCES clause — db.rs:117 is a comment arrow, not a constraint. Adding a real FK there, or copying that write order into a v2 table that does have FKs, breaks under foreign_keys=ON.
- upsert_library_item opens its own transaction via conn.unchecked_transaction() (:846) while taking only &Connection, so it cannot be called from inside an outer transaction. migrate_existing_into_library avoids this by writing only `exams` inside its tx (library_commands.rs:479) — which is also why the existing one-time migration never populated library_items at all.
- Two separate transient connections per save. upsert_reading_job calls upsert_exam (own connection, :104) then upsert_library_item_from_exam (a second connection, :177). exams and library_items are never written atomically, and both failures are swallowed to stderr (job_store.rs:37-42).
- Deleting a job hard-deletes only the exams row. job_commands.rs:149 and writing_store.rs:189 call delete_exam_by_id, leaving library_items + all revisions with deleted_at NULL — invisible to reads and absent from the trash list. The codebase already ships a workaround for the resulting state (restore_exam_from_library_item, db.rs:1001) and a test named restore_rehydrates_legacy_exam_row_deleted_by_old_flow (library_commands.rs:689).
- Nothing ever deletes a library_items or library_item_revisions row. The complete DELETE inventory in db.rs is two statements, both on exams (:766, :801). Every save appends a revision — including the extra save_job that a metadata edit triggers through write_back_meta_to_source (library_commands.rs:339) — so revisions grow unbounded, which is precisely what plan 4.4 wants to stop.
- publish/library.db is a different database with a colliding table name. export_nas_library.rs:693-726 creates its own library_meta plus reading_assets, and write_library_db (:728-737) deletes and recreates the file on every publish. Grepping for library_meta or "library.db" hits both DBs; only authoring_hub.db has migrations.
- The startup migration's return value and error are both discarded: `let _ = library_commands::migrate_existing_into_library(&root);` (lib.rs:1412). A migration that fails on a user's machine is completely silent, and because the done-flag is set inside the transaction (library_commands.rs:487), the only evidence is that it silently retries next launch.
- migrate_existing_into_library also runs prune_orphan_exams (library_commands.rs:483), which DELETEs any exams row whose id has no backing jobs/*/job.json or writing-jobs/*/writing-job.json. It is safe today only because migration_done_v1 makes it run once ever. Once v2-native items exist without job directories, anything that re-runs or generalizes that migration will delete their legacy mirror rows.
- The status vocabulary is mapped in six places: two Rust fns in db.rs (:309, :317), a third in library_commands.rs:160-168 that differs by a `_ => "draft"` catch-all, inline SQL CASE in both source-SQL builders (:336-338, :373-375), and the reverse direction in library_status_to_reading/library_status_to_writing (library_commands.rs:346-364). Plan 11.3's LibraryItemStatusV2 is a seventh vocabulary, incompatible with the frontend's current draft|needs_review|ready|exported contract.
- exams.subject and library_items.subject are both CHECKed IN ('reading','writing') (db.rs:87, :110), so plan 4.3's modality='listening' cannot be mirrored into the legacy tables — and SQLite cannot relax a CHECK via ALTER TABLE. Listening cannot ship behind the current exams-driven read path.
- open_connection redacts its own failure to the literal string "open_db_failed" (db.rs:58), logging the real path only to stderr. Debugging a schema or permissions failure from the frontend error alone is impossible; check stderr.

---

## job.json as source of truth + best-effort DB double write (src-tauri/src/job_store.rs and its 28 production write callers)
**数据流**：On-disk root is `app.path().app_data_dir()` (lib.rs:357). ensure_app_dirs (util.rs:63) creates config, config/secrets, jobs, writing-jobs, logs, cache, cache/parser, cache/thumbnails, cache/preview-server. The SQLite file is a sibling: `<root>/authoring_hub.db` (db.rs:24/:27), renamed from a legacy `library.db` on first open (db.rs:33).

JOB DIRECTORY LAYOUT, `<root>/jobs/<job_id>/`. Directories (28 create_dir_all calls across two functions, 15 unique from util::ensure_job_dirs:224 and 13 overlapping from artifact_store::ensure_job_artifact_layout:147): uploads/, sources/, extraction/, preview/, preview/runtime/, exports/, export-history/, legacy/, assets/{blobs,metadata,previews}/, authoring/, authoring/revisions/, authoring/patches/. Two more appear lazily: `debug/` (pdf_facts_shadow.rs:2324) and `revisions/<rfc3339>-source-replaced/` (authoring_commands.rs:113-122, a sibling of `authoring/revisions`, not created by ensure_job_dirs).

Files at the job-dir root: job.json (the ImportJob), authoring-ir.json (V1 content), authoring-project.json (cleanup.rs:72), document-ir.json, split-candidates.json, validation-report.json, publish-readiness-report.json, pipeline-report.json, pipeline-report-summary.json, source-review.json (source_review.rs:94), cleanup-summary.json, llm-last-suggestion.json, llm-calls.jsonl, manual-transcription.txt, vision-transcription.txt, vision-transcription-output.json, vision-answer-output.json, vision-answer-candidates.json, and the V2 shadows document-ir-v2.shadow{,.error,.compare}.json + document-ir-v2.shadow.overlay.svg (pdf_facts_shadow.rs:19-22) and authoring-ir-v2.shadow{,.compare,.error}.json (ielts_grammar/mod.rs:56-58). Under authoring/: current-revision.json (the V2 revision pointer), revision.lock, revisions/<n>.json, revisions/<n>.meta.json, patches/<n>.jsonl. Under uploads/: `<sha256[..8]>-<sanitized-name>` staged via a `.staging-<uuid>-<name>` temp then renamed (job_commands.rs:174-192). Under preview/: preview-assets.json. Under export-history/: phase5-export.lock. Under llm-suggestions/: per-suggestion files (llm_suggestions.rs:283/:300).

FORWARD WRITE (file -> DB), the only shape that exists: some *_core calls `update_job(root, job_id, |job| {...})` -> load_job reads job.json -> mutator -> `job.updated_at = Utc::now()` -> save_job -> `write_json(jobs/<id>/job.json)` (plain fs::write, non-atomic) -> `library_commands::upsert_reading_job` -> `reading_payload` reads authoring-ir.json (or serializes the ImportJob if that file is absent) -> `exam_record_from_reading_job` flattens ImportJob into ExamRecord with id = job_id -> `db::upsert_exam` INSERT..ON CONFLICT into `exams` -> `upsert_library_item_from_exam` -> skip if soft-deleted, else `db::upsert_library_item` which in one transaction upserts `library_items` AND inserts a brand-new `library_item_revisions` row (revision_no = max+1) holding a full copy of the payload, then repoints current_revision_id. Every failure after the file write is swallowed to stderr.

REVERSE WRITE (DB -> file), one path only: `update_library_exam_meta` -> `update_library_exam_meta_core` (library_commands.rs:227) -> `db::update_exam_meta` UPDATEs `exams` then `update_library_item_meta` UPDATEs `library_items` (db.rs:692) -> `write_back_meta_to_source` (:293) -> load_job, apply title/category/frequency/tags/status, then `update_job(root, id, |j| { *j = job.clone(); })` which re-enters the forward write. Failure is logged, DB is not rolled back.

READ PATHS. job.json is read in exactly three literal places: job_store.rs:30 (load_job), job_store.rs:66 (list_saved_jobs full dir scan), library_commands.rs:407-411 (startup migration, direct read_json). Production load_job callers: job_commands.rs:104, authoring_commands.rs:159/:257/:291/:371/:400, auto_pipeline.rs:1399/:2227/:2608, cleanup.rs:43, llm_commands.rs:221, library_commands.rs:316, pdf_facts_shadow.rs:2315, lib.rs:603, plus update_job's own internal load at job_store.rs:51. The library list is read from the DB via `list_exams` (db.rs:510) but driven `FROM exams e LEFT JOIN library_items li` (:356-357).

STARTUP. lib.rs:1408-1413 setup: app_root -> ensure_app_dirs -> `let _ = library_commands::migrate_existing_into_library(&root)` (return value discarded). That migration (library_commands.rs:386) is gated on `library_meta.migration_done_v1`, scans jobs/*/job.json and writing-jobs/*/writing-job.json, builds ExamRecords outside the transaction, then in one transaction upsert_exam_conn each, `prune_orphan_exams` deletes `exams` rows whose id has no live job.json, and sets migration_done_v1. It only writes `exams`, never `library_items`.

CONCURRENCY. run_auto_pipeline spawns a cloud worker in a thread::scope (auto_pipeline.rs:1420-1435); that worker does NOT touch job state (run_cloud_conversion_worker, :1226-1266, only sends over channels). Every DB touch opens a fresh transient WAL connection (db.rs:51, documented at db.rs:5-7) so save_job needs only `root: &Path`. There is no lock or version check around job.json — two concurrent update_job calls silently lose one mutation.

### 关键签名
- `save_job` — `pub(crate) fn save_job(root: &Path, job: &ImportJob) -> CommandResult<()> {     validate_path_segment("job_id", &job.job_id)?;     write_json(&job_dir(root, &job.job_id).join("job.json"), job)?;     // 双写题库 DB（失败记日志但不阻断主流程，文件仍是导出源）。     if let Err(error) = crate::library_commands::upsert_reading_job(root, job) {         eprintln!(             "[library] upsert_reading_job failed for {}: {}",             job.job_id, error         );     }     Ok(()) }`
  - The entire function, verbatim. Exactly three steps: validate the id as a path segment, write jobs/<id>/job.json via util::write_json (non-atomic fs::write, creates parents), then call upsert_reading_job and swallow any error into eprintln!. The DB is never allowed to fail the call. Note the asymmetry: it uses job_dir() (infallible, falls back to "__invalid_job_id__") while load_job uses safe_job_dir().
- `update_job` — `pub(crate) fn update_job(     root: &Path,     job_id: &str,     mutator: impl FnOnce(&mut ImportJob), ) -> CommandResult<ImportJob> {     let mut job = load_job(root, job_id)?;     mutator(&mut job);     job.updated_at = Utc::now();     save_job(root, &job)?;     Ok(job) }`
  - Read-modify-write with NO locking and NO optimistic-concurrency check. 26 of the 28 production write paths go through here. It is the only place updated_at is bumped.
- `load_job` — `pub(crate) fn load_job(root: &Path, job_id: &str) -> CommandResult<ImportJob> {     let dir = safe_job_dir(root, job_id)?;     read_json(&dir.join("job.json")) }`
  - Hard-fails if job.json is missing or unparseable — no Option, no default. get_job therefore fails outright for a job whose job.json is gone, which is what the workspace page depends on.
- `list_saved_jobs` — `pub(crate) fn list_saved_jobs(     root: &Path,     filter: Option<JobFilter>, ) -> CommandResult<Vec<ImportJob>>`
  - Body scans fs::read_dir(root/"jobs") and read_json::<ImportJob> on every jobs/*/job.json (:64-70). One unparseable job.json fails the whole listing (the `?` at :68). Filters status/search in memory, sorts by Reverse(updated_at).
- `make_job` — `pub(crate) fn make_job(input: CreateJobInput) -> ImportJob`
  - job_id format is `format!("import-{}-{}", now.format("%Y%m%d%H%M%S"), suffix)` where suffix is the first 8 chars of a simple UUID (:9-11). Defaults: status Working, category Some("P1"), frequency Some("medium"), current_step Upload, issue_counts default.
- `ImportJob` — `pub struct ImportJob {     #[serde(rename = "jobId")] pub job_id: String,     pub title: String,     pub status: JobStatus,     pub category: Option<String>,     pub frequency: Option<String>,     pub tags: Vec<String>,     #[serde(rename = "sourceFiles")] pub source_files: Vec<SourceFile>,     #[serde(rename = "activeLlmProfileId")] pub active_llm_profile_id: Option<String>,     #[serde(rename = "createdAt")] pub created_at: DateTime<Utc>,     #[serde(rename = "updatedAt")] pub updated_at: DateTime<Utc>,     #[serde(rename = "currentStep")] pub current_step: WorkflowStep,     #[serde(rename = "issueCounts")] pub issue_counts: IssueCounts, }`
  - This is the whole on-disk job.json shape. JobStatus (lib.rs:94) = Working|NeedsReview|DraftSaved|ExportReady|Exported|Cleaned with serde aliases for older names (Draft/Uploaded/Parsed/SplitReady -> Working etc.). WorkflowStep (lib.rs:115) = Upload|DocumentReview|Split|Authoring|LlmReview|Preview|Export|Pack. IssueCounts (lib.rs:127) = {errors: u32, warnings: u32, needs_review: u32}.
- `upsert_reading_job` — `pub(crate) fn upsert_reading_job(root: &Path, job: &ImportJob) -> CommandResult<()> {     let payload = match reading_payload(root, job) {         Ok(v) => v,         Err(ReadingPayloadError::ReadFailed(e)) => {             eprintln!(                 "[library] upsert_reading_job skipped for {}: authoring-ir.json read failed (DB payload preserved): {}",                 job.job_id, e             );             return Ok(());         }     };     let record = exam_record_from_reading_job(job, payload);     upsert_exam(root, &record)?;     // Phase 1：同步写正式主模型 library_items + revisions（尊重软删除：已软删除则不复活）。     if let Err(e) = upsert_library_item_from_exam(root, &record, "ReadingAuthoringIRV1") {         eprintln!(             "[library] upsert_library_item (reading) failed for {}: {}",             job.job_id, e         );     }     Ok(()) }`
  - The double-write body, verbatim. THREE independent failure swallow points: (1) an unreadable authoring-ir.json returns Ok(()) with no DB write at all, (2) upsert_library_item_from_exam errors are logged only, (3) save_job logs the outer error. Only upsert_exam can propagate. schema_version is hardcoded "ReadingAuthoringIRV1" even when the payload is actually a serialized ImportJob.
- `reading_payload` — `fn reading_payload(root: &Path, job: &ImportJob) -> Result<Value, ReadingPayloadError> {     let ir_path = crate::util::job_dir(root, &job.job_id).join("authoring-ir.json");     if !ir_path.exists() {         return Ok(serde_json::to_value(job).unwrap_or(Value::Null));     }     // 文件存在：读取必须成功，否则报错而非静默回退。     match crate::util::read_json_opt(&ir_path) {         Ok(Some(ir)) => Ok(ir),         Ok(None) => Ok(serde_json::to_value(job).unwrap_or(Value::Null)),         Err(e) => Err(ReadingPayloadError::ReadFailed(e)),     } }`
  - Proves the DB payload is the V1 authoring-ir.json, or the serialized ImportJob when that file is absent. It NEVER reads the V2 revision store (authoring/revisions/*.json).
- `exam_record_from_reading_job` — `pub(crate) fn exam_record_from_reading_job(job: &ImportJob, payload: Value) -> ExamRecord`
  - ExamRecord.id = job.job_id (:50). This plus LibraryItemRecord.id = exam.id (:187) is the code proof of library_items.id == job.job_id. status_from_reading (:26) collapses 6 JobStatus values into 4: Working->draft, NeedsReview->needs_review, DraftSaved|ExportReady->ready, Exported|Cleaned->exported. issue_counts.needs_review is DROPPED — only errors and warnings reach the DB.
- `upsert_library_item_from_exam` — `fn upsert_library_item_from_exam(     root: &Path,     exam: &ExamRecord,     schema_version: &str, ) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - Skips the write entirely if is_library_item_soft_deleted (:178) so a trashed item is not resurrected by a later pipeline step. to_library_item_status (:160) maps draft->draft, needs_review->review_required, ready->ready, exported->published.
- `write_back_meta_to_source` — `fn write_back_meta_to_source(     root: &Path,     subject: &str,     id: &str,     patch: &LibraryMetaPatch, ) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - The reverse double write named in plan M4. For reading it does load_job -> mutate -> `crate::job_store::update_job(root, id, |j| { *j = job.clone(); })?` (:339-341), i.e. a whole-struct overwrite that re-triggers the forward double write. Special case at :332: if the job is already Cleaned, a library edit of status="exported" must not downgrade it.
- `upsert_library_item` — `pub(crate) fn upsert_library_item(     conn: &Connection,     record: &LibraryItemRecord, ) -> CommandResult<String>`
  - Appends a NEW library_item_revisions row on every call (revision_no = existing max + 1, :864; INSERT at :887-896) carrying a full copy of record.revision_payload_json, then points current_revision_id at it. There is no dedup on identical payloads. Because save_job calls this transitively, every job status tick writes another full-payload revision.

| 判定 | 计划说 | 代码实际 | 证据 |
|---|---|---|---|
| confirmed | 11.1: `save_job()` 先写文件，再 best-effort 双写 DB | Exactly right, and the swallowing is deeper than described: there are three independent failure-swallow points (unreadable authoring-ir.json returns Ok(()) with no DB write at all; upsert_library_item errors are eprintln-only; save_job's own call is eprintln-only). Only db::upsert_exam can propagate an error, and even that is caught by save_job. | `src-tauri/src/job_store.rs:33-44, src-tauri/src/library_commands.rs:92-113` |
| confirmed | 11.1: 元数据又先写 DB，再 best-effort 回写 JSON | Confirmed verbatim. update_library_exam_meta_core writes exams + library_items first, then calls write_back_meta_to_source, whose failure is logged and never rolls back the DB. | `src-tauri/src/library_commands.rs:227-252, :293-344` |
| partly-wrong | 11.1: `library_items + revisions` is the "新 DB 内容" source, distinct from `exams.payload_json` (legacy) and `authoring/revisions/*` (V2) | library_items_revisions.payload_json is a byte-for-byte copy of exams.payload_json (library_commands.rs:200 sets revision_payload_json = exam.payload_json), and both come from the V1 authoring-ir.json — or from the serialized ImportJob when that file does not exist. Neither table ever receives the V2 revision content. There are three content stores, not four, and the DB holds only V1. | `src-tauri/src/library_commands.rs:71-82, :103-106, :200; src-tauri/src/db.rs:887-896` |
| confirmed | 11.1: 任何一步失败都可能形成短时或永久分叉 | Confirmed, plus two divergence sources the plan does not name. (a) The workspace's own save path never writes job state or the DB at all, so the library row's status is permanently stale relative to the user's edits. (b) The two delete commands are asymmetric: delete_job removes files + the exams row but orphans library_items/library_item_revisions, while delete_library_exam soft-deletes only library_items and leaves both the files and the exams row. | `src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs:123-168 (no db/job_store refs anywhere in the file); src-tauri/src/job_commands.rs:142-156; src-tauri/src/db.rs:764-776; src-tauri/src/library_commands.rs:254-263` |
| partly-wrong | 11.2 M1: 建立 `library_items_v2`、`processing_jobs_v2`、`actionable_issues_v1`、`library_item_recovery_v1` | None of those four tables or names exist. But an unused `ingest_jobs` table already exists with almost exactly the ProcessingJob shape the plan wants: id, kind, subject, status CHECK IN ('created','importing','parsing','review_required','authoring','ready_to_publish','published','archived','failed'), current_step, source_asset_id, linked_library_item_id, issue_counts_json, created_at, updated_at, plus idx_ingest_jobs_status. It is created by SCHEMA_SQL and never read or written by any Rust code (nor are source_assets, publish_records, job_artifact_index). | `src-tauri/src/db.rs:143-190; grep for ingest_jobs\|source_assets\|publish_records\|job_artifact_index over src-tauri/src returns only db.rs comments and the DDL` |
| confirmed | 11.2 M4: 删除 `save_job -> upsert_reading_job` 和 `library meta -> write_back_meta_to_source` | Both call sites exist exactly as named and are one line each to remove (job_store.rs:37 and library_commands.rs:245). The hard part is not the deletion — it is that 26 update_job sites and the whole library list currently depend on the file side being authoritative. | `src-tauri/src/job_store.rs:37, src-tauri/src/library_commands.rs:245` |
| partly-wrong | 11.2 M4 / 17.12: 删除 legacy `exams` 查询 | Blocked as stated. Every production library read is driven `FROM exams e LEFT JOIN library_items li` — list_exams (db.rs:515), get_exam (:554), get_exam_summary (:699), search_exams, get_stats. A library_items row without a matching exams row is invisible to all of them. The exams table cannot be dropped from the query path until the driving table is flipped to library_items; only list_trashed_items (:1013) already reads library_items directly. | `src-tauri/src/db.rs:325-360, :362-399, :510-550, :552-576, :697-712, :1013-1031` |

| already-done | 17.14: source import 创建 DB job | Already true for the library tables, and richer than the plan assumes: create_import_job_core calls save_job, which creates the exams row AND the library_items row + revision_no 1 before any source file is attached. import_source_file then update_jobs again, creating revision 2. The frontend therefore still calls listJobs() alongside listLibraryExams() and merges by id. | `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:215-237; src-tauri/src/db.rs:510; src/features/library/libraryStore.ts:55-66; src/features/library/libraryTypes.ts:67-124, :151` |

The authoritative state file has strictly weaker durability than the shadow content it governs. list_saved_jobs also hard-fails the entire library listing on a single unparseable job.json. | `src-tauri/src/util.rs:259-265 vs src-tauri/src/artifact_store.rs:249-258, :306, :318-321; src-tauri/src/job_store.rs:64-70` |
