### 现有测试方式
There is no `src-tauri/tests/` directory — every test is an inline `#[cfg(test)] mod tests`. Test counts by file: lib.rs 136, pdf_facts_shadow.rs 20, authoring_v2_commands.rs 14, db.rs 12, library_commands.rs 6, workflow_state.rs 4, auto_pipeline.rs 4, job_store.rs 1. In lib.rs, `mod tests` starts at line 1480, so every job_store reference at or above 1479 is production and everything below is a test.

Helpers to copy (all in lib.rs mod tests): `fn temp_test_root() -> PathBuf { env::temp_dir().join(format!("epic8-test-{}", Uuid::new_v4().simple())) }` (:1576-1578), `fn test_job() -> ImportJob` wrapping make_job with a P1/medium fixture (:1540-1548), `fn test_source(file_type: &str) -> SourceFile` (:1550-1560), `fn parser_fixture(name: &str)` resolving `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR/../fixtures/parser/<name>` (:1580-1586). Tests do not clean up via a Drop guard; they call `let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(root);` at the end (e.g. job_store.rs:103).

The canonical state assertion to copy is `assert_eq!(load_job(&root, &job.job_id).unwrap().status, JobStatus::Cleaned);` (lib.rs:5013-5016). It appears in this exact shape at lib.rs:5014, :7319, :7593, :7632, :7678, :7682, :7747, :8114, and as `let saved = load_job(&root, &job.job_id).unwrap();` at :4350, :4667, :4733, :4834, :4894, :5096, :5135, :10196. workflow_state.rs has 6 more at :162, :169, :174, :198, :213, :218. Those ~20 assertions are the ones a DB-backed store must re-point; they are the de facto contract for what status/currentStep/issueCounts each command produces.

Named tests that pin this subsystem specifically:
- `job_store.rs:92 legacy_job_load_does_not_require_phase1_directories` — save_job then load_job on a root where ensure_job_dirs was never called, asserting `!job_dir(&root, &job.job_id).join("authoring").exists()`. Proves write_json's create_dir_all is the only directory requirement; a DB store must keep this "no eager layout" property.
- `library_commands.rs:501 make_reading_appdata()` — builds a temp appData with jobs/import-test-1/{job.json,authoring-ir.json} by calling util::write_json directly (:520-521). Several tests write job.json bypassing save_job (:717, :722), which is exactly the pattern that will need a repository seeding helper.
- `library_commands.rs:615-621` — asserts `"job.json title must be synced from library edit"` after update_library_exam_meta_core. This is the test that will fail first, and by design, when write_back_meta_to_source is deleted.
- `library_commands.rs:624-625` and `:673` — "模拟「后续任务保存」：直接调 save_job（不经题库），双写应保留新 title 而非覆盖回旧值", i.e. a test asserting the double write does not clobber a library edit.
- `library_commands.rs:780` — resets migration_done_v1 and re-runs the migration to assert prune_orphan_exams drops rows with no job.json. Directly encodes "job.json is the liveness oracle" and must be rewritten.
- `workflow_state.rs:95-127` — imports `job_store::{load_job, make_job, save_job}`, makes a job, save_job, then calls the transition helpers and re-reads with load_job. The tightest existing round-trip template.
- `auto_pipeline.rs` tests use `run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway` (auto_pipeline.rs:1381) with an injected `FnMut(&Path, &str, &str, &Value, Option<&str>) -> CommandResult<Value>` gateway — the seam to reuse for testing pipeline state transitions without network. `run_cloud_review_core_with_gateway` (:2217) is the same shape.
- db.rs's 12 tests operate on a `Connection` directly (open_connection on a temp root), so repository-level tests can follow that pattern rather than going through *_core.

Nothing outside Rust reads job.json — grep for `job.json` across src/, scripts/ and tools/ in .ts/.tsx/.js/.mjs/.cjs/.py returns nothing — so the migration has no sidecar or script consumers to coordinate with, only the Tauri command surface.

### 意外发现
- The converged workspace's save path writes neither job state nor the DB. `apply_authoring_v2_patches_core` (authoring_v2_commands.rs:123-168) does load_current_authoring -> conflict check -> patches -> refresh_quality_report -> validate -> append_revision, and stops. A grep for job_store|update_job|save_job|ImportJob|job.json|db:: over the whole 97 KB file returns nothing. So a user who opens the workspace and resolves every blocker leaves ImportJob.status, exams.status and library_items.status untouched, and the library row keeps showing the stale stage. `export_authoring_v2_core` (:449) and `publish_nas_package_v2` (nas_package_v2.rs, also zero job_store refs) have the same hole — while the V1 publishers (export_pack.rs:180/:277, export_nas_library.rs:1512) do write Exported. The V1 and V2 publish paths already disagree.
- An `ingest_jobs` table with almost exactly the plan's ProcessingJob shape already exists and is completely dead. db.rs:143-155 declares id, kind, subject, status CHECK IN ('created','importing','parsing','review_required','authoring','ready_to_publish','published','archived','failed'), current_step, source_asset_id, linked_library_item_id, issue_counts_json, created_at, updated_at, plus idx_ingest_jobs_status. Nothing reads or writes it. Same for source_assets (:158), publish_records (:170) and job_artifact_index (:184). Plan 11.2 M1 proposes creating processing_jobs_v2 from scratch without acknowledging these four empty tables.
- Every job status tick writes a full copy of the authoring IR into the DB. save_job -> upsert_reading_job -> upsert_library_item unconditionally INSERTs a new library_item_revisions row (revision_no = max+1, db.rs:864, INSERT :887-896) whose payload_json is the entire authoring-ir.json. One run_auto_pipeline does 4 update_job calls (auto_pipeline.rs:1466, :1679, :1686, :2123); one export does 2 (Exported then Cleaned). There is no payload dedup, so the revisions table grows by a full document per state transition, and none of those revisions correspond to a user edit.
- The library query is driven by the legacy table, not the new one. active_summary_source_sql and active_detail_source_sql are both `FROM exams e LEFT JOIN library_items li ON li.id = e.id WHERE li.deleted_at IS NULL` (db.rs:356-358, :394-397). A library_items row with no matching exams row is invisible to list_exams, get_exam, get_exam_summary, search_exams and get_stats. `exams` is therefore load-bearing today, which directly contradicts plan 17.12's "delete production queries against legacy exams" — that deletion has to be preceded by flipping the driver.
- The two delete paths are asymmetric and neither is complete. delete_job (job_commands.rs:142-156) removes the job directory and the `exams` row but never touches library_items or library_item_revisions, leaving orphans that prune_orphan_exams (db.rs:781) cannot reach because it only prunes exams. delete_library_exam (library_commands.rs:254-263) soft-deletes only library_items, leaving both the on-disk job directory and the exams row in place forever. The converged UI calls only the second one (src/features/library/libraryStore.ts:105), so in the current product nothing on disk is ever reclaimed.
- The authoritative state file has weaker durability than the shadow content it governs. job.json is persisted by util::write_json (:259-265) — plain fs::write, no temp file, no rename, no fsync, no lock, and update_job has no version check. One directory below it, authoring/revisions/*.json is written under an exclusive flock on authoring/revision.lock with create_new + an atomic rename for current-revision.json (artifact_store.rs:249-258, :306, :318-321). Also, a single unparseable job.json fails the entire library listing because of the `?` at job_store.rs:68.
- An unreadable authoring-ir.json makes the DB write silently vanish. reading_payload returns Err only when the file exists but cannot be read (library_commands.rs:80), and upsert_reading_job then logs and `return Ok(())` (:95-101) — no exams write, no library_items write, and save_job reports success. The comment says this is deliberate (preserve the existing DB payload rather than overwrite it with job.json), but the effect is that job.json and the DB diverge with no error surfaced anywhere.
- status_from_reading throws away information on the way into the DB. It collapses 6 JobStatus values into 4 (library_commands.rs:26-33: DraftSaved and ExportReady both become "ready"; Exported and Cleaned both become "exported"), and ExamRecord carries only issue_errors and issue_warnings — issue_counts.needs_review is dropped entirely (:61-62). Combined with LibraryExamSummary having no currentStep (lib.rs:215-237), the DB cannot currently reconstruct the job state that libraryTypes.ts:95-124 needs, which is exactly why libraryStore.ts still calls listJobs().
- library_items.id == job.job_id is confirmed in code, and the id is minted in Rust, not the DB: make_job builds `import-<YYYYMMDDHHMMSS>-<uuid8>` (job_store.rs:9-11), exam_record_from_reading_job sets ExamRecord.id = job.job_id (library_commands.rs:50), and LibraryItemRecord.id = exam.id (:187). ExamRecord.exam_id is a separate, oddly derived field: `job.category.as_ref().map(|_| job.job_id.clone())` (:51), i.e. it is Some(job_id) whenever a category exists and None otherwise.
- A third revision tree exists that nobody documents. archive_current_draft_for_source_replacement (authoring_commands.rs:109-152) creates jobs/<id>/revisions/<rfc3339-with-punctuation-stripped>-source-replaced/ and fs::renames 6 files into it (authoring-ir.json, split-candidates.json, pipeline-report.json, validation-report.json, publish-readiness-report.json, source-review.json) plus a revision-meta.json. This is a sibling of `authoring/revisions` (the V2 store), is not created by ensure_job_dirs, and is not in any cleanup list, so it accumulates on every source replacement.
- The startup migration's result is discarded. lib.rs:1412 is `let _ = library_commands::migrate_existing_into_library(&root);`. A failed migration leaves migration_done_v1 unset (correct, it retries next launch) but the app starts with a partially populated library and no user-visible signal. The migration also writes only `exams`, never library_items, so every migrated item has zero revision rows and get_exam falls back to e.payload_json via the COALESCE at db.rs:393.
- The plan's target symbols do not exist yet at all: no processing/ directory, no repository.rs, no LibraryRepository or LibraryRepositoryV2, no list_library_items, no library_items_v2 / processing_jobs_v2 / actionable_issues_v1 / library_item_recovery_v1, no cleanup_on_startup and no crash-recovery path (cleanup.rs only has the two export-triggered functions). Plan 17.14 reads as if job_store.rs were being renamed; it is being written from nothing, against 28 live write paths.

---

## library repository and legacy exams double write (src-tauri/src/library_commands.rs + db.rs)
**数据流**：CURRENT, verified end to end.

WRITE PATH A - forward double write (file first, DB best-effort). job_store::save_job (job_store.rs:33-44) writes jobs/<id>/job.json, then calls library_commands::upsert_reading_job (92-113) and swallows any error. upsert_reading_job reads jobs/<id>/authoring-ir.json via reading_payload (71-82; if the file exists but fails to parse it SKIPS the DB write entirely and returns Ok to protect the existing DB payload, library_commands.rs:95-101), builds an ExamRecord with id = job.job_id (47-66), calls db::upsert_exam (db.rs:277) which OPENS ITS OWN CONNECTION and autocommits an INSERT..ON CONFLICT into `exams`, then calls upsert_library_item_from_exam (172-207) which OPENS A SECOND CONNECTION, checks is_library_item_soft_deleted (280-289), and calls db::upsert_library_item (db.rs:841-900) which in its own unchecked_transaction upserts `library_items` AND unconditionally INSERTs a new `library_item_revisions` row carrying a full copy of the payload. So: 1 file + 2 connections + 3 statements in 2 uncoordinated transactions per save. writing_store::save_writing_job (122-136) is the mirror image via upsert_writing_job (139-150). One localOnly import of one PDF runs this ~6 times (job_commands.rs:87 create, :203 import_source_file, auto_pipeline.rs:1466 parse, :1679 split, :1686 authoring, :2123 final), i.e. ~6 full-payload revision rows and 12 connections, each connection re-running the whole CREATE TABLE/INDEX batch (db.rs:66).

WRITE PATH B - reverse write (DB first, file best-effort). update_library_exam_meta_core (227-252): open connection, get_exam to learn subject (234), db::update_exam_meta (239) which UPDATEs `exams` (db.rs:684-688) and then separately UPDATEs `library_items` (db.rs:692 -> 757-760) with no wrapping transaction, then write_back_meta_to_source (245, body 293-344) mutates job.json / writing-job.json and re-enters save_job / save_writing_job - which runs WRITE PATH A again. Net effect of renaming one title: UPDATE exams, UPDATE library_items, write job.json, UPSERT exams, UPSERT library_items, INSERT a brand-new content revision. Failure of the file half is only logged (246-250).

WRITE PATH C - the V2 canonical content, which never reaches the DB. The new workspace saves via apply_authoring_v2_patches_core (authoring_v2_commands.rs:123-168) -> append_revision (artifact_store.rs:240) -> jobs/<id>/authoring/revisions/<n>.json under a file lock. Reads come from load_current_authoring (authoring_v2_commands.rs:725-742): current revision file, else the V2 shadow file. authoring_v2_commands.rs contains zero references to save_job, update_job, library_commands or db::. So there are FIVE content stores and the DB holds a copy of only one of them (the V1 authoring-ir.json, or job.json itself before authoring-ir exists).

DELETE / RESTORE. delete_library_exam_core (254-263) = ensure_library_item_for_exam (backfill from legacy exams if needed) + soft_delete_library_item; the jobs/<id> directory and both JSON stores stay on disk. restore_library_exam_core (265-272) = restore_library_item + restore_exam_from_library_item, the latter re-INSERTING an `exams` row from library_items + its current revision (db.rs:1001-1010). Separately, delete_job (job_commands.rs:142-156) and delete_writing_job (writing_store.rs:183-196) rm -rf the directory and hard-DELETE only the `exams` row (db::delete_exam_by_id), leaving the library_items row and its whole revision chain orphaned.

READ PATH. All five readers (list_exams db.rs:510, get_exam :552, search_exams :577, get_stats :601, get_exam_summary :697) select from one of two generated subqueries, active_summary_source_sql (325-360) / active_detail_source_sql (362-399). Both are `FROM exams e LEFT JOIN library_items li ON li.id = e.id [LEFT JOIN library_item_revisions rev ON rev.id = li.current_revision_id] WHERE li.deleted_at IS NULL`. Legacy `exams` is the DRIVING table; every user-visible field is COALESCE(li.x, e.x) with library_items winning; status is a nested CASE that maps review_required->needs_review and published->exported; updated_at is max(li, e); issue_errors/issue_warnings/source_hash/exam_id come ONLY from exams because library_items has no such columns; the detail payload is COALESCE(rev.payload_json, e.payload_json). list_trashed_items (1013-1031) is the one query that reads library_items alone, and it fabricates exam_id=NULL, source_hash=NULL, issue_errors=0, issue_warnings=0.

TARGET, single direction. One LibraryRepository owning a single Connection in AppState (today lib.rs:1407 manages an empty AppState marker), schema+migration run once at startup instead of on every open_connection. One transaction per user intent:

  commit_canonical(&mut self, item_id, ds: &Value, meta: MetaDelta, reason) -> Result<RevisionId>
    tx = conn.transaction()
    SELECT status, deleted_at, current_revision_id, content_hash FROM library_items WHERE id=?  -- refuse if deleted_at IS NOT NULL
    if hash(ds) != current content_hash { INSERT library_item_revisions (revision_no+1, payload=ds, schema_version=ds.schemaVersion, change_reason=reason) }
    UPDATE library_items SET title/category/difficulty/status/tags_json/issue_errors/issue_warnings/current_revision_id/updated_at
    tx.commit()

with the three metadata-only intents (rename, retag, status) sharing the same tx but taking the no-new-revision branch, and delete/restore/purge as their own single-statement transactions on library_items only. That requires: (1) db::upsert_library_item's signature changing from &Connection + unchecked_transaction() to &Transaction, otherwise it cannot nest inside this tx or inside migration's conn.transaction(); (2) library_items gaining real issue_errors/issue_warnings/source-reference/content_hash columns, since today those values exist only on the legacy exams row; (3) the V2 revision commit at authoring_v2_commands.rs:149 calling commit_canonical, because that is the only place the canonical DS actually changes; (4) file writes becoming derived output (job.json as a cache, or dropped) with no path from a file write back into the DB.

### 关键签名
- `upsert_reading_job` — `pub(crate) fn upsert_reading_job(root: &Path, job: &ImportJob) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - DOUBLE-WRITE ENTRY #1, called only from job_store.rs:37. Returns Ok(()) and merely logs when authoring-ir.json is unreadable (95-101) - a deliberate 'preserve the DB payload' skip. Also returns Ok when the library_items half fails (106-111).
- `upsert_writing_job` — `pub(crate) fn upsert_writing_job(root: &Path, job: &WritingJob) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - DOUBLE-WRITE ENTRY #2, called only from writing_store.rs:129.
- `upsert_library_item_from_exam` — `fn upsert_library_item_from_exam(     root: &Path,     exam: &ExamRecord,     schema_version: &str, ) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - Private. Opens its OWN connection at line 177 so it can never join a caller's transaction. schema_version is a caller-supplied literal, unlike db::infer_revision_schema_version.
- `write_back_meta_to_source` — `fn write_back_meta_to_source(     root: &Path,     subject: &str,     id: &str,     patch: &LibraryMetaPatch, ) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - Private. The reverse write. Reading branch ends in job_store::update_job with `*j = job.clone()` (339-341), which discards update_job's own fresh disk read - a lost-update window. Writing branch calls save_writing_job directly (314).
- `update_library_exam_meta_core` — `pub(crate) fn update_library_exam_meta_core(     root: &Path,     id: &str,     patch: LibraryMetaPatch, ) -> CommandResult<Option<LibraryExamSummary>>`
  - Calls write_back_meta_to_source at line 245 with the error only logged. Three uncoordinated statements on one connection, no transaction.
- `delete_library_exam_core` — `pub(crate) fn delete_library_exam_core(root: &Path, id: &str) -> CommandResult<bool>`
  - Returns Ok(false) - not an error - when neither a library_items row nor a legacy exams row exists. The frontend discards the boolean (libraryStore.ts:105).
- `restore_library_exam_core` — `pub(crate) fn restore_library_exam_core(root: &Path, id: &str) -> CommandResult<bool>`
  - Two writes, no transaction: restore_library_item then the reverse-direction restore_exam_from_library_item (line 269).
- `list_library_exams_core` — `pub(crate) fn list_library_exams_core(     root: &Path,     filter: Option<LibraryFilter>, ) -> CommandResult<Vec<LibraryExamSummary>>`
  - 
- `get_library_exam_core` — `pub(crate) fn get_library_exam_core(     root: &Path,     id: &str, ) -> CommandResult<Option<LibraryExamDetail>>`
  - 
- `search_library_exams_core` — `pub(crate) fn search_library_exams_core(     root: &Path,     query: &str, ) -> CommandResult<Vec<LibraryExamSummary>>`
  - No consumer in the new frontend; only src/pages/LibraryPage.tsx:37.
- `get_library_stats_core` — `pub(crate) fn get_library_stats_core(root: &Path) -> CommandResult<LibraryStats>`
  - No consumer in the new frontend; only src/pages/Dashboard.tsx:16 and src/pages/LibraryPage.tsx:52.
- `list_trashed_exams_core` — `pub(crate) fn list_trashed_exams_core(root: &Path) -> CommandResult<Vec<LibraryExamSummary>>`
  - 

### 计划断言复核

| 判定 | 计划说 | 代码实际 | 证据 |
|---|---|---|---|
| confirmed | 11.1: `save_job()` writes files first, then best-effort double-writes the DB; metadata writes the DB first, then best-effort writes back to JSON. Any step can fork. | Exactly right, and worse than stated: the 'DB' half is itself two uncoordinated writes on two separate connections (upsert_exam at library_commands.rs:104 then upsert_library_item_from_exam at :106, which opens its own connection at :177), so the fork can also occur between exams and library_items. | `job_store.rs:33-44, library_commands.rs:92-113, library_commands.rs:227-252, library_commands.rs:293-344` |
| partly-wrong | 11.1: the five stores are job.json, authoring-ir.json, authoring/revisions/*, exams.payload_json, library_items+revisions. | All five exist. But the plan reads as if the DB mirrors the canonical content; it does not. library_item_revisions.payload_json is a copy of authoring-ir.json (V1), or of job.json itself when authoring-ir.json does not exist yet (library_commands.rs:73-74). The authoring/revisions/* V2 store - the one the new workspace edits - has no DB representation at all. | `job_store.rs:35; authoring_commands.rs:417 and :503; artifact_store.rs:272 via authoring_v2_commands.rs:149; db.rs:93; db.rs:129-139` |
| wrong | 11.2 M2: migration inserts library_items_v2 rows (insert_library_item_v2 / insert_shell_item) preferring current V2 revision, then the V2 shadow, then converted V1. | migrate_existing_into_library writes ONLY the legacy `exams` table. It never inserts a library_items row, never a revision, and never looks at the V2 revision store or shadow. After a fresh migration library_items is empty and every active query serves pure legacy data through the LEFT JOIN. library_items rows appear later only as a side effect of save_job or of delete_library_exam's backfill. | `library_commands.rs:386-490, specifically the only write at :479 (upsert_exam_conn) plus :483 prune and :487 marker` |
| partly-wrong | 11.2 M2: migration must be idempotent, marked by `migration_version`, and must not overwrite user-edited V2 rows. | Idempotency exists but it is a single boolean key `migration_done_v1`, not a version. There is no migration_version anywhere in the crate (grep returns nothing), so a second migration wave cannot be expressed, and the test at library_commands.rs:783 has to DELETE the marker row to re-run. | `library_commands.rs:388 and :487 (library_meta.migration_done_v1), db.rs:195-215` |
| wrong | 11.2 M1: add library_items_v2, processing_jobs_v2, actionable_issues_v1, library_item_recovery_v1 while keeping old tables readable. | None of the four exist. What does exist from an earlier phase: exams, library_meta, library_items, library_item_revisions, plus four tables that are created and then never read or written by any code - ingest_jobs (db.rs:143), source_assets (:158), publish_records (:170), job_artifact_index (:184). | `grep for those four names across src-tauri/src returns nothing; db.rs:77-191 is the whole schema` |
| wrong | 11.2 M3: the new UI only reads/writes the new tables; job_store and legacy library_commands are no longer called by the new UI. | The opposite is true today. The new library page calls listJobs() (a jobs/ directory scan) alongside listLibraryExams(), and buildRow prefers job.title over summary.title (libraryTypes.ts:147), job.updatedAt over summary.updatedAt (:154), and derives the user-visible stage from job.status/job.currentStep first (:95-124). The new workspace uses only getAuthoringV2/applyAuthoringV2Patches, which touch no table at all. The new UI is file-first, not table-first. | `src/features/library/libraryStore.ts:2 and :55-65; src/features/library/libraryTypes.ts:147,154,95-124; src/features/editor/useCanonicalEditor.ts:2,67,107` |
| confirmed | 11.2 M4 / 17.13: delete `save_job -> upsert_reading_job`, delete `library meta -> write_back_meta_to_source`, delete legacy `exams` queries. | All three targets exist and are precisely identifiable. The third is much bigger than one line: `exams` is the DRIVING table of both generated subqueries, so removing it means rewriting the FROM clause and finding new homes for issue_errors, issue_warnings, source_hash and exam_id, none of which exist as columns on library_items. | `job_store.rs:36-42, library_commands.rs:245, db.rs:325-399 with readers at db.rs:514, 553, 582, 604, 614, 698` |
| partly-wrong | 17.13: new thin Tauri commands calling LibraryRepository. | No LibraryRepository type exists. The eight commands are, however, already thin in the right shape (app_root + spawn_blocking + a *_core in library_commands.rs), so they can be re-pointed without changing the invoke surface. AppState (lib.rs:1407) is an empty marker struct, so there is no place to hold a repository connection yet. | `lib.rs:1317-1401, lib.rs:1467-1474; grep for LibraryRepository returns nothing` |
| confirmed | 17.13: during the legacy-data phase, only READ the old file/job - stop writing back to it. | Confirmed as a gap, and there are four reverse/legacy writers, not one: write_back_meta_to_source (DB -> JSON), restore_exam_from_library_item (library_items -> exams), prune_orphan_exams (filesystem -> DELETE FROM exams), and delete_exam_by_id from the two file-deletion commands. | `library_commands.rs:293-344 (write-back), db.rs:1001-1010 (restore_exam_from_library_item), db.rs:781-807 (prune_orphan_exams), job_commands.rs:149, writing_store.rs:189` |
| partly-wrong | 11.3: LibraryItemStatusV2 = Processing \| ActionRequired \| Ready \| Publishing \| Published \| Failed \| Archived. | Three enums are in play and none is the target. library_items allows draft\|review_required\|ready\|published\|archived; exams allows only draft\|needs_review\|ready\|exported - narrower, so 'archived' is unreachable while exams is still written (the exams UPDATE in update_exam_meta runs first and would hit the CHECK). There is no Processing, Publishing or Failed state anywhere, which is why the new UI's 'failed' stage tab can never match a row. | `db.rs:115 CHECK (draft\|review_required\|ready\|published\|archived), db.rs:90 CHECK (draft\|needs_review\|ready\|exported), library_commands.rs:160-168, db.rs:309-323, src/features/library/libraryTypes.ts:5-13` |
| confirmed | The verified fact library_items.id == job.job_id. | Confirmed for both subjects. The id is the job id three times over. Note library_items.id is declared 'stable UUID' at db.rs:109 but is actually the import-<timestamp>-<suffix> string from job_store.rs:11 (or writing-<timestamp>-<suffix>, writing_store.rs:102). | `library_commands.rs:50 (ExamRecord.id = job.job_id), library_commands.rs:187 (LibraryItemRecord.id = exam.id), library_commands.rs:197 and :202 (linked_ingest_job_id and created_from_job_id also = exam.id)` |
| confirmed | 17.12: move data access out of db.rs into a repository; split the SQL out. | Accurate and cheap to do: SQL is already almost fully centralised in db.rs, with exactly one leak (is_library_item_soft_deleted's raw SELECT in library_commands.rs). | `db.rs is 1349 lines / 53 KB; all library SQL is in db.rs except one raw query at library_commands.rs:281-283` |

### 现有测试方式
All tests are inline `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` in the same files; there is no src-tauri/tests/ directory and no [[test]] target in Cargo.toml (package `ielts-author-studio`, lib `ielts_author_studio_lib`). Filter with `cargo test -p ielts-author-studio library_commands::` or `... db::`.

TWO STYLES TO COPY.
(1) db.rs uses pure in-memory SQLite - `fn mem_conn() -> Connection { let conn = Connection::open_in_memory().unwrap(); ensure_schema(&conn).unwrap(); conn }` (db.rs:1041-1045) plus builders `sample_record(id, subject, status)` (1047-1069) and `sample_library_item(id, subject, status)` (1208-1238). This is the right template for LibraryRepository unit tests: no filesystem, no app root.
(2) library_commands.rs uses real temp directories and real files - `make_reading_appdata()` (501-543) creates std::env::temp_dir()/ielts-lib-test-<uuid> with a full job.json plus a minimal ReadingAuthoringIRV1 authoring-ir.json, `make_writing_appdata()` (545-567) the writing equivalent, `cleanup(&root)` (569-571) removes the tree. Note these tests write to the real temp dir and are not isolated per thread; there is no #[serial] guard.

EXISTING TESTS, and which ones the convergence deliberately breaks.
library_commands.rs (6): `migrate_imports_reading_and_writing` (573) asserts migration returns 2 and is idempotent; `update_meta_writes_back_to_job_json_and_survives_resave` (597) asserts job.json.title is synced from a library edit AND that a later save_job does not overwrite the DB - DIES with the reverse write-back, and its second half is the closest thing to a regression test for the fork; `delete_library_exam_moves_to_trash_and_restore_brings_it_back` (637) asserts the job dir survives a soft delete, that a resave does not revive a trashed item (669-677 - keep this assertion, it is the anti-resurrection contract), and that restore returns it to the active list; `restore_rehydrates_legacy_exam_row_deleted_by_old_flow` (688) exists purely to pin the reverse library_items -> exams write - DELETE with restore_exam_from_library_item; `update_meta_keeps_cleaned_reading_jobs_cleaned` (712) pins the Cleaned-preservation branch inside write_back_meta_to_source - DIES with it, but the underlying rule (editing metadata must not downgrade a published/cleaned item) needs re-expressing against the repository status enum; `migrate_prunes_orphan_db_rows` (753) pins filesystem-driven pruning - DELETE.
db.rs (13): `schema_is_idempotent` (1071), `upsert_inserts_then_updates` (1078, also pins created_at preservation), `list_filters_by_subject_and_status` (1098), `search_matches_title_and_tags` (1130), `stats_group_counts` (1142), `update_meta_partial_and_delete` (1156), `meta_get_set` (1179), `check_constraint_rejects_invalid_status_and_subject` (1189), `library_item_upsert_creates_revisions` (1239 - asserts a new revision per save, i.e. it pins exactly the revision-spam behaviour that must change; rewrite it to assert no new revision for a metadata-only write), `soft_delete_and_restore` (1277), `active_queries_follow_soft_delete_and_normalize_statuses` (1295 - the single best template for a repository read test: it walks list/get/search/stats/trash/restore in one pass), `migrate_db_file_renames_legacy` (1324).

WHAT IS NOT COVERED and needs new tests. No test asserts the exams/library_items pair stays consistent when one half fails. No test covers the delete_job -> orphaned library_items path (job_commands.rs:149). No test covers title divergence across delete -> save -> restore. No test asserts anything about the V2 revision store reaching the DB, because it does not. No test exercises the eight lib.rs command shells (they need a Tauri AppHandle) - the *_core split at library_commands.rs:209 is what makes them testable, so keep that boundary.

### 意外发现
- The new workspace's saves never reach the library at all. apply_authoring_v2_patches_core writes only jobs/<id>/authoring/revisions/<n>.json (authoring_v2_commands.rs:149 -> artifact_store.rs:240); grep for save_job|update_job|library_commands|db:: across authoring_v2_commands.rs returns nothing. So the DB copy (library_item_revisions.payload_json) is a copy of the V1 authoring-ir.json, and the plan's premise that the DB is one of two mirrors of the canonical content is wrong - it is a mirror of a different, older document.
- migrate_existing_into_library never creates a library_items row. Its only write is upsert_exam_conn (library_commands.rs:479). On a fresh install library_items is EMPTY after migration, and every active query returns pure legacy data through the LEFT JOIN. Plan 11.2 M2's insert_library_item_v2 loop does not exist in any form.
- `exams`, not library_items, is the DRIVING table of every active query (db.rs:356-357, :394-395). A library_items row without an exams sibling is invisible to list_exams, get_exam, search_exams and get_stats. This makes the legacy table load-bearing for the entire library surface, not a compatibility shim.
- Orphan + resurrection path. delete_job (job_commands.rs:149) and delete_writing_job (writing_store.rs:189) hard-delete only the exams row, leaving the library_items row and its whole revision chain with deleted_at NULL - invisible in the active list (no exams sibling) and invisible in trash (not soft-deleted). A later delete_library_exam on that id succeeds and puts it in trash, and restore_library_exam then re-INSERTs an exams row from the stale revision (db.rs:1001-1010), resurrecting a library entry whose files were deleted.
- Silent title/status regression through the trash. upsert_exam (library_commands.rs:104) runs BEFORE the soft-delete guard at :178, so a trashed item's exams row keeps absorbing pipeline updates while its library_items row is frozen. Because the read view is COALESCE(li.title, e.title) with library_items winning, delete -> any pipeline save -> restore makes the title and status revert to their pre-delete values. The existing restore test (library_commands.rs:638-686) re-saves the job and never asserts the title, so this is untested.
- Every save appends a full content revision. db::upsert_library_item unconditionally INSERTs into library_item_revisions (db.rs:887-896) with no content comparison, and it is driven by save_job. One localOnly import of one PDF produces ~6 full-payload revision rows (job_commands.rs:87, :203; auto_pipeline.rs:1466, 1679, 1686, 2123), a metadata-only rename produces a 7th, and even delete_library_exam can produce one via the ensure_library_item_for_exam backfill.
- Early revisions carry a false schema_version. upsert_reading_job hardcodes "ReadingAuthoringIRV1" (library_commands.rs:106) but reading_payload falls back to serializing the ImportJob itself when authoring-ir.json does not exist yet (:73-74) - so the first revisions of every item are labelled as authoring IR while actually containing job.json. db.rs:440-456 infers schema_version from the payload instead, so the crate has two contradictory derivations.
- prune_orphan_exams can delete every exams row. It builds live_ids by scanning jobs/ and writing-jobs/ with `if let Ok(entries) = read_dir(...)` and `.flatten()` (library_commands.rs:405, 443), silently dropping IO failures, then hard-deletes every exams row not in the set (db.rs:799-804). ensure_app_dirs (lib.rs:1410) creates both directories before the call so the normal path is safe, but a permission or enumeration failure turns into DB deletion. It is gated by the one-shot migration_done_v1 marker, so it fires exactly on the run most likely to be a fresh or moved appData root.
- unchecked_transaction() is a landmine for the migration work plan 11.2 M2 asks for. upsert_library_item issues BEGIN via conn.unchecked_transaction() (db.rs:845); calling it inside migrate_existing_into_library's conn.transaction() (library_commands.rs:470) would fail with 'cannot start a transaction within a transaction'. The signature has to become &Transaction before any item can be written during migration.
- Four Phase-1 tables are dead. ingest_jobs (db.rs:143), source_assets (:158), publish_records (:170) and job_artifact_index (:184) are created on every open_connection and never read or written anywhere in the crate. library_items.source_asset_id is filled with a raw sha256 from the source file (library_commands.rs:196), not an id into source_assets, despite the comment at db.rs:118.
- The status CHECKs disagree, and the narrower one runs first. library_items allows 'archived' (db.rs:115) but exams does not (db.rs:90), and update_exam_meta UPDATEs exams before library_items (db.rs:684 then :692), so 'archived' is unreachable while the double write stands. patch.status is also written into exams.status with no validation - an unexpected value from the frontend surfaces as a raw CHECK-constraint error string.
- The new library UI is file-first, the opposite of the plan's direction. libraryStore.ts merges listJobs() (a directory scan) with listLibraryExams(), and buildRow prefers job.title (libraryTypes.ts:147) and job.updatedAt (:154) over the DB summary while deriveStage branches on job.status/job.currentStep first (:95-124). Consequence for sequencing: removing the save_job -> DB hook alone changes nothing the user sees; dropping listJobs() is the step that makes repository correctness load-bearing.
- Four of the eight library commands have no consumer in the converged UI. get_library_exam and update_library_exam_meta are used only by src/pages/LibraryExamDetail.tsx, search_library_exams only by src/pages/LibraryPage.tsx, get_library_stats only by src/pages/Dashboard.tsx and src/pages/LibraryPage.tsx - all reachable only through #/legacy/... (src/app/router.ts:11-24). The metadata write-back (and its whole reverse direction) therefore has no new-UI caller to preserve.
- There is no permanent delete. Nothing purges a trashed library item: no purge/hard_delete/empty_trash symbol exists in src-tauri/src, and PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON plus library_item_revisions.library_item_id REFERENCES library_items(id) (db.rs:63, :131) means a naive DELETE of an item row would fail while revisions exist. Any repository redesign has to introduce a purge that deletes revisions first.
- open_connection re-runs the entire schema on every call (db.rs:66). One save_job opens two connections, so a single import of one PDF executes the 11 CREATE TABLE + 9 CREATE INDEX batch about a dozen times. AppState (lib.rs:1407) is an empty marker struct, so there is currently nowhere for a repository to hold a long-lived connection.
- The library's 'failed' stage can never appear. src/features/library/libraryTypes.ts:5-13 defines a 'failed' stage and a 'failed' filter tab, but neither JobStatus (lib.rs:92-111) nor the library_items status CHECK has a failed state, so no data source can produce it. Plan 11.3's Processing / Publishing / Failed all need new repository states.

---

## cleanup, environment, settings and model profiles (src-tauri: cleanup.rs / environment.rs / diagnostics.rs / llm_commands.rs, plus their real dependencies llm_profiles.rs, llm_gateway.rs, job_commands.rs)
**数据流**：SETTINGS WRITE PATH. The frontend has two disjoint stores. (a) Model connection goes to Rust: SettingsPage.saveAndTest -> tauriCommands.saveLlmProfile({input}) -> save_llm_profile (lib.rs:1076) -> ensure_app_dirs -> save_llm_profile_core (llm_commands.rs:85). That function (1) checks input.provider against SUPPORTED_LLM_PROVIDERS, (2) validate_llm_base_url, (3) if api_key.is_some() calls save_profile_secret -> keyring::Entry(\"com.ielts.author.studio.llm\", profile_id).set_password, falling back to <root>/config/secrets/<id>.key only under EPIC8_ALLOW_PLAINTEXT_SECRET_FALLBACK, (4) rebuilds a fixed 13-key JSON object, (5) load_profiles -> retain -> insert(0) -> save_profiles writes the whole array to <root>/config/llm-profiles.json. (b) Everything else the plan calls a setting — keepSourceFiles, nasDestination, localConcurrency, cloudConcurrency, developerMode — never leaves the browser: appSettings.ts writes localStorage key \"ielts-author-studio.app-settings.v1\" (appSettings.ts:11, 59-73). The only true backend setting is keepFullProcessArtifacts -> save_diagnostics_settings -> <root>/config/diagnostics-settings.json.

SETTINGS READ PATH. list_llm_profiles -> load_profiles -> for each profile redact_profile_for_ui, which performs one OS keyring get_password per profile (llm_profiles.rs:137) and stamps hasApiKey/secretStorageBackend/secretStorageMessage. Because find_profile also calls load_profiles, a single test_llm_profile does 2N+1 keyring reads.

GATEWAY PATH. A profile is chosen either explicitly (UI passes profileId) or by select_llm_profile (auto_pipeline.rs:115) from job.active_llm_profile_id then by score. The chosen id is resolved with find_profile, reduced to a 7-field payload by make_llm_input / profile_payload (llm_suggestions.rs:139-147, 154-164) — provider, baseUrl, model, temperature, timeoutMs, forceJson, profileId — and the secret is fetched separately by load_llm_api_key and passed as an out-of-band argument, never inside the payload. run_llm_gateway (llm_gateway.rs:23) writes a redacted copy of the input to jobs/<id>/cache/llm/<cmd>-input-<ts>.json, dispatches on command_name to one of four routes, appends a record to jobs/<id>/llm-calls.jsonl, and writes the output next to the input. Every route reaches openai_post_once -> openai_chat_completions_endpoint -> require_openai_compatible_provider, so a non-OpenAI/Ollama provider fails at request time with llm_provider_unsupported_for_openai_route.

CLEANUP PATH. Two functions, six call sites, all inside other commands — there is no cleanup command and no scheduler. minimize_process_artifacts_after_authoring fires at the END of draft generation: authoring_commands.rs:458 (\"build_authoring_ir\"), auto_pipeline.rs:2186 (\"run_auto_pipeline\"), and on publish-gate failure at export_pack.rs:147, export_pack.rs:254, export_nas_library.rs:1118. cleanup_transient_job_artifacts fires only on successful V1 export: export_pack.rs:184 (single reading-assets), export_pack.rs:303 (reading-js batch), export_nas_library.rs:1516 (NAS library). Both first call write_authoring_project (which read_json's authoring-ir.json), then consult load_diagnostics_settings and return early if keepFullProcessArtifacts. Deletion is a literal list against jobs/<job_id>/ plus a prefix scan of <root>/cache/parser for entries starting with job_id (cleanup.rs:76-97). Nothing consults the SQLite library_items table, no asset reference counting, no age policy.

WHAT SURVIVES CLEANUP (verified against the dirs ensure_job_dirs/ensure_job_artifact_layout create): job.json, authoring-ir.json, authoring-project.json, source-review.json, uploads/, exports/, and — unlisted by the summary but never deleted — sources/, extraction/, assets/{blobs,metadata,previews}/, authoring/{revisions,patches}/, export-history/, legacy/, authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json, authoring-ir-v2.shadow.{error,compare}.json, document-ir-v2.shadow.json, document-ir-v2.shadow.{error,overlay.svg,compare}.json. The V2 workspace is therefore cleanup-safe today: get_authoring_v2 -> build_editor_session -> load_current_authoring reads authoring/revisions/<n>.json or falls back to authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json (authoring_v2_commands.rs:725-742), and refresh_quality_report reads document-ir-v2.shadow.json (744-748) — none of which cleanup touches.

### 关键签名
- `cleanup_transient_job_artifacts` — `pub(crate) fn cleanup_transient_job_artifacts(     root: &Path,     job_id: &str,     export_summary: Value, ) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - The "publish succeeded" cleanup. Writes authoring-project.json first, then if keepFullProcessArtifacts is false: removes dirs ["cache","preview","llm-suggestions"] (cleanup.rs:121) and files document-ir.json, split-candidates.json, pipeline-report.json, pipeline-report-summary.json, llm-last-suggestion.json, llm-calls.jsonl, vision-transcription-output.json, vision-answer-output.json, vision-transcription.txt, manual-transcription.txt, validation-report.json, publish-readiness-report.json (cleanup.rs:124-137), then cleanup_parser_cache_for_job. Sets job.status = JobStatus::Cleaned and current_step = WorkflowStep::Export (cleanup.rs:154-157). Returns CleanupSummaryV1 and also writes cleanup-summary.json ONLY in the retention branch (cleanup.rs:117).
- `minimize_process_artifacts_after_authoring` — `pub(crate) fn minimize_process_artifacts_after_authoring(     root: &Path,     job_id: &str,     reason: &str, ) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - Runs right after draft generation, NOT at publish. Same dir list; file list differs from the export variant: it ALSO deletes publish-readiness-report.json/validation-report.json but NOT pipeline-report-summary.json (compare cleanup.rs:124-137 vs 184-196). Does not change job status. Returns ArtifactMinimizationV1.
- `write_authoring_project` — `pub(crate) fn write_authoring_project(     root: &Path,     job_id: &str,     export_summary: Option<Value>, ) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - Hard dependency on V1: `let authoring: Value = read_json(&dir.join("authoring-ir.json"))?;` (cleanup.rs:45) uses read_json, not read_json_opt. If authoring-ir.json is absent this errors and BOTH cleanup entry points fail. Emits schemaVersion "AuthoringProjectV1" to jobs/<id>/authoring-project.json.
- `validation_summary` — `pub(crate) fn validation_summary(report: &Value) -> Value`
  - Also consumed by export_pack.rs:173.
- `DiagnosticsSettings` — `#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize, Clone, Default)] pub struct DiagnosticsSettings {     #[serde(rename = "keepFullProcessArtifacts")]     pub keep_full_process_artifacts: bool, }`
  - One field, defaults to false. Path: root.join("config").join("diagnostics-settings.json") (diagnostics.rs:12-14). Missing file returns Default (diagnostics.rs:18-20).
- `load_diagnostics_settings / write_diagnostics_settings` — `pub(crate) fn load_diagnostics_settings(root: &Path) -> CommandResult<DiagnosticsSettings> pub(crate) fn write_diagnostics_settings(     root: &Path,     settings: &DiagnosticsSettings, ) -> CommandResult<DiagnosticsSettings>`
  - Only three production readers: cleanup.rs:105, cleanup.rs:167, runtime_validation.rs:382 (where it gates writing publish-readiness-report.json).
- `environment_preflight_report` — `pub(crate) fn environment_preflight_report() -> Value`
  - Returns {schemaVersion:"EnvironmentPreflightV1", ok, errors, warnings, checks[], generatedAt}. `ok` = errors == 0 where errors counts only checks with severity=="error" (environment.rs:683-689) — and NO check in the file ever uses "error" (all are "warning"/"info"; see the severity list at environment.rs:321-673). So ok is unconditionally true. Fully synchronous: spawns node --version, every python candidate twice (once at 360-364, again inside resolve_python_command at 398), a pypdf import, and on macOS sips.
- `ResolvedCommand` — `#[derive(Debug, Clone)] pub(crate) struct ResolvedCommand {     pub(crate) program: String,     pub(crate) args: Vec<String>,     pub(crate) source: String, }`
  - Has fn display() -> String (environment.rs:26).
- `environment feature flags` — `pub(crate) fn document_ir_v2_shadow_enabled() -> bool { true } pub(crate) fn authoring_v2_shadow_enabled() -> bool { true } pub(crate) fn quality_gate_v2_enabled() -> bool { true }`
  - Hardcoded true, no env read. These three are the V2 kill switches the convergence relies on; they are already permanently on.
- `environment env-flag readers` — `pub(crate) fn runtime_gate_strict_mode() -> bool          // EPIC8_RUNTIME_GATE_STRICT, default true pub(crate) fn node_validator_diagnostics_enabled() -> bool // EPIC8_NODE_VALIDATOR_DIAGNOSTICS, default false pub(crate) fn cloud_pdf_vision_enabled() -> bool           // EPIC8_ENABLE_CLOUD_PDF_VISION, default false pub(crate) fn local_ocr_enabled() -> bool                  // EPIC8_ENABLE_LOCAL_OCR, default false pub(crate) fn pdf_renderer_setting() -> String             // EPIC8_PDF_RENDERER, default "auto"`
  - Every user-facing toggle the plan wants in Settings is currently an environment variable with no command to set it. pdf_renderer_setting accepts only auto|none|sips|pdfium|poppler|pymupdf (environment.rs:236-239).
- `SUPPORTED_LLM_PROVIDERS` — `const SUPPORTED_LLM_PROVIDERS: [&str; 4] = [     "OpenAiCompatible",     "AnthropicCompatible",     "Ollama",     "Custom", ];`
  - THE WRITE SIDE of the §7.14 mismatch. Enforced at llm_commands.rs:89-91: `if !SUPPORTED_LLM_PROVIDERS.contains(&input.provider.as_str()) { return Err(format!("unsupported_llm_provider:{}", input.provider)); }`
- `require_openai_compatible_provider` — `fn require_openai_compatible_provider(profile: &Value) -> CommandResult<()> {     let provider = profile         .get("provider")         .and_then(Value::as_str)         .map(str::trim)         .filter(|value| !value.is_empty())         .ok_or_else(|| "llm_provider_missing".to_string())?;     if !matches!(provider, "OpenAiCompatible" | "Ollama") {         return Err(format!(             "llm_provider_unsupported_for_openai_route:{}",             provider         ));     }     Ok(()) }`
  - THE READ SIDE of the §7.14 mismatch — the matches! is at llm_gateway.rs:146. Called only from openai_chat_completions_endpoint (llm_gateway.rs:156), which is called only from openai_post_once (llm_gateway.rs:378). All four gateway commands (classify_group/extract_group/test_profile, transcribe_pdf_images, extract_pdf_image_answers, generate_pdf_reading_outline — dispatch at llm_gateway.rs:36-48) go through it, so the gate is complete but fires at HTTP-request time, not at save time.

### 计划断言复核

| 判定 | 计划说 | 代码实际 | 证据 |
|---|---|---|---|
| partly-wrong | §7.14: "当前 UI 可选择 AnthropicCompatible 和 Custom，但 gateway 路由只接受 OpenAiCompatible 和 Ollama" | Directionally right but understated: the mismatch is not only in the UI, it is in the Rust write path. llm_commands.rs:78-83 accepts all four strings and persists them; llm_gateway.rs:146 rejects two of them at HTTP time. So the invalid state is durably storable, auto-selectable, and only reported as a free-text test-connection failure. | `src-tauri/src/llm_commands.rs:78-83 and :89-91 (accept) vs src-tauri/src/llm_gateway.rs:146-151 (reject); src/types/settings.ts:2 and src/pages/Settings.tsx:289-292 (UI union still 4-wide)` |
| wrong | §11.5: cleanup_on_startup deletes tmp jobs by age (completed 24h / failed 7d / orphaned 24h) and GCs unreferenced assets after a 7-day grace period | Does not exist in any form. The only startup work is lib.rs setup(): ensure_app_dirs plus library_commands::migrate_existing_into_library. No age tracking, no orphan detection, no asset reference counting anywhere in cleanup.rs. | `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:1408-1414 (whole setup hook); src-tauri/src/cleanup.rs:1-213 (no time or DB logic; grep for older_than/retention/prune over src-tauri/src returns only db.rs:781 prune_orphan_exams, unrelated to job dirs)` |
| partly-wrong | §11.5: cleanup_after_canonical_commit keeps only [source_if_retention_enabled, assets_referenced_by_canonical_ds, minimal_processing_summary] | cleanup_transient_job_artifacts is the nearest thing but is keyed to V1 export success, keeps a hardcoded list rather than a reference set, never inspects assets, and its own reported `kept` array is incomplete — it omits sources/, extraction/, assets/*, authoring/revisions/, authoring/patches/, export-history/, legacy/ and every *.shadow.* file, all of which are retained. | `src-tauri/src/cleanup.rs:121-139 (deletion lists), :148 (the inaccurate `kept` literal) vs src-tauri/src/util.rs:223-244 and src-tauri/src/artifact_store.rs:147-164 (the 13-15 dirs actually created per job)` |
| wrong | §11.5: cleanup_on_close_best_effort flushes editor buffers, marks running jobs interrupted, deletes disposable render cache — and "应用正常关闭不是唯一触发点" | No exit hook at all. lib.rs:1404-1477 registers no on_window_event and no RunEvent handler; tauri::Builder goes straight from invoke_handler to .run(). Nothing marks interrupted jobs and nothing deletes render cache on close or after a crash. | `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:1403-1478 (entire run(); grep for RunEvent/on_window_event in lib.rs returns nothing)` |
| partly-wrong | §17.17: "启动、成功、取消、退出四个触发点" | Two categories exist, neither of them the plan's four. Success is only V1 export (export_pack.rs:184/303, export_nas_library.rs:1516); the V2 publisher never calls cleanup and never touches job status at all, so once §17.24 retires V1 export the success trigger vanishes. There is no cancel path (no cancel command exists) and no startup/exit path. The de-facto third trigger the plan does not mention is "immediately after draft generation" (authoring_commands.rs:458, auto_pipeline.rs:2186). | `src-tauri/src/nas_package_v2.rs (grep cleanup\|minimize returns only its own local cleanup_transaction_artifacts at :1069; grep update_job\|JobStatus:: returns nothing); src-tauri/src/authoring_commands.rs:458; src-tauri/src/auto_pipeline.rs:2186` |
| wrong | §17.17: "清理失败记录日志，不中断用户关闭" | The opposite today. Every call site propagates with `?`, including inside `let _ = ...?`, so an I/O failure aborts the caller. remove_file_if_exists / remove_dir_if_exists turn any fs error into Err (util.rs:281-295). At export_pack.rs:184 the export files are already on disk and job.status is already Exported (export_pack.rs:180-183) when cleanup runs, so a single locked file makes a successful publish report failure. | `src-tauri/src/export_pack.rs:180-184; src-tauri/src/authoring_commands.rs:458; src-tauri/src/auto_pipeline.rs:2186; src-tauri/src/util.rs:281-295` |
| confirmed | §17.17: "当前 kept 列表不再固定保留 job/project/source-review/uploads/exports" | Confirmed — the list is a literal in the summary JSON and the retention behaviour is a literal in code, with no policy input. | `src-tauri/src/cleanup.rs:148 and :208` |
| wrong | §17.23: "环境检测在应用启动后台运行一次" | Not implemented. environment_preflight_report runs on demand from the settings page mount (SettingsPage.tsx:97) and nowhere else, and it runs synchronously on the Tauri async runtime — run_environment_preflight is the one command in this group that does NOT use spawn_blocking, while it spawns 5-7 subprocesses (node, each python candidate probed twice, pypdf import). | `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:1051-1054 vs :1089-1096 (test_llm_profile's spawn_blocking); src-tauri/src/job_commands.rs:278-280; src-tauri/src/environment.rs:316, 360-364, 398-399` |
| wrong | §17.23: "只有 blocking 项显示用户摘要" | Unimplementable against the current report. Not one of the 17 checks is emitted with severity "error" — all are "warning" or "info" — so report.ok is always true and the new page's blockingChecks filter (severity === "error") can never match. The alert block is dead code until the backend classifies blockers. | `src-tauri/src/environment.rs:321-673 (every severity literal) and :683-696 (errors/warnings counting); src/features/settings/SettingsPage.tsx:113-115, 201-203` |
| already-done | §17.23: "不让可选 Python/Poppler 缺失阻止 born-digital PDF 主链" | Already satisfied. python and python:pypdf are severity "warning" with messages that explicitly say Rust parsers are primary, and no gate reads the preflight result. | `src-tauri/src/environment.rs:370-396 (python), :410-426 (pypdf)` |
| partly-wrong | §14.2: "不受支持 provider -> 删除" | Done on the new page only. SettingsPage.tsx narrows to two providers with an explicit comment citing llm_gateway.rs:146, but src/types/settings.ts:2 still exports the 4-wide LlmProvider union that types SaveLlmProfileInput.provider, and the Rust allow-list is untouched — so the invalid value is still type-legal and still accepted. | `src/features/settings/SettingsPage.tsx:28-32 vs src/types/settings.ts:2 and src-tauri/src/llm_commands.rs:78-83` |
| partly-wrong | §14.2: advanced settings show 请求超时 / 云端并发数 / 本地并发数 / Ollama 模式 / 过程文件保留 / 环境诊断 | Only two of the six have a backend. Request timeout is profile.timeoutMs (clamped 1000..300000 at llm_gateway.rs:121-128) and 过程文件保留 is keepFullProcessArtifacts. Ollama is a provider string. Cloud and local concurrency have NO Rust representation whatsoever — no command, no config file; appSettings.ts keeps them in localStorage and its own comment says the backend will get them at P9. | `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:1415-1475 (full invoke_handler, no app-settings command); src/features/settings/appSettings.ts:5-7, 20-21` |
| partly-wrong | §14.3: "第一版普通用户只维护一个 active profile" | Cannot hold with the current store. When config/llm-profiles.json is absent load_profiles synthesizes the profile-local-placeholder entry, and save_llm_profile_core builds its new array from that same load_profiles result — so the very first real save persists the placeholder alongside the real profile, permanently. delete_llm_profile_core has the same effect. select_llm_profile has to special-case the id (auto_pipeline.rs:155) and the new UI has to filter it out by string (SettingsPage.tsx:26, 74). | `src-tauri/src/llm_profiles.rs:236-247; src-tauri/src/llm_commands.rs:152-161; src-tauri/src/auto_pipeline.rs:52,155; src/features/settings/SettingsPage.tsx:26,74` |
| already-done | §14.4: "不要在 Base URL 每输入一个字符时 700ms 自动保存并立即使配置生效" | Fixed by construction, not by deletion. The 700ms debounce still exists at src/pages/Settings.tsx:151-178, but that file is now unreferenced — no file under src/ imports "pages/Settings", and it is not in router.ts's LEGACY_PAGES list. The new page saves only on explicit 保存并测试. | `src/pages/Settings.tsx:151-178 (dead); src/app/App.tsx:5 imports only features/settings/SettingsPage; src/app/router.ts:11-24 (LEGACY_PAGES has no "settings")` |

But secretStorageBackend and secretStorageMessage are still injected into every profile the backend returns and still persisted into llm-profiles.json; only the new UI declines to render them. | `src-tauri/src/llm_profiles.rs:158-168; src-tauri/src/llm_commands.rs:149-150` |

Setting it to false changes no backend behaviour. | `src/features/settings/SettingsPage.tsx:277-279 and appSettings.ts:16-17 vs src-tauri/src/cleanup.rs:121-139, :184-198 (uploads/ and sources/ appear in neither deletion list; grep keep_source_files over src-tauri returns nothing)` |

LlmProfile is Vec<Value>, DiagnosticsSettings is a 1-field struct, EnvironmentPreflightV1 is an ad-hoc json! literal. | `F:/workspace/PDF2Test/contracts/ listing; src-tauri/src/schema/ listing (9 modules, none for settings)` |

### 现有测试方式
All tests for this subsystem are `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` inside src-tauri/src/lib.rs (136 #[test] fns starting at lib.rs:1480). cleanup.rs, environment.rs, diagnostics.rs, llm_commands.rs, llm_profiles.rs and llm_gateway.rs each contain zero test modules (verified by grepping for #[cfg(test)]). There is no src-tauri/tests/ integration directory.

Helpers to copy: `fn temp_test_root() -> PathBuf` (lib.rs:1576) returns env::temp_dir().join(format!("epic8-test-{}", Uuid::new_v4().simple())) and every test ends with `let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(root);`. `fn env_test_lock() -> &'static Mutex<()>` (lib.rs:1858) serializes tests that mutate process env — mandatory for anything touching EPIC8_*. `fn make_publishable_fixture(root: &Path) -> (ImportJob, Value)` (lib.rs:1863) builds a job that can reach the publish gate. `fn parser_fixture(name: &str)` (lib.rs:1580) resolves fixtures/parser/<name>. `fn live_llm_profile_from_env()` (lib.rs:1811) gates real-API tests behind EPIC8_LIVE_LLM_*.

Exact tests to extend or mirror:
- lib.rs:8085 `cleanup_respects_diagnostics_artifact_retention` — the only direct cleanup_transient_job_artifacts test. Asserts cleaned == false, cleanup-summary.json exists, document-ir.json / split-candidates.json / validation-report.json survive, and status stays Working. There is NO test of the cleaned == true branch of this function; add one.
- lib.rs:4438 `auto_pipeline_retains_process_artifacts_only_when_diagnostics_enabled` — retention-on path through run_auto_pipeline, including the <root>/cache/parser retention assertion (4479-4490).
- lib.rs:10061 `files_pdf_samples_auto_pipeline_minimizes_artifacts_and_preserves_review_gate` — the authoritative minimization contract. Enumerates the 8 files that must be gone (10159-10175), asserts cache/, preview/, llm-suggestions/ are gone (10176-10178), asserts no <root>/cache/parser entry is prefixed with the job id (10179-10193), and asserts authoring-ir.json / authoring-project.json / source-review.json survive (10144-10158). Requires exactly 4 PDFs in Files/ (asserts samples.len() == 4 at 10083-10087) and skips with a message if the dir is empty. Uses run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway with a closure gateway that panics if called — the pattern for testing without network.
- lib.rs:4381 `auto_pipeline_persists_llm_review_in_authoring_ir_after_minimization`.
- lib.rs:7566 `export_core_publish_gate_failure_writes_no_export_or_cleanup`.
- lib.rs:10517 `llm_profile_save_rejects_public_http_unknown_provider_and_accepts_localhost` — the provider/base-URL gate. Constructs SaveLlmProfileInput literals directly and asserts on error substrings (unsupported_llm_provider, llm_profile_base_url_insecure_http, llm_profile_base_url_credentials_in_url). This is the test to extend when narrowing SUPPORTED_LLM_PROVIDERS; it already covers "MistralNative" but not "AnthropicCompatible".
- lib.rs:2059 `environment_preflight_reports_required_dependency_names` — asserts schemaVersion plus 17 required check names, with cfg!(target_os) branches for renderer:macos-sips and renderer:windows-pdfium. Any preflight restructuring must keep these names or update this list. Note it asserts nothing about severity, which is how the all-"warning" state went unnoticed.
- lib.rs:2011 `secret_file_helpers_reject_unsafe_profile_ids`, lib.rs:2115 `plaintext_secret_fallback_is_disabled_by_default`, lib.rs:2137 `plaintext_secret_fallback_requires_explicit_opt_in` — secret storage; the last two take env_test_lock and set/remove EPIC8_ALLOW_PLAINTEXT_SECRET_FALLBACK.
- lib.rs:2040 `llm_cache_input_redacts_api_key` and lib.rs:2162 `make_llm_input_never_contains_api_key` — the two guards that keep secrets out of on-disk cache and gateway payloads. Any change to the profile payload shape must keep both green.

Not covered anywhere, and worth adding alongside the changes: the cleaned == true branch of cleanup_transient_job_artifacts; cleanup behaviour when authoring-ir.json is absent (V2-only job); cleanup error propagation on an undeletable file; any assertion that preflight severities are classifiable; any test of select_llm_profile's provider preference.

### 意外发现
- The provider-enum mismatch is worse than §7.14 describes. The plan frames it as a UI-only problem, but src-tauri/src/llm_commands.rs:78-83 accepts AnthropicCompatible and Custom and persists them to config/llm-profiles.json. auto_pipeline.rs:select_llm_profile (115-172) then treats provider=="OpenAiCompatible" as only the lowest of three tiebreaks, so an AnthropicCompatible profile that has an API key OUTRANKS an OpenAiCompatible one that does not, gets auto-selected, and fails in the gateway with llm_provider_unsupported_for_openai_route. Narrowing the frontend dropdown alone does not close this — an existing config file keeps the broken profile.
- The V2 publisher never triggers cleanup and never touches job status. nas_package_v2.rs contains no reference to cleanup::* (its cleanup_transaction_artifacts at :1069 is package-staging temp removal) and no reference to update_job or JobStatus at all. Every cleanup_transient_job_artifacts call site is V1 export (export_pack.rs:184/303, export_nas_library.rs:1516). So the moment §17.24 retires V1 export, the success-side cleanup trigger and the JobStatus::Cleaned transition both vanish with no compile error.
- No preflight check ever has severity "error". All 17 use "warning" or "info" (environment.rs:321-673), and report.ok is computed as errors == 0 (683-689) — so ok is unconditionally true. The new SettingsPage's blocking-item alert filters on severity === "error" (SettingsPage.tsx:113-115), which means §17.23's "only blocking items get a user summary" is currently implemented as "nothing is ever shown". The existing test (lib.rs:2059) only checks check names, never severities, which is why this went unnoticed.
- cleanup.rs's own reported `kept` array is factually wrong. cleanup.rs:148 lists 6 entries, but ensure_job_dirs (util.rs:223-244) and ensure_job_artifact_layout (artifact_store.rs:147-164) create 13-15 directories per job, and cleanup deletes only 3 of them. sources/, extraction/, assets/{blobs,metadata,previews}/, authoring/{revisions,patches}/, export-history/, legacy/ and every *.shadow.* sidecar (including document-ir-v2.shadow.overlay.svg) are silently retained. Anyone reading the summary JSON to reason about disk usage will be misled.
- `let _ = cleanup(...)?` does NOT swallow the error. The `?` binds to the call, so an I/O failure aborts the caller. At export_pack.rs:180-184 this happens after the export files are written and after job.status is already set to Exported — a single locked file turns a successful publish into a reported failure with the side effects already committed. §17.17's "cleanup failure must not interrupt" is currently inverted at all 8 call sites.
- Deleting a library item leaves the entire job directory on disk permanently. delete_library_exam_core (library_commands.rs:254-263) is a DB soft-delete only; delete_job_core (job_commands.rs:142-156) is the only path that removes jobs/<id>/. With no startup reaper (lib.rs setup() at 1408-1414 does only ensure_app_dirs + migrate_existing_into_library) and no exit hook (lib.rs:1404-1477 registers no RunEvent/on_window_event), trashed items keep their original PDFs and full revision history forever.
- environment.rs advertises four LLM capability fields that nothing reads and that save would erase anyway. environment.rs:552-557 emits profileCapabilityFields [supportsVisionImages, supportsPdfFileInput, maxPdfBytes, maxVisionImages]; a repo-wide grep finds no reader (the only other hit is an unrelated local constant at pdf_facts_shadow.rs:1083), and save_llm_profile_core's fixed 13-key literal (llm_commands.rs:137-151) would drop them on the next save. The vision and PDF-outline routes therefore run against whatever model is configured with no capability check.
- Only ONE real setting exists in Rust. config/diagnostics-settings.json holds a single bool. keepSourceFiles, nasDestination, localConcurrency, cloudConcurrency and developerMode live in localStorage (appSettings.ts:11-24) with no backend counterpart, and appSettings.ts's own header comment (lines 5-7) says so. Notably keepSourceFiles is already a no-op: cleanup never deletes uploads/ or sources/, and grep for keep_source_files over src-tauri returns nothing — so the §14.1 toggle changes no backend behaviour today.
- run_environment_preflight is the only command in this group that skips spawn_blocking (lib.rs:1051-1054) while being by far the most blocking — 5-7 synchronous subprocess spawns, with the python candidates probed twice because resolve_python_command at environment.rs:398 re-runs the probes already done at 360-364. On Windows `py -3 --version` with no Python installed can stall on the App Execution Alias. The settings page pays this on every mount (SettingsPage.tsx:97).
- src/pages/Settings.tsx is already dead code — no file under src/ imports "pages/Settings", and "settings" is absent from router.ts's LEGACY_PAGES (11-24), so unlike the other retired pages it is not even reachable via #/legacy/. It still holds the AnthropicCompatible/Custom options (289-292) and the 700ms autosave §14.4 objects to (151-178), which makes both look unfixed to anyone grepping.
- METHOD NOTE, not a code defect: src/features/settings/SettingsPage.tsx was being actively rewritten by another agent while I read it — 193 lines and brace-unbalanced at 14:32, 412 lines and complete at 14:38. I re-read it and my findings reflect the 412-line version. Anyone else reporting that file as truncated is seeing the same race, not a real bug.
- Two divergent copies of the base-URL security policy exist: llm_commands.rs:30-76 (save-side) and llm_gateway.rs:161-188 (request-side). The gateway copy additionally rejects URL fragments and handles IPv6 unicast-link-local; the save copy does not. A URL can therefore pass save and fail at request time for a shape reason rather than a connectivity one.

## ielts_grammar local recognition — src-tauri/src/ielts_grammar/ (entry points, V1-vs-V2 input reality, module breakdown, and which plan 6.8 hard-closure checks already exist)
**数据流**：Auto-import (the product path, auto_pipeline.rs:run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway, line 1381):

1. parse_source_document (auto_pipeline.rs:1449) writes document-ir.json — V1 DocumentIRV1. For PDF this is pdf_extract text only: parser.rs:535 pdf_extract::extract_text_by_pages, chunked per page by semantic_text_chunks (parser.rs:269, blank lines or the markers 'reading passage '/'questions '/'question '/'answer key'/'answers'), and every block gets a FABRICATED bbox from parser.rs:317-319 — [72, y0, 520, min(y0+36,794)] with y0 = 72 + (ordinal % 16) * 42 — on a hardcoded 595x842 page.

2. auto_pipeline.rs:1487-1519 writes document-ir-v2.shadow.json (DocumentIRV2, real geometry from pdf_geometry.rs) — but only when main_source_file(&job).filter(|s| s.file_type == "pdf"). DOCX gets nothing here.

3. auto_pipeline.rs:1675 make_dynamic_split_candidates(&job_id, &job, doc.as_ref()) -> split-candidates.json. Input is the V1 doc only (authoring_pipeline.rs:4193-4199 -> dynamic_document_blocks(doc)). DocumentIRV2 is not a parameter. Each questionGroupCandidate carries groupId, heading, instructionText, kindHint, range (block ids) and sectionEvidence[] built by split_section_evidence_for_blocks (authoring_pipeline.rs:542) whose textPreview is dynamic_block_text_preview = text.chars().take(120) (authoring_pipeline.rs:513-516).

4. auto_pipeline.rs:1684 make_dynamic_authoring_ir(&job, &split, doc.as_ref()) -> authoring-ir.json (V1 authoring: groups, passage.htmlBlocks, answerKey, questionOrder).

5. auto_pipeline.rs:2027 write_pipeline_authoring_v2_shadow -> write_authoring_v2_shadow -> build_authoring_v2_shadow(job, &ir, &split, doc.as_ref(), physical_shadow.as_ref()).

Inside build_authoring_v2_shadow (mod.rs:76):
- mod.rs:105-107 physical_lines = physical_shadow.map(semantic_lines_from_v2_shadow).unwrap_or_default()
- mod.rs:108 v1_lines = semantic_lines_from_v1_document(v1_document)
- mod.rs:109-113 fallback_lines = physical_lines if non-empty else v1_lines
- mod.rs:119 build_passage(...) — passage line set comes from split["passageCandidates"][0]["range"] block ids matched against v1_lines (mod.rs:428-442); fallbacks are v1_authoring./passage/htmlBlocks then visual_passage_lines(fallback_lines). Emits "paragraphMap": {} unconditionally (mod.rs:535).
- mod.rs:134-140 THE LOOP: for candidate in split["questionGroupCandidates"]
  - mod.rs:146 group_lines(candidate, v1_lines, fallback_lines, physical_lines, ...) builds the group's SemanticLine vector, in priority order: (a) candidate.sectionEvidence[] using evidence.textPreview + evidence.bbox (mod.rs:566-616), (b) v1_lines filtered by candidate range block ids (mod.rs:617-623), (c) the whole fallback_lines document (mod.rs:624-626). Then rebind_lines_to_physical (mod.rs:627) overwrites ONLY source_anchor and bbox by matching the alphanumeric-lowercase text key against consecutive physical lines on the same page, falling back to bbox intersection (mod.rs:635-687). line.text is never replaced.
  - mod.rs:184 heading_index, 190 collect_instruction_zone, 197 infer_instruction_signature (task type decided HERE), 205 detect_question_anchors, 207 detect_option_runs, 208 detect_option_bank / 218 detect_completion_option_bank, 273 recover_completion_structure, 284 build_responses_and_slots, 309 build_stimulus.
- mod.rs:375 quality::evaluate_quality(&authoring, physical_shadow) — the only place DocumentIRV2 geometry beyond flat lines is read, and only for the coverage ledger (quality.rs:2981 source_coverage_summary -> significant_physical_nodes 3311 over spans/lines/regions/glyphs/vectorPaths/tables/annotations/imagePlacements).
- mod.rs:377 refresh_group_quality, mod.rs:378 serde_json::from_value::<IeltsAuthoringIRV2> validation.

Same chain runs three more times per job: auto_pipeline.rs:201-208 (has_reliable_question_groups, for the vision-transcription gate), auto_pipeline.rs:2050 (the v2 quality gate), auto_pipeline.rs:2432-2440 (cloud review). Each rebuilds split + V1 authoring + the full V2 shadow from scratch.

Workspace save path (already converged per Phases 1-3): get_authoring_v2 -> get_authoring_v2_core (authoring_v2_commands.rs:54) -> build_editor_session (711) -> load_current_authoring (725) which reads the highest revision or falls back to authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json. apply_authoring_v2_patches -> apply_authoring_v2_patches_core (123) applies patches then refresh_quality_report (146) -> evaluate_quality (748) with document-ir-v2.shadow.json read directly. Recognition is NOT re-run on save.

### 关键签名
- `write_authoring_v2_shadow` — `pub(crate) fn write_authoring_v2_shadow(     job_dir: &Path,     job: &ImportJob,     v1_authoring: &Value,     split: &Value,     v1_document: Option<&Value>,     physical_shadow: Option<&Value>,     output_path: &Path, ) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - Thin wrapper: calls build_authoring_v2_shadow, writes authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json, then writes authoring-ir-v2.shadow.compare.json. Two call sites: auto_pipeline.rs:288, authoring_commands.rs:431.
- `build_authoring_v2_shadow` — `pub(crate) fn build_authoring_v2_shadow(     job: &ImportJob,     v1_authoring: &Value,     split: &Value,     v1_document: Option<&Value>,     physical_shadow: Option<&Value>, ) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - THE recognition entry point. Of its five inputs, only `split` (V1 split-candidates) is structurally required — the group loop at mod.rs:134 iterates split["questionGroupCandidates"]; an empty/missing array yields zero task groups. `physical_shadow` (DocumentIRV2) is Option and never drives structure. `v1_authoring` supplies answerKey, fixed options and passage htmlBlocks fallback. `v1_document` supplies V1 SemanticLines. Returns the full IeltsAuthoringIRV2 JSON, schema-validated at mod.rs:378.
- `evaluate_quality` — `pub(crate) fn evaluate_quality(authoring: &Value, physical_shadow: Option<&Value>) -> Value`
  - Second public entry point and the only one the converged workspace already calls: authoring_v2_commands.rs:748 inside refresh_quality_report. Operates on an already-built IeltsAuthoringIRV2 — it does NOT re-run recognition. Returns QualityReportV2 with state in {blocked, review_required, ready}.
- `SHADOW_ARTIFACT_FILE / SHADOW_COMPARE_FILE / SHADOW_ERROR_FILE` — `pub(crate) const SHADOW_ARTIFACT_FILE: &str = "authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json"; pub(crate) const SHADOW_COMPARE_FILE: &str = "authoring-ir-v2.shadow.compare.json"; pub(crate) const SHADOW_ERROR_FILE: &str = "authoring-ir-v2.shadow.error.json";`
  - Imported and aliased as AUTHORING_V2_SHADOW_* in auto_pipeline.rs:11-13 and authoring_commands.rs:13-15. authoring_v2_commands.rs:34 hardcodes the same string independently.
- `SemanticLine` — `pub(crate) struct SemanticLine {     pub id: String,     pub text: String,     pub source_anchor: Value,     pub page_index: i32,     pub order: usize,     pub role: String,     pub bbox: Option<[f64; 4]>, }`
  - The single substrate every recognizer consumes. This is the bottleneck: no spans, no style, no baseline, no indentation, no hanging indent, no region id, no column. Widening this struct is the highest-leverage single change for 6.5/6.6/6.7.
- `semantic_lines_from_v2_shadow` — `pub(crate) fn semantic_lines_from_v2_shadow(shadow: &Value) -> Vec<SemanticLine>`
  - The complete extent of DocumentIRV2's contribution to recognition: reads only pages[].pageIndex, pages[].lines[].text, .id, .bbox and .sourceAnchors. Everything else in PageNodeV2 (spans, glyphs, regions, tables, vectorPaths, readingOrder, indentation_pt, hanging_indent_pt, baseline, style) is discarded here.
- `semantic_lines_from_v1_document` — `pub(crate) fn semantic_lines_from_v1_document(document: Option<&Value>) -> Vec<SemanticLine>`
  - Projects V1 pages[].blocks[] (text or html), using blockId as the line id and the V1 block bbox.
- `collect_instruction_zone` — `pub(crate) fn collect_instruction_zone(     lines: &[SemanticLine],     heading_index: usize,     expected_numbers: &[u32], ) -> InstructionZone`
  - Scans forward from heading_index, stopping at is_task_boundary / is_option_run_start / is_new_task_heading (243-261). Returns text, line_ids, source_anchors, start/end index, confidence, warnings.
- `detect_question_anchors` — `pub(crate) fn detect_question_anchors(     lines: &[SemanticLine],     expected_numbers: &[u32], ) -> Vec<QuestionAnchor>`
  - Line-first, exactly as 6.5 describes the current state. parse_leading_question_number (anchors.rs:59) takes up to 3 leading ASCII digits off the trimmed line, scores 0.62 for a bare number / 0.72 unpunctuated / 0.90 with . ) : , then filters by expected_numbers.contains(&number). No span iteration, no bbox scoring, no header/footer or year/measurement rejection.
- `assemble_prompt` — `pub(crate) fn assemble_prompt(     candidate_prompt: Option<&str>,     question_number: u32,     question_anchor: Option<&QuestionAnchor>,     next_question_anchor: Option<&QuestionAnchor>,     lines: &[SemanticLine],     fallback_anchor: Option<Value>, ) -> PromptResult`
  - Short-circuits on the V1 candidate prompt string when it is non-empty and not a known placeholder (24-40). Geometric path is a plain line-index slice lines[start..option_start] where option_start comes from detect_option_runs. source_coverage is binary 1.0/0.0, not a ratio.
- `detect_option_runs` — `pub(crate) fn detect_option_runs(lines: &[SemanticLine]) -> Vec<OptionRun>`
  - parse_option_line (146) requires the label to be the leading token of line.text (single uppercase letter, or lowercase roman i/v/x). One continuation absorb only: an empty-text label absorbs the immediately next non-label prose line (26-46). No x-column stability, no hanging-indent matching, no multi-line wrap.
- `detect_option_bank` — `pub(crate) fn detect_option_bank(     lines: &[SemanticLine],     instruction_text: &str,     allow_reuse: Option<bool>,     matching_semantics: bool, ) -> Option<OptionBankCandidate>`
  - Returns None unless the instruction contains one of 6 literal 'list of ...'/'choose from the box' markers OR matching_semantics is true (option_bank.rs:33). Then takes the first complete run with >=2 options.
