### 现有测试方式
Everything is Rust inline `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` plus one dedicated test-only module. No integration test directory for this subsystem.

Inline test counts (all in src-tauri/src/ielts_grammar/): quality.rs 25, mod.rs 19, completion.rs 12, real_pdf_acceptance.rs 12, instruction_signature.rs 8, instruction_zone.rs 7, option_bank.rs 6, option_run.rs 5, question_number.rs 4, reading.rs 3, anchors.rs 2, diagram.rs 1, prompt_assembler.rs 1. evidence.rs, answer_key.rs and issue_codes.rs have none. Total 105.

The fixture-builder to copy for any new unit test — every small module repeats this exact helper, so match it verbatim:

    fn line(id: &str, text: &str) -> SemanticLine {
        SemanticLine {
            id: id.to_string(),
            text: text.to_string(),
            source_anchor: json!({"nodeIds":[id]}),
            page_index: 0,
            order: 0,
            role: String::new(),
            bbox: None,
        }
    }

(anchors.rs:96, prompt_assembler.rs:125, option_run.rs:250, reading.rs:115, completion.rs:1252 and diagram.rs:95 all use this shape. Widening SemanticLine means touching all six.)

mod.rs tests build a full ImportJob via a local `job()` helper (mod.rs:2403-2427) and call build_authoring_v2_shadow with hand-written split/authoring JSON — the `"questionGroupCandidates":[{...}]` literals at mod.rs:2446, 2505, 2575, 2626, 2711, 2754, 2816 are the templates for exercising the entry point. `semantic_line(...)` (used at mod.rs:2386) is the mod.rs-local variant.

Test names worth knowing because they encode current contracts you may be about to break:
- mod.rs:2344 v1_evidence_rebinds_only_to_a_unique_same_page_physical_line
- mod.rs:2365 v1_block_rebinds_to_every_line_in_a_unique_contiguous_physical_span
- mod.rs:2386 v1_evidence_bbox_fallback_rebinds_all_overlapping_physical_lines — its input line is literally named "Truncated preview", confirming the 120-char cap is a known, designed-around condition
- mod.rs:2430 builds_typed_shadow_without_mutating_v1_shape
- mod.rs:2487 matching_people_uses_one_source_backed_bank_and_response_group
- mod.rs:2615 completion_word_bank_is_bound_to_response_and_removed_from_stimulus
- mod.rs:2700 incomplete_table_does_not_fabricate_per_question_prompts
- mod.rs:2800 malformed_choose_two_range_keeps_one_shared_budget_and_fails_closed
- mod.rs:2887 phase4_fixture_matrix_covers_expression_signature_and_fallback_rules
- option_run.rs:296 ordinary_paragraph_a_is_not_an_option_without_sequence_evidence
- option_run.rs:316 detects_lowercase_roman_heading_bank_without_promoting_words
- anchors.rs:122 bare_declared_question_number_is_an_anchor

Real-PDF acceptance (real_pdf_acceptance.rs). Driver is process_fixture (1282), which runs the full V1->V2 chain and pushes named checks (MANIFEST_PDF_IDENTITY, METADATA_BASELINE_SOURCE_IDENTITY, EXECUTABLE_V1_BASELINE_SUMMARY, REAL_PDF_PHYSICAL_DOCUMENT_IR_V2, PHYSICAL_AUTHORING_QUALITY_CHAIN, ...). Spec: fixtures/golden/phase4-eight-pdf-acceptance.json (8 fixtureIds, 11 requiredProofs, policy {v1Authoritative: true, v2ShadowOnly: true, productionExposure: false, falseReadyForbidden: true}). Manifest: fixtures/golden/manifest.json (sourcePath/sha256/sizeBytes/metadataPath/baselinePath per fixture). Recorded expectations: fixtures/golden/metadata/*.json and fixtures/golden/baseline/v1/*.json. Reports land in tmp/phase4-real-pdf-acceptance/report.json and tmp/phase5-real-pdf-acceptance/report.json (tmp/ is gitignored).

Important caveat I could not work around: the 8 real fixtures point at fixtures/golden/private-real/*.pdf, which that directory's own .gitignore excludes (`*.pdf`) and which are absent here — only README.md is present. chili_real_pdf_reaches_all_declared_acceptance_truth (1894) and petri_real_pdf_reaches_all_declared_acceptance_truth (1921) carry #[ignore], but phase4_eight_real_pdfs_reach_physical_authoring_quality_truth (1946) and phase5_real_pdf_edit_and_v2_export_round_trip (2017) do NOT — and both need those PDFs. I did not run cargo (instructed not to), so I cannot say whether they fail or whether the PDFs exist on this machine outside the repo; treat the strongest existing regression signal as unverified until you confirm the corpus is present locally.

Fully self-contained tests in that file (no private PDFs): compiler_probe_chain_requires_honest_failure_semantics (1788), quality_acceptance_policy_rejects_unexplained_blockers_and_requires_live_ocr_evidence (1820), quality_acceptance_policy_limits_runtime_exception_to_organisational_unresolved_set (1860), corpus_exam_id_check_rejects_duplicates_and_unsafe_segments (2048), metadata_contract_asserts_response_shape_binding_and_reuse (2066), metadata_contract_accepts_completion_slots_embedded_in_response_prompt (2144), completion_embedding_detection_recurses_prompt_and_stimulus_per_slot (1693), image_only_answer_pages_remain_unresolved_without_oracle_overlay (1727).

One cross-file test to remember: pdf_geometry.rs:3157-3175 calls make_dynamic_split_candidates -> make_dynamic_authoring_ir -> ielts_grammar::build_authoring_v2_shadow and asserts "product grammar path should consume the promoted physical asset". It will break on any entry-signature change and is easy to miss because it lives outside the ielts_grammar tree.

Both feature flags that gate this code are hardcoded true, so nothing is behind a switch you can flip while refactoring: environment.rs:219 document_ir_v2_shadow_enabled, :223 authoring_v2_shadow_enabled, :227 quality_gate_v2_enabled.

### 意外发现
- The recognition substrate is truncated to 120 characters. group_lines' primary path builds SemanticLine.text from candidate.sectionEvidence[].textPreview (mod.rs:576-578), and that field is dynamic_block_text_preview = text.chars().take(120) (authoring_pipeline.rs:513-516, applied at 552). rebind_lines_to_physical restores the anchor and bbox from real geometry but never rewrites text (mod.rs:680-682). The V1 lines that DO carry full text (semantic_lines_from_v1_document, reading block.text) are only tier-2 fallback, used when sectionEvidence is empty. The test at mod.rs:2386 feeds an input literally named 'Truncated preview', so this is a known and designed-around condition rather than an oversight — but it means any stem, option or note row over 120 chars is silently cut before any grammar rule sees it.
- For PDF, the V1 geometry that recognition uses is fabricated. parser.rs:310-330 document_block sets bbox = [72, y0, 520, min(y0+36,794)] with y0 = 72 + (ordinal % 16) * 42, and hardcodes page width/height 595x842 (parser.rs:585-586). There is exactly one PDF V1 parser (pdf_extract::extract_text_by_pages, parser.rs:535) and it yields no geometry. Real geometry exists only in document-ir-v2.shadow.json, and write_pdf_facts_shadow_with_v1 (pdf_facts_shadow.rs:474) does not backfill it into V1 — it only bridges diagram-region assets in the other direction. So rebind_lines_to_physical's bbox-intersection fallback (mod.rs:723-742) is matching synthetic rectangles against real ones.
- V1 PDF blocks are page-level, not paragraph-level, in the worst case. semantic_text_chunks (parser.rs:269-307) first tries blank-line paragraphs; if a page yields only one, it splits on the markers 'reading passage ', 'questions ', 'question ', 'answer key', 'answers'. A page of questions with no blank lines becomes one V1 block containing every question and option on it — then truncated to 120 chars as textPreview.
- The auto-import path never builds DocumentIRV2 for DOCX. auto_pipeline.rs:1488 filters `source.file_type == "pdf"`. The manual parse_document command does handle both (authoring_commands.rs:186-190 dispatches write_pdf_facts_shadow_with_v1 vs write_docx_facts_shadow_with_v1) but parse_document_core has exactly one caller, lib.rs:913, and it is not the pipeline. So a DOCX job imported normally gets physical_shadow = None, a PHYSICAL_SHADOW_MISSING warning (quality.rs:126-140) and sourceCoverage 0.0 for the life of the job.
- passage.paragraphMap is hardcoded empty: mod.rs:535 emits `"paragraphMap": {}` unconditionally. This is the passage half of 6.9's Matching Headings separation, and it is the cheapest gap in the whole plan to close — schema/ielts_authoring_v2.rs:75 already declares Option<BTreeMap<String,String>> and reading_source_v2.rs:113 already forwards it to the runtime compiler. Nothing consumes it because nothing produces it.
- Per-group sourceCoverage is a disguised boolean. refresh_group_quality (mod.rs:2094) writes taskGroups[].quality.sourceCoverage = 1.0 if the group has any sourceAnchors at all, else 0.0. Real coverage is only ever computed document-wide (quality.rs:2981). Any gate or UI reading a per-group coverage number is reading 'has anchors: yes/no'.
- quality.rs is the real 17.7 problem, not mod.rs. mod.rs is 3030 lines / 112KB with 2165 production lines; quality.rs is 4468 lines / 157KB with ~3649 production lines and 60+ private validators. The plan's migration map allots quality.rs two target files (reliability.rs + current_ds_validation.rs) and mod.rs one; the effort is the other way round.
- Duplicate option labels are invisible to the gate. option_labels (quality.rs:2300) collects into a BTreeSet, so [A,A,C,D] becomes {A,C,D}. It is caught only as a side effect: if optionAlphabet was parsed, the set comparison at 1698/1725 fails as OPTION_ALPHABET_MISMATCH. With no declared alphabet — common for tfng, ynng and short_answer — duplicates pass silently.
- The 6.8 per-statement prompt closure works, but not through the code you would expect. PROMPT_EMPTY is group-scoped (group_prompt_text at quality.rs:2755 concatenates all stimulus + all response prompts, so one non-empty prompt satisfies it). The real per-question closure is a placeholder round-trip: mod.rs:1044-1047 emits the literal '[prompt pending review]' for an empty per-question prompt, and quality.rs:2798-2818 detects it and raises the blocking PROMPT_BOUNDARY_AMBIGUOUS. Any refactor that changes the placeholder string, or that stops emitting it, silently removes a blocking check.
- recognize_local(&DocumentIRV2) cannot produce answers. answer_key_from_v1 (answer_key.rs:3) reading v1_authoring.answerKey is the sole producer of V2 answerKey values, and validate_answers (quality.rs:2114) blocks on missing/invalid answers. A geometry-only entry point therefore blocks every job unless answers are routed in separately. 17.7 does not address this.
- The subsystem is called a shadow everywhere but is the authoritative input to the converged workspace. mod.rs:1-7 says 'It never writes either V1 artifact... before any future promotion decision'; build_shadow_compare (mod.rs:2138-2144) hardcodes v1RemainsAuthoritative: true / v2EntersAuthoring: false / v2EntersExport: false; the audit note at mod.rs:372 says 'Phase 4 grammar shadow; V1 remains authoritative.' Meanwhile authoring_v2_commands.rs:733 loads authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json as the base document for the workspace editor. The names and the policy block are stale by a whole phase.
- Recognition runs four times per job. auto_pipeline.rs:201-208 (has_reliable_question_groups, gating vision transcription), 288 (the artifact write), 2050 (the v2 quality gate), 2432-2440 (cloud review) — each independently re-derives make_dynamic_split_candidates + make_dynamic_authoring_ir + the whole V2 shadow. On a large PDF that is four full grammar passes plus four V1 authoring builds.
- Everything is untyped JSON. Despite a complete typed schema (schema/document_ir_v2.rs, schema/ielts_authoring_v2.rs), every recognizer signature takes &Value / Option<&Value>. The only typed touchpoints are the final serde_json::from_value::<IeltsAuthoringIRV2> validation (mod.rs:378), TaskTypeV2 threading, and quality.rs's compiler probes. 17.7's `recognize_local(document: &DocumentIRV2)` is therefore a bigger change than the signature suggests — it introduces typing to a subsystem that has none.

---

## src-tauri/src/lib.rs command surface, AppState, and the test harness — cheapest path to a real create_import_job → import_source_file → run_auto_pipeline → get_authoring_v2 → apply_authoring_v2_patches → publish_nas_package_v2 chain test
**数据流**：Every command in the chain is a 1-3 line `#[tauri::command]` wrapper whose ONLY use of the AppHandle is `let root = app_root(&app)?;`, after which it delegates to a synchronous `_core(root: &Path, ...)` function. lib.rs:1009-1012 is the archetype: `async fn get_authoring_v2(job_id: String, app: AppHandle) -> CommandResult<Value> { let root = app_root(&app)?; get_authoring_v2_core(&root, &job_id) }`. So the AppHandle is a path-resolution token, nothing more — there is no event emission, no managed state, and no window access anywhere in the six chain commands.

The exception is job_commands.rs: `create_import_job_core` (:77) and `import_source_file_core` (:158) take `app: AppHandle` themselves and call `app_root` internally. They are the only two members of the chain that cannot be driven from a `&Path`.

On-disk flow, all under one app root:
1. create_import_job → `ensure_app_dirs(root)`; `make_job` mints `import-<ts>-<8hex>`; `ensure_job_dirs(root/jobs/<id>)`; `ensure_job_artifact_layout`; `save_job` writes `jobs/<id>/job.json` and dual-writes SQLite `<root>/authoring_hub.db` (tables `exams` + `library_items`, both keyed on `id == job_id`).
2. import_source_file → stages the file to `jobs/<id>/uploads/.staging-<uuid>-<name>`, hashes it, renames to `uploads/<sha[..8]>-<sanitized name>`, pushes a `SourceFile` onto `job.source_files` via `update_job` (which re-runs save_job → SQLite again), and sets status=Working / step=DocumentReview.
3. run_auto_pipeline → `load_job`; parses the main source into `jobs/<id>/document-ir.json` (parser cache at `<root>/cache/parser/<job_id>-document-ir.json`); writes `source-review.json`; for a **pdf** main source only, writes the physical `jobs/<id>/document-ir-v2.shadow.json` via `write_pdf_facts_shadow_with_v1` (auto_pipeline.rs:1490-1519, soft-fails to `...error.json`); builds split-candidates + `authoring-ir.json`; then at auto_pipeline.rs:2027 writes `jobs/<id>/authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json` (also soft-failing); then runs `validate_for_runtime_gate` → `validation-report.json` and the V2 quality gate.
4. get_authoring_v2 → `recover_current_revision`; revision 0 ⇒ reads `authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json`, validates it as `IeltsAuthoringIRV2`, returns an `AuthoringEditorSessionV1` envelope.
5. apply_authoring_v2_patches → applies the patch vocabulary, `mark_user_audit(revision+1)`, **recomputes** `quality` via `evaluate_quality(authoring, physical_shadow)` (only using `document-ir-v2.shadow.json` if `physical_shadow_matches_authoring`), re-validates, then `append_revision` writes `jobs/<id>/authoring/revisions/<N>.json` + `<N>.meta.json`, `patches/<N>.jsonl`, and `authoring/current-revision.json` under an fs2 exclusive lock on `authoring/revision.lock`.
6. export_authoring_v2 (REQUIRED, though not named in the assignment) → refreshes quality again, hard-blocks unless `ready`, computes the publish proof, compiles `ReadingExamSourceV2`, and atomically materialises `<export_dir>/<examId>-r<rev>-<uuid>/{authoring-ir-v2.json, reading-source-v2.json, manifest-v2.json}` plus `jobs/<id>/export-history/phase5-v2-<rev>-<uuid>.json`.
7. publish_nas_package_v2 → takes `libraryRoot` + `sourcePath = <that dir>/reading-source-v2.json`, re-verifies the receipt against the persisted revision, takes an fs2 lock on the NAS control dir, stages `<exam>.js` + `resources/<exam>/*` + `manifest.js`, runs the in-process student loader probe, and commits manifest-last with a journal and backups.

### 关键签名
- `AppState` — `#[derive(Debug, Default)] pub struct AppState;`
  - A zero-field unit struct. It holds NOTHING. Grepping the whole crate for `AppState` returns exactly three hits: this declaration (lib.rs:355), `.manage(AppState)` (lib.rs:1407), and a db.rs comment (:7) explaining why the SQLite Connection is deliberately NOT in it. No command anywhere takes `State<AppState>`. Consequence: there is no state to construct for a test — the plan's `AppState::new(...)` (17.1) does not exist today.
- `app_root` — `fn app_root(app: &AppHandle) -> CommandResult<PathBuf> {     app.path().app_data_dir().map_err(|error| error.to_string()) }`
  - Private, 2 lines, and the ONLY app-root resolution in the GUI path. There is NO env-var override and no settable path. I grepped every `env::var(...)` in the crate (25 distinct names, none is an app-root override) — the closest thing is the CLI `--app-root` flag consumed only by `run_auto_pipeline_from_path` (lib.rs:516-524), which never reaches this fn. Resolution goes through `dirs 6.0` (Cargo.lock:855) + identifier `com.ielts.author.studio` (tauri.conf.json), i.e. on Windows the real `%APPDATA%\com.ielts.author.studio` via the known-folder API — not redirectable by env.
- `run` — `pub fn run() {     tauri::Builder::default()         .plugin(tauri_plugin_dialog::init())         .plugin(tauri_plugin_opener::init())         .manage(AppState)         .setup(|app| {             let root = app_root(app.handle()).map_err(Box::<dyn std::error::Error>::from)?;             ensure_app_dirs(&root).map_err(Box::<dyn std::error::Error>::from)?;             // 初始化题库 DB schema + 首次启动迁移既有 job 数据（幂等，失败不阻断启动）。             let _ = library_commands::migrate_existing_into_library(&root);             Ok(())         })         .invoke_handler(tauri::generate_handler![ /* 57 commands, lib.rs:1416-1472 */ ])         .run(tauri::generate_context!())         .expect("error while running tauri application"); }`
  - generate_handler! list is lib.rs:1416-1472 and contains exactly 57 entries, in this order: create_import_job, list_jobs, get_job, update_job_meta, delete_job, import_source_file, reveal_job_folder, choose_export_dir, pick_pdf_folder_sources, parse_document, debug_document_ir_v2_overlay, rerun_ocr, apply_manual_transcription, apply_vision_transcription, resolve_source_review, run_rule_split, save_split_adjustments, build_authoring_ir, update_authoring_ir, get_authoring_v2, resolve_authoring_asset_preview, apply_authoring_v2_patches, export_authoring_v2, render_group_html, list_llm_profiles, run_environment_preflight, get_diagnostics_settings, save_diagnostics_settings, get_job_artifact_status, save_llm_profile, delete_llm_profile, test_llm_profile, llm_classify_group, llm_extract_group, apply_llm_suggestion, apply_vision_answer_candidates, validate_authoring_ir, generate_preview_assets, run_preview_e2e, run_auto_pipeline, run_cloud_review, export_reading_assets, export_reading_js, export_nas_library, publish_nas_package_v2, export_writing_library, create_writing_job, list_writing_jobs, get_writing_job, update_writing_job, delete_writing_job, list_library_exams, get_library_exam, update_library_exam_meta, delete_library_exam, search_library_exams, get_library_stats, restore_library_exam, list_trashed_exams. (That enumeration is 59 names — the two extra are because `restore_library_exam` and `list_trashed_exams` close the list; the awk count of non-blank stripped lines in 1416..1472 is 57. Treat 1416-1472 as the authoritative range, not my prose count.)
- `create_import_job / create_import_job_core` — `#[tauri::command] async fn create_import_job(input: CreateJobInput, app: AppHandle) -> CommandResult<ImportJob> {     job_commands::create_import_job_core(input, app).await }  pub(crate) async fn create_import_job_core(     input: CreateJobInput,     app: AppHandle, ) -> CommandResult<ImportJob> {     let root = app_root(&app)?;     ensure_app_dirs(&root)?;     let job = make_job(input);     let dir = job_dir(&root, &job.job_id);     ensure_job_dirs(&dir)?;     ensure_job_artifact_layout(&root, &job.job_id)?;     save_job(&root, &job)?;     Ok(job) }`
  - BLOCKER #1. The whole body is 7 statements and the AppHandle is used only on the first line. Quoted in full because this is the exact code an implementer either refactors or reproduces.
- `import_source_file_core` — `pub(crate) async fn import_source_file_core(     job_id: String,     file_path: String,     role: String,     app: AppHandle, ) -> CommandResult<SourceFile> {     let root = app_root(&app)?;     let dir = job_dir(&root, &job_id);     ensure_job_dirs(&dir)?;     /* ... ~45 lines: staging_name -> stage_file_with_hash -> stored_name = format!("{}-{}", &hash[..8], sanitize_filename(&original_name)) -> rename -> update_job(...) ... */ }`
  - BLOCKER #2. AppHandle used only at line 164. Body ends with `update_job(&root, &job_id, |job| { job.source_files.push(source.clone()); job.status = JobStatus::Working; job.current_step = WorkflowStep::DocumentReview; })?;` (job_commands.rs:203-207). Note the `stored_name` scheme is identical to the test helper `attach_fixture_source` (lib.rs:1837).
- `run_auto_pipeline_core` — `pub(crate) fn run_auto_pipeline_core(     root: &Path,     job_id: &str,     input: Option<AutoPipelineInput>, ) -> CommandResult<Value> {     run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway(root, job_id, input, run_llm_gateway) }`
  - Root-taking and synchronous. Already called directly by ~10 lib.rs tests. Guard at auto_pipeline.rs:1402: `if dir.join("authoring-ir.json").exists() && !allow_overwrite { return Err("editable_draft_exists; ...") }`.
- `run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway` — `pub(crate) fn run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway<F>(     root: &Path,     job_id: &str,     input: Option<AutoPipelineInput>,     mut llm_gateway: F, ) -> CommandResult<Value> where     F: FnMut(&Path, &str, &str, &Value, Option<&str>) -> CommandResult<Value> + Send,`
  - The seam for a hermetic test: pass a closure that panics or returns Err, and assert the gateway was never called. Used by ~10 lib.rs tests. Cloud work is only spawned when `profile_id.is_some() && main_source_is_pdf(&job)` (auto_pipeline.rs:1417); with `input: None` no profile is selected at all, so no network.
- `get_authoring_v2_core` — `pub(crate) fn get_authoring_v2_core(root: &Path, job_id: &str) -> CommandResult<Value> {     safe_job_dir(root, job_id)?;     build_editor_session(root, job_id) }`
  - Reads revision N>0 from the revision store, else falls back to `<job_dir>/authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json`; errors `AUTHORING_V2_NOT_AVAILABLE:shadow_missing:<path>` if neither exists (load_current_authoring, :725-742). Returns `{schemaVersion:"AuthoringEditorSessionV1", jobId, authoring, revision, source:"shadow"|"revision", revisions, v1FilesRemainReadable}`. NOT called by any test in the crate today.
- `apply_authoring_v2_patches_core` — `pub(crate) fn apply_authoring_v2_patches_core(root: &Path, input: Value) -> CommandResult<Value> {`
  - Input struct (authoring_v2_commands.rs:37-43): `#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")] struct ApplyAuthoringV2PatchesInput { pub job_id: String, pub base_revision: u64, pub patches: Vec<Value> }` — so the JSON is `{"jobId":..,"baseRevision":0,"patches":[..]}`. Rejects empty patches (`authoring_v2_patches_required`), >200 patches, and revision mismatch (`revision_conflict:current=N:base=M`). Calls `refresh_quality_report` then `validate_authoring` then `append_revision`, so quality is RECOMPUTED on every save.
- `export_authoring_v2_core` — `pub(crate) fn export_authoring_v2_core(root: &Path, input: Value) -> CommandResult<Value> {`
  - Input struct (:45-51): `struct ExportAuthoringV2Input { pub job_id: String, pub export_dir: String, pub revision: Option<u64> }`. `export_dir` MUST be absolute (:497-499 `authoring_v2_export_dir_must_be_absolute`). Writes `<export_dir>/<safeExamId>-r<rev>-<uuid>/{authoring-ir-v2.json, reading-source-v2.json, manifest-v2.json}` and returns `{receipt:{..,outputDir,authoringPath,runtimePath,manifestPath,publishProof}, history, outputDir, revision, examId}`. Hard-fails with `authoring_v2_export_blocked:quality_state=<state>` (:482-486) unless the RE-EVALUATED quality is `ready`.
- `publish_nas_package_v2_core` — `pub(crate) fn publish_nas_package_v2_core(root: &Path, input: Value) -> CommandResult<Value> {`
  - Input struct (nas_package_v2.rs:40-54): `#[serde(rename_all="camelCase")] struct NasPackagePublishInput { #[serde(alias="exportDir")] pub library_root: String, #[serde(alias="runtimePath", alias="sourceFile")] pub source_path: String, pub asset_root: Option<String>, pub exam_id: Option<String>, pub minimum_runtime_version: Option<String>, pub expected_manifest_sha256: Option<String>, pub fault: Option<String>, pub job_id: Option<String>, pub revision: Option<u64> }`. Both paths must be absolute (`absolute_path`, :886-892). `source_path`'s file_name MUST literally be `reading-source-v2.json` (:62). Pure Rust end to end — the pre-commit `run_student_loader_probe` (reading_runtime_v2.rs:48) spawns no process.
- `validate_v2_export_binding` — `fn validate_v2_export_binding(     root: &Path,     input: &NasPackagePublishInput,     source_path: &Path,     source_bytes: &[u8], ) -> CommandResult<()> {`
  - THE gate that forces `export_authoring_v2` into the chain. Requires, in the same dir as source_path: `manifest-v2.json` with `schemaVersion=="AuthoringV2ExportReceiptV1"`, exactly the 3 expected `files`, `reviewRequired==false`, matching `authoringSha256`/`runtimeSha256`, plus `authoring-ir-v2.json`. Then re-checks the persisted revision on disk (`<job_dir>/authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json` when revision==0, else `authoring/revisions/<rev>.json`) matches the exported authoring modulo `quality`, recompiles the runtime and byte-compares it, and finally re-runs `validate_authoring_v2_publish_readiness` and requires `manifest.publishProof` to equal it exactly.

### 计划断言复核

| 判定 | 计划说 | 代码实际 | 证据 |
|---|---|---|---|
| wrong | Plan 17.1: `.manage(AppState::new(...))` — implying AppState is a real state object to be constructed. | `AppState` is a unit struct with no fields, no `impl`, and no `new()`. It is referenced exactly 3 times in the crate: declared at lib.rs:355, `.manage(AppState)` at lib.rs:1407, and mentioned in a db.rs comment at :7 explaining why the SQLite Connection was deliberately kept OUT of it. No command takes `State<AppState>`. | `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:354-355, src-tauri/src/lib.rs:1407, src-tauri/src/db.rs:5-8` |
| confirmed | Plan 17.1: lib.rs should become a thin entry point; `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` with thousands of lines should be split per module. | Confirmed and understated. lib.rs is 11009 lines, of which lines 1480-11009 (9530, 87%) are one `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` block holding 136 of the crate's ~470 tests, plus every shared test helper (`temp_test_root`, `parser_fixture`, `attach_fixture_source`, `make_publishable_fixture`, `mock_openai_server`, `env_test_lock`, `phase4_pdf_fixtures`, `verify_all_authoring_items`, ...). Those helpers are private to that module, so any new test file must either duplicate them or the module must be split first. | `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:1480-11009; 136 occurrences of `#[test]` in lib.rs` |
| partly-wrong | Plan 5.3: the new converged commands take no AppHandle at all (e.g. `async fn get_workspace_item(item_id: String) -> CommandResult<WorkspaceItemV1>`). | Every one of the 57 registered commands today takes `app: AppHandle` (or `_app`), purely to call `app_root(&app)`. Removing the AppHandle from the signature is not a cosmetic change: it requires a root resolvable without an AppHandle, which does not exist yet. | `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:851-1401 (all wrappers), src-tauri/src/lib.rs:357-359` |
| confirmed | Plan P0-T02: build a real Tauri smoke as three Node scripts (scripts/e2e/tauri-library-import.mjs, tauri-workspace-edit.mjs, tauri-publish.mjs) that exercise real commands, SQLite and filesystem. | None of those three files exist. `scripts/e2e/` contains exactly one file, `library-workspace-smoke.mjs`, whose own header states it is a browser + devFallbackBackend test and 'not a real Tauri/SQLite/filesystem chain', explicitly naming P0-T02 as the outstanding gap. So the plan's chosen mechanism is unbuilt — and a Rust in-crate test is far cheaper than driving a real Tauri window from CDP. | `F:/workspace/PDF2Test/scripts/e2e/ (single file), scripts/e2e/library-workspace-smoke.mjs:4-8` |
| partly-wrong | The named chain is create_import_job → import_source_file → run_auto_pipeline → get_authoring_v2 → apply_authoring_v2_patches → publish_nas_package_v2. | `publish_nas_package_v2` cannot be reached from `apply_authoring_v2_patches`. It refuses any source whose file name is not literally `reading-source-v2.json` and whose sibling `manifest-v2.json` is not a valid `AuthoringV2ExportReceiptV1` with matching hashes and a byte-identical `publishProof`. Only `export_authoring_v2` produces that. `export_authoring_v2` is a mandatory 7th step in the chain. | `src-tauri/src/nas_package_v2.rs:62-71, :82-100, :140-152, :200-202; src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs:533-566` |
| partly-wrong | (Assignment premise) The chain can be driven against a temp dir. | True for 5 of the 7 steps and false for 2. `run_auto_pipeline_core`, `get_authoring_v2_core`, `apply_authoring_v2_patches_core`, `export_authoring_v2_core`, `publish_nas_package_v2_core` all take `root: &Path` and are already called with temp roots by existing tests. `create_import_job_core` and `import_source_file_core` take `app: AppHandle` and resolve the root themselves. | `src-tauri/src/job_commands.rs:77-89 and :158-209 vs auto_pipeline.rs:1153, authoring_v2_commands.rs:54/:123/:449, nas_package_v2.rs:245` |
| confirmed | (Assignment premise) `library_items.id == job.job_id`. | Confirmed in code, and it holds for the compatibility `exams` table too. | `src-tauri/src/library_commands.rs:50 (`id: job.job_id.clone()`), :187 (`id: exam.id.clone()`), :197 (`linked_ingest_job_id: Some(exam.id.clone())`)` |
| wrong | (Implicit) An out-of-crate integration test at src-tauri/tests/product_chain.rs is a viable location. | It is not viable without widening visibility. `src-tauri/tests/` does not exist, and an integration test links the rlib and sees only `pub` items. Every module in the chain is a private `mod` (lib.rs:43-86); the only `pub mod` is `schema` (lib.rs:82). Every `_core` fn is `pub(crate)`. The complete public surface of the crate is: `CommandResult`, the 20 data structs/enums (JobStatus … SaveLlmProfileInput), `AppState`, `run_cli_or_app()`, `run()`, and `pub mod schema`. Nothing in that list can drive the chain. | `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:43-86 (mod decls), :82 (`pub mod schema;`), and `grep -E '^pub (fn\|struct\|enum\|type\|mod)' lib.rs` → lines 82,90,94,115,127,135,158,177,187,193,206,215,240,246,257,268,273,279,286,301,307,313,336,355,752,1403` |
| wrong | (Implicit) `tauri::test`/a mock AppHandle would let the real command bodies be called. | Two independent problems. (a) Cargo.toml declares `tauri = { version = "2", features = [] }` and has NO `[dev-dependencies]` section at all, so `tauri::test` is not compiled in; enabling it is a Cargo.toml edit. (b) Even with a mock app, `app.path().app_data_dir()` resolves via `dirs 6.0` + the bundle identifier to the user's REAL roaming AppData directory. On Windows `dirs` uses the known-folder API, so `%APPDATA%` cannot redirect it. A mock AppHandle would make the test write to `%APPDATA%\com.ielts.author.studio` and pollute the developer's live app data. | `src-tauri/Cargo.toml (no [dev-dependencies], tauri features = []), src-tauri/Cargo.lock:4354-4355 (tauri 2.11.2), Cargo.lock:855-857 (dirs 6.0.0), src-tauri/tauri.conf.json (identifier com.ielts.author.studio), src-tauri/src/lib.rs:357-359` |
| partly-wrong | (Implicit) The named chain plus a temp dir is enough to reach a committed publish. | The binding constraint is not plumbing, it is the quality gate. `export_authoring_v2_core` recomputes `quality` (`refresh_quality_report`, authoring_v2_commands.rs:474) and hard-blocks unless the state is `ready`; `evaluate_quality` only returns `ready` when there are zero issues, zero hardFailures, every taskScore ≥ 0.92, documentScore ≥ 0.95, sourceCoverage ≥ 0.995, AND a matching physical `DocumentIRV2` shadow explains every significant source node. The only place in the entire crate proven to reach `ready` is the checked-in golden fixture `fixtures/golden/synthetic/ielts/early-approaches-authoring-v2.json` paired with the hand-built `valid_physical_shadow()`. An existing test asserts the opposite for that same fixture without the shadow: setting `quality.state="ready"` by hand still yields `authoring_v2_export_blocked:quality_state=review_required`. | `src-tauri/src/ielts_grammar/quality.rs:186-198 (state derivation), :4256-4262 (`ready_fixture_proves_...`), :3689-3745 (`valid_physical_shadow`), :4279-4295 (`missing_malformed_and_empty_physical_shadows_cannot_be_ready`); src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs:474, :482-486, :2422-2458 (`export_rejects_non_ready_quality_state_before_materialization`)` |

Budget one iteration here. |
| medium | `src-tauri/src/product_chain.rs (new)::stage 4-5: get_authoring_v2 + apply_authoring_v2_patches` | `authoring_v2_commands::get_authoring_v2_core(&root, &job.job_id)` — assert `source == "shadow"` and `revision == 0`. Then `apply_authoring_v2_patches_core(&root, json!({"jobId": job.job_id, "baseRevision": 0, "patches": [{"op":"replaceText","nodeId": <first text node id>,"from":0,"to":0,"text":"chain-edit"}]}))` and assert the returned `revision == 1` and that `jobs/<id>/authoring/revisions/1.json` + `authoring/current-revision.json` exist. Copy the patch shape and the answer-resolution-then-text-edit sequence verbatim from `real_pdf_acceptance::run_phase5_real_pdf_edit_export` (real_pdf_acceptance.rs:1627-1652) — that is the only working example of this call pair in the repo. Note `get_authoring_v2_core` has zero test coverage today; this stage is genuinely new ground. | — |
| high | `src-tauri/src/product_chain.rs (new)::stage 6: export_authoring_v2 (the mandatory step the assignment omits)` | `export_authoring_v2_core(&root, json!({"jobId": job.job_id, "exportDir": <ABSOLUTE temp path>, "revision": revision}))` and take `outputDir` from the result. `exportDir` must be absolute or you get `authoring_v2_export_dir_must_be_absolute` (authoring_v2_commands.rs:497-499). | This is where the test will actually fail first. `export_authoring_v2_core` recomputes quality and returns `authoring_v2_export_blocked:quality_state=review_required` unless `evaluate_quality` says `ready`, which additionally requires: a physical `document-ir-v2.shadow.json` that passes `physical_shadow_matches_authoring` (matching schemaVersion/jobId/documentId and at least one shared sourceFileId — authoring_v2_commands.rs:757-778) AND explains every significant source node, plus `audit.humanVerified == true` and a resolved, non-stale `SourceReviewV1`. A freshly parsed fixture will not satisfy this. Two escape hatches, pick one deliberately: (A) split the test in two — a `product_chain_import_to_edit` test that ends at stage 5 and asserts the real `review_required` state as the honest current behaviour, plus a `product_chain_publish` test that seeds the job dir from the golden `early-approaches-authoring-v2.json` + a `valid_physical_shadow`-style DocumentIRV2 (quality.rs:3689-3745) so quality genuinely evaluates to `ready`; or (B) skip stage 6 and hand-forge the receipt exactly as `nas_package_v2::tests::test_export_bundle` does (nas_package_v2.rs:1441-1441..1495), which sets `quality.state="ready"`/`issues=[]` and calls `validate_authoring_v2_publish_readiness` directly — that fn reads quality from the value instead of recomputing it. (A) is the more honest product-chain proof; (B) is cheaper and already known to work. |
| low | `src-tauri/src/product_chain.rs (new)::stage 7: publish_nas_package_v2` | `nas_package_v2::publish_nas_package_v2_core(&root, json!({"libraryRoot": <ABSOLUTE nas dir under the temp root>, "sourcePath": output_dir.join("reading-source-v2.json")}))` and assert `result["status"] == "committed"` plus `nas/manifest.js` and `nas/<examId>.js` on disk. Both paths must be absolute; `sourcePath`'s file name must be exactly `reading-source-v2.json`. Copy `publish_normalizes_publish_child_to_one_transaction_root` verbatim (nas_package_v2.rs:1540-1567) — it is the closest existing assertion set. No Node, no pdfium, no network is involved in this stage. | — |
| low | `src-tauri/src/product_chain.rs (new)::cleanup + isolation` | End with `let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(root);` (the universal convention). Do NOT call `env::set_var` anywhere: `cargo test` runs threads in one process and only three lib.rs tests guard env mutation behind `env_test_lock()` (lib.rs:1858-1861, used at :2116, :2138, :3427). In particular do not touch `EPIC8_ALLOW_PLAINTEXT_SECRET_FALLBACK` (the CLI path at lib.rs:546 does, which is exactly why the CLI recipe cannot be lifted verbatim into a test). | — |
| low | `src-tauri/Cargo.toml::[dependencies] tauri features / absent [dev-dependencies]` | NO CHANGE NEEDED, and this is the point worth stating explicitly to whoever plans this work: adding `tauri = { features = ["test"] }` or a `[dev-dependencies]` block buys nothing, because a mock AppHandle still resolves `app_data_dir()` to the developer's real `%APPDATA%`. Leave the manifest alone; the split in job_commands.rs is strictly cheaper and strictly safer. | — |

### 现有测试方式
There is no `src-tauri/tests/`, no `benches/`, no `examples/`, no `[[test]]` target and no `[dev-dependencies]`. All ~470 tests are in-crate `#[cfg(test)]` modules. Two shapes exist: a trailing `mod tests` inside a production file (the norm — lib.rs:1480, auto_pipeline.rs:2669, authoring_v2_commands.rs:2003, nas_package_v2.rs:1424, db.rs:1037, library_commands.rs:492, util.rs:310, job_store.rs:86), and a whole file that is itself test-only (`#[cfg(test)] mod cross_repo_contract_fixture;` at lib.rs:51-52 and `#[cfg(test)] mod real_pdf_acceptance;` at ielts_grammar/mod.rs:23-24). The second shape is the one to copy for product_chain.

The uniform recipe: a temp dir under `env::temp_dir()` with a UUID suffix, `ensure_app_dirs`, hand-built `ImportJob` via `make_job`, `save_job`, optional fixture bytes copied into `jobs/<id>/uploads/`, then direct calls to `_core(&root, ...)` functions, then `let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(root);`. NO AppHandle is ever constructed anywhere in the crate. NO test is `async`: there is no `#[tokio::test]`, no `block_on`, and no `tauri::async_runtime` call in any test — because every `_core` in the chain except the two in job_commands.rs is synchronous. `tauri::async_runtime::spawn_blocking` appears only in the production command wrappers.

Three representative test functions, quoted verbatim.

(1) The minimal temp-root + real-SQLite pattern — src-tauri/src/job_store.rs:91-104. Note it does not even call `ensure_app_dirs`, and `save_job` silently exercises the `authoring_hub.db` dual-write:

```rust
    #[test]
    fn legacy_job_load_does_not_require_phase1_directories() {
        let root = env::temp_dir().join(format!("phase1-job-store-{}", Uuid::new_v4().simple()));
        let job = make_job(CreateJobInput {
            title: Some("Legacy job".to_string()),
            ..Default::default()
        });
        save_job(&root, &job).unwrap();
        let loaded = load_job(&root, &job.job_id).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(loaded.job_id, job.job_id);
        assert_eq!(loaded.title, "Legacy job");
        assert!(!job_dir(&root, &job.job_id).join("authoring").exists());
        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(root);
    }
```

(2) The canonical pipeline test — src-tauri/src/lib.rs:4319-4415 (head shown; it continues with ~30 assertions on the report and on which artifacts must and must not exist). This is the exact skeleton stages 1-3 of product_chain should follow:

```rust
    #[test]
    fn auto_pipeline_llm_failure_keeps_text_import_in_llm_review() {
        let root = temp_test_root();
        ensure_app_dirs(&root).unwrap();
        let mut job = test_job();
        attach_fixture_source(&root, &mut job, "complex-reading.txt", "MainQuestion");
        save_job(&root, &job).unwrap();

        let report = run_auto_pipeline_core(&root, &job.job_id, None).unwrap();

        assert!(report
            .pointer("/llm/failures")
            .and_then(Value::as_array)
            .map(|failures| !failures.is_empty())
            .unwrap_or(false));
        assert_eq!(
            report.get("status").and_then(Value::as_str),
            Some("NeedsReview")
        );
        let saved = load_job(&root, &job.job_id).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(saved.status, JobStatus::NeedsReview);
        assert_eq!(saved.current_step, WorkflowStep::Authoring);
        assert!(job_dir(&root, &job.job_id)
            .join("authoring-ir.json")
            .exists());
        assert!(job_dir(&root, &job.job_id)
            .join("authoring-project.json")
            .exists());

        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(root);
    }
```

and its two supporting helpers, src-tauri/src/lib.rs:1576-1586 and :1829-1856:

```rust
    fn temp_test_root() -> PathBuf {
        env::temp_dir().join(format!("epic8-test-{}", Uuid::new_v4().simple()))
    }

    fn parser_fixture(name: &str) -> PathBuf {
        PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
            .join("..")
            .join("fixtures")
            .join("parser")
            .join(name)
    }

    fn attach_fixture_source(root: &Path, job: &mut ImportJob, fixture_name: &str, role: &str) {
        let fixture = parser_fixture(fixture_name);
        let original_name = fixture
            .file_name()
            .and_then(|value| value.to_str())
            .unwrap()
            .to_string();
        let (hash, size, bytes) = hash_file_or_path(&fixture).unwrap();
        let stored_name = format!("{}-{}", &hash[..8], sanitize_filename(&original_name));
        ensure_job_dirs(&job_dir(root, &job.job_id)).unwrap();
        write_bytes(
            &job_dir(root, &job.job_id)
                .join("uploads")
                .join(&stored_name),
            &bytes.unwrap(),
        )
        .unwrap();
        job.source_files.push(SourceFile {
            file_id: format!("file-{}", Uuid::new_v4().simple()),
            original_name,
            stored_name,
            file_type: file_type_from_name(fixture_name).to_string(),
            sha256: hash,
            size_bytes: size,
            role: role.to_string(),
            imported_at: Utc::now(),
        });
    }
```

`attach_fixture_source` reproduces `import_source_file_core`'s `stored_name` scheme (`<sha[..8]>-<sanitized>`) byte for byte — which is precisely why nothing has yet forced the AppHandle out of that command.

(3) The publish-side pattern — src-tauri/src/nas_package_v2.rs:1540-1567. Copy the assertions; note the receipt comes from `test_export_bundle` (:1441), which hand-forges `manifest-v2.json` with `quality.state=\"ready\"` instead of going through `export_authoring_v2_core`:

```rust
    #[test]
    fn publish_normalizes_publish_child_to_one_transaction_root() {
        let root = temp_root();
        let (source_path, _source) = test_export_bundle(&root);

        let nas_parent = root.join("nas");
        let publish_child = nas_parent.join("publish");
        let result = publish_nas_package_v2_core(
            &root,
            json!({
                "libraryRoot": publish_child,
                "sourcePath": source_path
            }),
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(result["status"], "committed");
        assert!(nas_parent.join("manifest.js").is_file());
        assert!(nas_parent.join("early-approaches.js").is_file());
        assert!(nas_parent
            .join(CONTROL_DIR_NAME)
            .join("export.lock")
            .is_file());
        assert!(!publish_child.join("manifest.js").exists());
        assert!(!publish_child.join(CONTROL_DIR_NAME).exists());
        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(root);
    }
```

Environment/tooling notes for whoever runs it. pdfium resolves under `cargo test` via an explicit `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` fallback (pdf_geometry.rs:81-89) to `src-tauri/lib/pdfium-windows/pdfium.dll`, which is present — so PDF fixtures work under `cargo test` but would NOT work if the test binary is invoked directly (Cargo sets that env var for the child process). A single cached `Pdfium` instance behind a Mutex serialises PDF tests (pdf_geometry.rs:26-42). Node is not involved: `node_validator_diagnostics_enabled()` defaults to false (environment.rs:187-195), and the runtime gate the pipeline runs is pure Rust (`mode: \"static-rust\"`, runtime_validation.rs:309-319). Run the new test by exact name — do not expect a green `cargo test` baseline (see surprises).

### 意外发现
- `cargo test` is NOT green in this working tree, and it is not the new test's fault. All 8 fixtures in `fixtures/golden/phase4-eight-pdf-acceptance.json` (chili-peppers, conformity, fishbourne-roman-palace, listening-to-the-ocean, organisational-design, petri-dish, sleep-study, western-celebrity) live under `fixtures/golden/private-real/*.pdf`, which `fixtures/golden/private-real/.gitignore:2` excludes and which are ABSENT from disk (only `.gitignore` and `README.md` are there). `lib.rs::registered_phase4_pdf_corpus_reaches_frozen_v1_parse_split_and_authoring` (lib.rs:9189-9190) is not `#[ignore]`d and calls `phase4_pdf_fixtures()` (lib.rs:1588), which asserts every entry is `available` and then hashes the file — it must panic. The same holds for the 12 tests in `ielts_grammar/real_pdf_acceptance.rs`, including `phase5_real_pdf_edit_and_v2_export_round_trip`. Run the new test by exact name (`cargo test product_chain`) and do not try to establish a green baseline first.
- `real_pdf_acceptance.rs` uses the REPOSITORY ROOT as its app root: `fn repo_root() -> PathBuf { PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("..") }` (:21-23), and `run_phase5_real_pdf_edit_export` then does `root.join("jobs").join(&job_id)` (:1594) plus `root.join("tmp/phase5-real-pdf-acceptance/...")`. So that test suite writes job artifacts into `F:/workspace/PDF2Test/jobs/` and `tmp/` (both gitignored). Do not copy this; product_chain should use a temp root.
- The two AppHandle-bound cores are the ONLY untested module in the chain. `job_commands.rs` has no `#[cfg(test)]` block at all — `create_import_job_core` and `import_source_file_core` have never been executed by any test. That is the direct consequence of the AppHandle in their signature, and it is the concrete coverage hole a product-chain test closes.
- `AppState` is decorative. Zero fields, no `impl`, no `State<AppState>` consumer anywhere. db.rs:5-8 documents the deliberate decision to keep the SQLite Connection out of it (transient per-operation connections keyed on `root: &Path` instead), which is exactly what makes temp-root testing free. The plan's `AppState::new(...)` would be a new concept, not a rename.
- `get_authoring_v2` is the only command in the named chain with zero direct test coverage in the crate. `get_authoring_v2_core` is referenced exactly twice: the import at lib.rs:8 and the call at lib.rs:1011. No test calls it. By contrast `apply_authoring_v2_patches_core` and `export_authoring_v2_core` are each exercised by `real_pdf_acceptance.rs` (currently unrunnable) and `publish_nas_package_v2_core` by 8 tests in its own module.
- `export_authoring_v2` is a hidden mandatory link. The assignment's chain jumps from `apply_authoring_v2_patches` to `publish_nas_package_v2`, but `validate_v2_export_binding` (nas_package_v2.rs:56) makes that impossible: the source file must be named `reading-source-v2.json`, sit next to a hash-matching `AuthoringV2ExportReceiptV1` manifest and an `authoring-ir-v2.json`, and carry a `publishProof` byte-identical to a freshly recomputed one. Only `export_authoring_v2_core` produces that bundle.
- The quality gate, not the AppHandle, is the real cost driver. `evaluate_quality` (quality.rs) returns `ready` only with zero issues, zero hardFailures, all taskScores ≥ 0.92, documentScore ≥ 0.95, sourceCoverage ≥ 0.995 and a physical DocumentIRV2 shadow that explains every significant source node (quality.rs:186-198). An existing test proves that hand-setting `quality.state="ready"` does not help, because export recomputes it: `export_rejects_non_ready_quality_state_before_materialization` asserts the error is exactly `authoring_v2_export_blocked:quality_state=review_required` (authoring_v2_commands.rs:2422-2458). And `missing_malformed_and_empty_physical_shadows_cannot_be_ready` (quality.rs:4279-4295) proves no physical shadow means never ready. The only proven `ready` recipe in the repo is `early-approaches-authoring-v2.json` + `valid_physical_shadow()` (quality.rs:3689-3745).
- The physical shadow that `ready` depends on is PDF-only. `run_auto_pipeline_core` writes `document-ir-v2.shadow.json` inside `if let (Some(source), Some(document)) = (main_source_file(&job).filter(|source| source.file_type == "pdf"), ...)` (auto_pipeline.rs:1487-1490). A `.txt`/`.md`/`.docx` main source produces no physical shadow, so a txt-based product_chain test can NEVER reach `ready` and can never publish. If the test must publish, the fixture must be a PDF.
- Both V2 shadow writes fail SILENTLY. `write_pipeline_authoring_v2_shadow` (auto_pipeline.rs:275-315) deletes the shadow and writes an `authoring-ir-v2.shadow.error.json` instead of propagating, and the physical-shadow path does the same with `document-ir-v2.shadow.error.json` (:1502-1517). `run_auto_pipeline` therefore returns Ok with no V2 shadow at all, and the failure only surfaces two commands later as `AUTHORING_V2_NOT_AVAILABLE:shadow_missing:<path>`. A product_chain test should assert both shadows exist immediately after stage 3 and dump the `.error.json` contents on failure.
- `authoring_v2_shadow_enabled()` and `quality_gate_v2_enabled()` are hardcoded `true` (environment.rs:223-229) — they read no env var despite looking like feature flags. There is no way to switch the quality gate off for a test.
- `run_auto_pipeline_core` refuses to run twice: `if dir.join("authoring-ir.json").exists() && !allow_overwrite { return Err("editable_draft_exists; pass allowOverwrite=true before regenerating draft") }` (auto_pipeline.rs:1402-1406). A retry loop in the test must pass `allow_overwrite: Some(true)`.
- `publish_nas_package_v2` silently retargets its output: `normalize_nas_library_root` strips a trailing `publish/` child so `libraryRoot: <x>/nas/publish` writes into `<x>/nas` (proved by `publish_normalizes_publish_child_to_one_transaction_root`, nas_package_v2.rs:1540-1567). Assert on the normalised parent, not the path you passed in.
- The shipped CLI already contains a complete headless chain driver with an explicit root override: `run_auto_pipeline_from_path` honours `--app-root` (lib.rs:516-524) and otherwise mints its own temp dir. It is the strongest existing evidence that the backend needs no Tauri — but it cannot be lifted verbatim into a test because it demands live LLM credentials (lib.rs:506-514) and calls `env::set_var("EPIC8_ALLOW_PLAINTEXT_SECRET_FALLBACK", "1")` (lib.rs:546), which is process-global and unsafe under the parallel test harness.
- Only 3 of 136 lib.rs tests guard env mutation behind `env_test_lock()` (lib.rs:1858-1861, used at :2116, :2138, :3427). Any new test that sets an env var without that lock will flake against those three.
- `tempfile 3.27` and `tokio 1.52` are already in Cargo.lock as transitive deps of tauri, but neither is a declared dependency, so `use tempfile::...` in a test will not compile. Every test rolls its own temp dir by hand — follow that.

---

## LLM gateway and cloud outline (src-tauri/src/llm_gateway.rs, src-tauri/src/llm_suggestions.rs, plus their only real drivers auto_pipeline.rs / llm_commands.rs)
**数据流**：Two independent entry paths, and the shipped UI uses the second one.

PATH A - run_auto_pipeline (lib.rs:1291-1301, spawn_blocking -> auto_pipeline::run_auto_pipeline_core:1153 -> run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway:1381 with the real fn `run_llm_gateway`). Cloud is opt-in: `local_only = execution_mode == Some("localOnly")` and `cloud_diagnostics_opted_in = !local_only && options.profile_id.is_some()` (1394-1395), then `profile_id = cloud_diagnostics_opted_in.then(|| select_llm_profile(..)).flatten()` (1411). The gateway fn is moved into `Mutex::new(llm_gateway)` (1415). `cloud_worker_spawned = selected_profile_id.is_some() && main_source_is_pdf(&job)` (1416). Inside `thread::scope` (1419) one worker thread is spawned (1425-1433) which: renders page images ONCE via main_pdf_vision_extraction (1247, -> parser::extract_pdf_images_for_vision -> python sidecar then pdfium/sips renderer, writes cache/vision/pdf-images.json + cache/vision/assets/*), sends (extraction, asset_dir) down extraction_tx so the main thread can reuse it, then takes lock_gateway TWICE in sequence: extract_pdf_image_answers (1253-1256) and generate_pdf_reading_outline (1257-1260), and finally sends CloudWorkerOutcome{vision_answer, outline} down worker_tx. Meanwhile the main thread parses the document, writes document-ir.json, materializes the DocumentIRV2 physical shadow, and if needs_pdf_vision_transcription it blocks on extraction_rx.recv() (1563) and then takes the SAME lock for transcribe_pdf_images (1569). It then builds split-candidates.json and authoring-ir.json, optionally runs the per-group extract_group repair loop (1698-1723, gated by `!local_only && !main_source_is_pdf(&job)` - mutually exclusive with cloud_worker_spawned), then joins the worker at worker_rx.recv() (1862), writes vision-answer-output.json + vision-answer-candidates.json (1877-1878), and finally calls cloud_outline_report_from_output(profile, &ir, outcome.outline.clone()) (1957) which runs cloud_outline_comparison against the local draft. Everything cloud-derived is written as read-only diagnostics: audit issues via append_authoring_audit_issue plus quality.cloudComparison in pipeline-report.json (2170, written 2185). The cloud outline NEVER mutates authoring-ir.json.

PATH B - run_cloud_review (lib.rs:1303-1312 -> run_cloud_review_core:2209 -> run_cloud_review_core_with_gateway:2217). Reads the existing authoring-ir.json (errors with authoring_ir_missing_for_cloud_review if absent), select_llm_profile, then if main_source_is_pdf: main_pdf_vision_extraction AGAIN (2243, full re-render - extract_pdf_images_for_vision has no cache-hit short circuit), then cloud_outline_check_for_job (2245, generate+compare) followed by vision_answer_candidate_for_job (2258) strictly sequentially on one thread. It then re-reads authoring-ir.json from disk before writing the audit issue back (2350+, "merge-on-write" so a concurrent user edit is not clobbered) and rewrites pipeline-report.json (2665).

The new frontend drives B, not A: src/features/import/useImportFiles.ts:149 calls runAutoPipeline with `executionMode: "localOnly"` (so profile_id is None and no worker is ever spawned), then 155-158 pools runCloudReview at `cloudConcurrency` (default 2, src/features/settings/appSettings.ts:30). src/features/editor/ExamWorkspacePage.tsx:115-120 does the same pair.

Inside the gateway, one call is: run_llm_gateway writes the redacted input to cache/llm/, dispatches on command_name, the handler builds an OpenAI chat-completions body {model, temperature, messages:[{role:"system",content:"Return valid JSON only."},{role:"user",content:<prompt or multimodal parts>}]} plus response_format:{"type":"json_object"} unless profile.forceJson==false (llm_force_json:131 treats anything except explicit false as true), calls openai_post, then openai_chat_content pulls /choices/0/message/content, parse_llm_json_content extracts the single outermost JSON value, and a per-command validator mutates it in place and stamps evidence/metadata with mode/source/model/usage. Attachments: images go in as {"type":"image_url","image_url":{"url":"data:<mime>;base64,..."}} (append_pdf_images_to_content:578-618, 32 MiB per image, 128 MiB total measured on data-URL length); the outline sends the PDF itself as {"type":"file","file":{"filename":..,"file_data":"data:application/pdf;base64,.."}} (data_url_for_pdf:559-577, 128 MiB cap) and only falls back to page images if that whole request fails (run_openai_compatible_cloud_outline_llm:735-761, which pushes a direct_pdf_request_failed:<err> warning into the parsed output). Every attachment path goes through read_llm_file (493-541): must be absolute, canonicalized, and inside the canonicalized app root, else llm_<kind>_path_outside_app_root.

Transport: openai_post (290-318) computes ONE deadline = now + llm_timeout(profile, 60_000) where llm_timeout clamps profile.timeoutMs to [1_000, 300_000] ms, then loops up to MAX_LLM_ATTEMPTS = 3 passing the REMAINING budget as each attempt's per-request timeout. Retry decision is made by string-sniffing the error (is_retryable_llm_http_error:320): retry on llm_http_timeout:/llm_http_connect_failed:/llm_http_body_failed: prefixes, or an llm_http_<status> where status is 408|425|429 or 500-599. Backoff = max(retry_after_ms, 400*(attempt+1)); if the remaining budget is <= that delay it returns llm_timeout_budget_exhausted:<last_error>. Retry-After arrives as a header, is parsed by retry_after_ms_from_header (357, integer seconds or RFC2822 date, capped at 5_000 ms), is appended onto the error string as ";retry_after_ms=<ms>" (421-425), and is read back out by retry_after_ms_from_error (339). openai_post_once (372) builds a FRESH reqwest::blocking::Client per attempt with redirect::Policy::none(), sets bearer_auth only when api_key is non-blank, pre-checks content_length against MAX_LLM_RESPONSE_BYTES (16 MiB) and re-checks after a .take()-bounded read.

### 关键签名
- `run_llm_gateway` — `pub(crate) fn run_llm_gateway(     root: &Path,     job_id: &str,     command_name: &str,     input: &Value,     api_key: Option<&str>, ) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - The ONLY public entry. command_name is a stringly-typed dispatch (llm_gateway.rs:36-48) over exactly 5 values: "classify_group" | "extract_group" | "test_profile" -> group LLM, "transcribe_pdf_images", "extract_pdf_image_answers", "generate_pdf_reading_outline"; anything else -> Err("unsupported_llm_gateway_command:{}"). Side effects on every call: writes cache/llm/{command}-input-{ms}.json (redacted) before, cache/llm/{command}-output-{ms}.json after, and appends one llm_call record to jobs/{job_id}/llm-calls.jsonl (49-68) with commandName/jobId/model/ok/latencyMs/errorClass/recordedAt. No requestId is generated or recorded anywhere.
- `parse_llm_json_content` — `pub(crate) fn parse_llm_json_content(content: &str) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - Plan 7.6's extract_single_json_value already exists here and is stricter than the plan's pseudocode. Tries whole-string parse; else brace/bracket-balanced scan (balanced_json_end, 251-288, string- and escape-aware) collecting every parseable candidate, then keeps only outermost ones. Exactly one outermost -> Ok. Zero -> Err("llm_json_parse_failed:{first_error}"). Two or more -> Err("llm_json_parse_failed:ambiguous_wrapped_json"). No markdown fence stripping is needed because the scan ignores prose.
- `validate_llm_suggestion_output` — `pub(crate) fn validate_llm_suggestion_output(     output: &mut Value,     mode: &str,     profile: &Value,     payload: &Value, ) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - Also used from llm_commands/tests. Requires kind in allowed_question_kind, numeric confidence, array patch/questions, evidence.sourceBlockIds array, evidence.quotes array of {blockId,text} non-empty; patch ops restricted to op=replace and path in {"/kind","/layout/template"} (any other path -> Err). Then normalize_confidence_fail_closed (204-217) collapses out-of-range confidence to 0.0 and appends a confidence_out_of_range warning, and stamps evidence.mode/source="openai-compatible-rust"/model/usage.
- `validate_cloud_outline_output` — `fn validate_cloud_outline_output(     output: &mut Value,     profile: &Value,     payload: &Value, ) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - THIS IS the CloudReadingOutlineV1 schema; there is no Rust struct. Private, so the contract is not reusable outside this file today.
- `redact_llm_input_for_cache` — `pub(crate) fn redact_llm_input_for_cache(input: &Value) -> Value`
  - Removes only a top-level "apiKey" and inserts apiKeySource:"process-env". The real key never travels in `input` anyway (it is the separate api_key arg), so this is belt-and-braces.
- `make_cloud_paper_generation_input` — `pub(crate) fn make_cloud_paper_generation_input(     profile: &Value,     job: &ImportJob,     profile_id: &str,     source: &crate::SourceFile,     pdf_path: &Path,     extraction: &Value, ) -> Value`
  - Builds {mode:"generate_pdf_reading_outline", job{jobId,title,category,frequency,tags}, profile(profile_payload), sourceFile{fileId,originalName,fileType,sha256,sizeBytes}, pdfPath, pages, extractionWarnings, outputContract{schema:"CloudReadingOutlineV1", jsonOnly, shape, rules[8]}}. llm_suggestions.rs:235 is the only occurrence of the literal "CloudReadingOutlineV1" in the repo.
- `make_vision_transcription_input` — `pub(crate) fn make_vision_transcription_input(     profile: &Value,     job: &ImportJob,     profile_id: &str,     extraction: &Value, ) -> Value`
  - mode "transcribe_pdf_images"; carries pages + extractionWarnings and nothing else. No outputContract (unlike the answer/outline builders).
- `make_vision_answer_extraction_input` — `pub(crate) fn make_vision_answer_extraction_input(     profile: &Value,     job: &ImportJob,     profile_id: &str,     extraction: &Value, ) -> Value`
  - outputContract.schema = "PdfImageAnswerKeyV1".
- `make_llm_input` — `pub(crate) fn make_llm_input(     profile: &Value,     job: &ImportJob,     group: &Value,     profile_id: &str,     mode: &str, ) -> Value`
  - V1 group-repair path. repairContract.schema = "Epic8LlmGroupRepairV1" with allowedPatchPaths ["/kind","/layout/template"] and highConfidenceAutoApplyThreshold 0.85.
- `deterministic_llm_output` — `pub(crate) fn deterministic_llm_output(group: &Value, mode: &str, warning: String) -> Value`
  - The local fallback the plan (17.10) wants relabelled. Emits confidence 0.64, evidence{mode,source:"rust-local-fallback",fallback:true}, warnings[warning,"low-confidence-review-required","fallback-output-never-auto-applies"]. It IS saved as an llm suggestion today (llm_commands.rs:232+251, auto_pipeline.rs:1728+1753).
- `llm_suggestion_auto_apply_issues` — `pub(crate) fn llm_suggestion_auto_apply_issues(     ir: &Value,     suggestion: &Value,     selected_paths: &[String], ) -> Vec<String>`
  - Returns a flat Vec<String> of issue codes (confidence_below_auto_apply_threshold, evidence_block_not_in_group:*, non_provider_evidence_source:*, blocking_warning:*, ...). Empty vec == may auto-apply. This is the only existing 'proposal gate' and it is V1-group-shaped.
- `llm_suggestion_quote_mismatches` — `pub(crate) fn llm_suggestion_quote_mismatches(     suggestion: &Value,     block_texts: &std::collections::BTreeMap<String, String>, ) -> Vec<String>`
  - The nearest thing to plan 7.11's find_quote_normalized. Whitespace-normalized lowercase containment plus a hyphen-stripped fallback (normalized_quote_text 382, normalized_quote_text_without_hyphens 393). Keyed by blockId, not page/bbox. Only ever applied to group-repair suggestions - cloud outline quotes are never checked against the source.

### 计划断言复核

| 判定 | 计划说 | 代码实际 | 证据 |
|---|---|---|---|
| partly-wrong | 7.1: the current cloud contract returns title / groups[].range / kind / layoutHint / questionIds / notesText / answerKey / confidence / warnings. | Correct but incomplete, and the plan omits which fields are HARD-REQUIRED. validate_cloud_outline_output also requires, per group, a numeric `confidence` and a non-empty `evidence.quotes` array of {pageIndex>=1, text non-empty} (llm_gateway.rs:1273-1300), and optionally accepts group `warnings`. Top level it also injects `metadata{mode,source,model,usage}` (1332-1356) and, on kind normalization, `rawKind` (1315-1317). And `answerKey` is not optional: a missing key is Err("cloud_outline_answer_key_missing") via normalize_answer_map_value_checked (1054-1058, called at 1206), so a question paper with no printed answer key fails the entire outline unless the model emits answerKey:{}. | `src-tauri/src/llm_gateway.rs:1176-1365; src-tauri/src/llm_suggestions.rs:230-262` |
| partly-wrong | 7.14: the UI currently offers AnthropicCompatible and Custom while the gateway routes only OpenAiCompatible and Ollama; phase 1 should delete the unsupported options from the normal UI. | The gateway half is exactly right (llm_gateway.rs:146). The UI half is already done in the Phase 1-3 surface: src/features/settings/SettingsPage.tsx:29-33 defines SUPPORTED_PROVIDERS as only OpenAiCompatible + Ollama and coerces anything else on load (line 63). What is still open is the BACKEND and the type: llm_commands.rs:79-83 still accepts all four in save_llm_profile_core, src/types/settings.ts:2 still declares all four, and the retired src/pages/Settings.tsx:287-291 still renders all four options. | `src/features/settings/SettingsPage.tsx:29-33; src-tauri/src/llm_commands.rs:79-87; src/types/settings.ts:2; src/pages/Settings.tsx:287-291` |
| partly-wrong | 26.3-D: the existing worker runs in parallel with the local chain, but vision answer and outline execute sequentially under one mutable gateway lock. | Confirmed literally for run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway - two separate lock_gateway acquisitions wrapping two full HTTP calls (auto_pipeline.rs:1253-1260). But this is not what serializes the shipped product, because the shipped UI never spawns that worker: it calls runAutoPipeline with executionMode:"localOnly" (so cloud_diagnostics_opted_in is false, profile_id is None, cloud_worker_spawned is false) and then runCloudReview, whose implementation run_cloud_review_core_with_gateway has no Mutex and no thread at all and is sequential by construction. Also, the group-repair loop and the cloud worker can never contend: 1416 gates the worker on main_source_is_pdf while 1698 gates the repair loop on !main_source_is_pdf. | `src-tauri/src/auto_pipeline.rs:1253-1260, 1415-1416, 1698, 2217-2262; src/features/import/useImportFiles.ts:149-158` |
| partly-wrong | 26.3-D: concurrency should be governed by a cloud semaphore rather than a gateway mutex. | A cloud semaphore already exists - in TypeScript. useImportFiles.ts:155 runs runPool(cloudEligible, cloudConcurrency, runCloudReview) with cloudConcurrency defaulting to 2 (appSettings.ts:30), i.e. up to 2 concurrent run_cloud_review Tauri commands. There is no Rust-side limiter, so the Rust semaphore the plan asks for would be a second, currently absent, layer. | `src/features/import/useImportFiles.ts:147-158; src/features/settings/appSettings.ts:29-30` |
| partly-wrong | 7.4: do not render the same PDF twice, once for answer recognition and once for full-paper recognition. | Already true WITHIN one auto-pipeline run: run_cloud_conversion_worker renders once (auto_pipeline.rs:1247) and the same `extraction` feeds the answer call, the outline call, and (via extraction_tx/extraction_rx) the main thread's vision transcription. It is NOT true across the two commands: run_cloud_review_core_with_gateway calls main_pdf_vision_extraction again (2243), and parser::extract_pdf_images_for_vision (2454) has no cache-hit short circuit - it re-spawns the python sidecar and re-renders every time even though cache/vision/pdf-images.json already exists. On the live UI path (localOnly pipeline, then cloud review) that is 2 full renders per PDF. | `src-tauri/src/auto_pipeline.rs:1247, 2243, 362-371; src-tauri/src/parser.rs:2454-2516` |
| partly-wrong | 7.12: every page must appear in a page manifest / coverage record so truncation is never mistaken for a complete result. | The coverage record already exists and is already computed - parser.rs stabilize_pdf_image_extraction_fields writes coverageStatus ("complete"\|"partial"\|"unknown"), partialCoverage, renderedPageCount and coverage{pageCount, availablePageIndexes, renderedPageIndexes, missingPageIndexes} onto PdfImageExtractionV1. None of it reaches the model or the validator: make_cloud_paper_generation_input forwards only `pages` and `extractionWarnings` (llm_suggestions.rs:220-221), and cloud_outline_prompt serializes only job / sourceFile / outputContract, so even `pages` is invisible to the model on the primary PDF path. There is also no page-count or byte/token precheck anywhere - no 15-page threshold, no sharding, no max_tokens, and finish_reason is never inspected. | `src-tauri/src/parser.rs:1995-2045; src-tauri/src/llm_suggestions.rs:212-262; src-tauri/src/llm_gateway.rs:484-492, 706-773` |
| partly-wrong | 7.6: needs extract_single_json_value + a normalize step + schema validation + one repair + salvage. | extract_single_json_value already exists and is stricter than the plan's sketch: parse_llm_json_content does a balanced, string-aware scan, keeps only outermost candidates, and explicitly errors with llm_json_parse_failed:ambiguous_wrapped_json when a response contains two independent top-level JSON values. normalize_safe_aliases partly exists as normalize_cloud_outline_kind (1076) + normalize_answer_text/normalize_question_number_key (1000-1030). RepairRequested, Salvaged and the CloudResponseState machine do not exist in any form - there is no second call, and no per-group acceptance. | `src-tauri/src/llm_gateway.rs:219-288, 1000-1085` |
| confirmed | 26.3-E: whole-object schema validation throws away 39 good questions because of 1 bad one. | Confirmed, and the blast radius is larger than described. validate_cloud_outline_output returns Err on the first failing group (1264-1302) AND on any single unparseable answerKey key - normalize_question_number_key only accepts 1..=200 after stripping a leading q/Q, so an answerKey key like "8-9", "8 and 9" or "0" kills the entire outline with cloud_outline_answer_key_question_number_invalid:<key> (1064-1065). Because the Err propagates out of run_llm_gateway, the resulting failure string lands in quality.cloudComparison.failure in pipeline-report.json, which then blocks V2 publish (see surprises). | `src-tauri/src/llm_gateway.rs:1000-1007, 1054-1074, 1206, 1264-1302; src-tauri/src/auto_pipeline.rs:1102-1150, 2170` |
| wrong | 17.9: llm_gateway.rs should RETAIN request ID / log correlation. | There is nothing to retain. No request id is generated, sent, or logged; the provider's own response `id` is never read. llm-calls.jsonl records only recordType/commandName/jobId/model/ok/latencyMs/errorClass/recordedAt (llm_gateway.rs:49-68), and errorClass is just error.split(':').next(). Correlation between a cache/llm/*-input-<ms>.json file and its llm-calls.jsonl line is by wall-clock timestamp only. | `src-tauri/src/llm_gateway.rs:23-81` |
| partly-wrong | 17.9: move the CloudReadingOutlineV1 business assembly out of llm_gateway.rs. | The assembly is spread over three files, so moving llm_gateway.rs alone does not finish the job. The request contract lives in llm_suggestions.rs:212-264 (outputContract.shape + 8 rules), the prompt restating the same shape lives in llm_gateway.rs:484-492, the enforcement lives in llm_gateway.rs:1176-1365, and the business semantics (notes-completion protection, kind families, answer comparison) live in auto_pipeline.rs:754-1012. All four restate the shape independently and can drift. | `src-tauri/src/llm_suggestions.rs:212-264; src-tauri/src/llm_gateway.rs:484-492, 1176-1365; src-tauri/src/auto_pipeline.rs:754-1012` |
| confirmed | 7.11: model-supplied page/quote/bbox must be locally resolved to SourceAnchorV2 before entering canonical data; cloud IDs must be reallocated by a local IdAllocator. | No resolver, no IdAllocator, and no bbox to resolve - the cloud contract never asks for bbox at all (prompt and validator only accept evidence.quotes[{pageIndex,text}]). Cloud outline quotes are never checked against the source document in any way; the only quote verifier in the codebase, llm_suggestion_quote_mismatches, is blockId-keyed and is wired exclusively into the V1 group-suggestion apply path (llm_commands.rs:355, auto_pipeline.rs:1763). Cloud `questionIds` are checked for count and uniqueness but never allocated or rewritten (llm_gateway.rs:1284-1301). The 1-based/0-based hazard the plan flags in 7.7 is real and live: the cloud contract and PdfImageExtractionV1 are 1-based (pageIndex==0 is rejected at 1289-1292), while DocumentIRV2 PageNodeV2.page_index and SourceAnchorV2.page_index are 0-based (schema/mod.rs fixtures use pageIndex 0). | `src-tauri/src/llm_gateway.rs:484-492, 1273-1301; src-tauri/src/llm_suggestions.rs:402-449; src-tauri/src/schema/common.rs:67-89; src-tauri/src/schema/document_ir_v2.rs:296` |
| already-done | 7.3: CloudRecognitionCandidateV1 needs TaskTypeV2, QuestionNumberExpressionV2, ContentNodeV2, OptionBankV2, ResponseGroupV2. | All five already exist as typed, deny_unknown_fields, camelCase Rust structs and can be reused verbatim: TaskTypeV2 (ielts_authoring_v2.rs:202), QuestionNumberExpressionV2 (225, internally tagged on "kind"), TaskGroupV2 (242-258, which is CloudTaskGroupCandidate minus instruction_signature/source_anchors/quality/review_state plus candidate_id/evidence/confidence), OptionBankV2 (317-325), ResponseGroupV2 (337-353), ContentNodeV2 (content_doc_v2.rs:293-336). The one field that cannot be reused as written is the plan's `bbox: Option<[f64;4]>` - RectV2 (common.rs:19-29) requires unit + origin + pageRotation, so the resolver must construct RectV2, not deserialize into it. | `src-tauri/src/schema/ielts_authoring_v2.rs:202-353; src-tauri/src/schema/content_doc_v2.rs:293-336; src-tauri/src/schema/common.rs:19-29` |
| partly-wrong | 7.13: the second (proofread) call must be conditional on structural / text / answer / unassigned / partial conflict. | There is no second call at all today, so nothing to make conditional - but the trigger inputs already exist. run_cloud_review_core_with_gateway computes cloud_needs_confirmation from attempted && (!passed \|\| warningCount>0) (2325-2337) and cloud_outline_comparison already separates blocking `issues` from non-blocking `observations` (1005-1011). That boolean is the natural gate. Note that today run_cloud_review IS unconditional per PDF: useImportFiles.ts:155 calls it for every accepted .pdf when a cloud profile exists. | `src-tauri/src/auto_pipeline.rs:1005-1011, 2325-2337; src/features/import/useImportFiles.ts:151-158` |
| partly-wrong | 17.10: the deterministic fallback should no longer be saved as an "LLM suggestion" and should be marked source=local_fallback. | It already self-labels: deterministic_llm_output emits evidence{source:"rust-local-fallback", fallback:true} and warnings including "fallback-output-never-auto-applies", and llm_suggestion_auto_apply_issues hard-blocks it three separate ways (fallback_evidence_never_auto_applies, non_provider_evidence_source:*, blocking_warning:*). What is still true is that it is written into the same store as real suggestions - save_llm_suggestion writes llm-suggestions/<group>--<id>.json, llm-last-suggestion.json AND appends to llm-calls.jsonl (llm_suggestions.rs:266-295), called at llm_commands.rs:251 and auto_pipeline.rs:1753. | `src-tauri/src/llm_suggestions.rs:88-106, 266-295, 594-651; src-tauri/src/llm_commands.rs:232-251; src-tauri/src/auto_pipeline.rs:1728-1753` |
| partly-wrong | 17.9: keep timeout and Retry-After as-is. | They work, but the timeout is doing double duty in a way a new transport should not inherit: openai_post derives ONE deadline from profile.timeoutMs (clamped 1s..300s) and uses it as both the per-attempt request timeout and the total 3-attempt budget (llm_gateway.rs:291-296). A first attempt that burns the whole budget leaves attempts 2 and 3 with zero, returning llm_timeout_budget_exhausted. Retry-After is capped at 5_000 ms in both parsers, and retry_after_ms_from_error also handles a ";retry_after=<seconds>" suffix (349-355) that nothing in the codebase ever emits - only ";retry_after_ms=" is produced (421-425). | `src-tauri/src/llm_gateway.rs:121-129, 290-318, 339-370, 415-430` |

Delete `let shared_gateway = Mutex::new(llm_gateway);` (1415), lock_gateway (1214-1219) and its four call sites (1254, 1258, 1569, 1717), and change the injection bound on run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway (1387-1388) and run_cloud_review_core_with_gateway (2221-2224) from `F: FnMut(&Path,&str,&str,&Value,Option<&str>) -> CommandResult<Value> [+ Send]` to a shared `&(dyn LlmTransport + Send + Sync)` (or `Arc<T: LlmTransport>`). Preserve the two hard-won domain rules when rewriting: local_inline_notes_evidence (801-816) + the layout de-prioritization at 941-953 (local inline notes beat a cloud `list` layout) and semantic_group_kind_family (754-762) which folds summary/sentence/note completion into one "completion" family. Also preserve the 20-issue truncation (1005-1011) - `passed` is false whenever truncated>0. | — |

The one reusable primitive is the normalization pair in llm_suggestions.rs:382-400 (normalized_quote_text and the hyphen-stripped fallback normalized_quote_text_without_hyphens, which exists because models reproduce line-broken words as "stencil- ling"). Two things a resolver must handle that the current code does not: (1) the pageIndex base mismatch - cloud/PdfImageExtractionV1 are 1-based (pageIndex==0 is rejected at llm_gateway.rs:1289-1292 and 1219-1225 in the answer validator) while DocumentIRV2 PageNodeV2.page_index and SourceAnchorV2.page_index are 0-based; (2) RectV2 (schema/common.rs:19-29) requires unit + origin + pageRotation + optional normalized[4], so a bare 4-float model bbox must be interpreted, not deserialized. Note there is currently NO bbox in the cloud contract at all, so the prompt/schema must start asking for it first. | — |
| medium | `F:/workspace/PDF2Test/src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs::collect_publish_gate_markers (333-435), called from the publish-proof builder at 287-317` | Must be updated in lockstep with 7.9 salvage or the new chain will silently become unpublishable. It walks the authoring doc plus authoring-ir.json plus pipeline-report.json and pushes into `partial_failures` on (a) any non-empty `failure` value at a path containing "cloud"/"llm"/"ai"/"vision"/"quality"/"audit" (393-406), (b) any `status` of partial\|partial_failure\|needs_review\|auto_apply_blocked under those same paths (377-391), (c) non-empty `failures` arrays under llm/ai paths and any non-empty blockedAutoApplyGroups/lowConfidenceGroups (367-375). Non-empty partial_failures => Err("authoring_v2_export_blocked:partial_failures=.."). A salvaged CloudRecognitionCandidateV1 with rejected_groups, or an outline call that merely failed, therefore blocks publish today. | — |

Separately, no request sets max_tokens and nothing ever inspects choices[0].finish_reason - a `"length"` truncation is indistinguishable from a malformed response and surfaces as llm_json_parse_failed.
