### 现有测试方式
All Rust tests for this subsystem live in the single `mod tests` at src-tauri/src/lib.rs:1481 (about 9,300 lines). There is no tests/ directory and no test module inside llm_gateway.rs or llm_suggestions.rs themselves, so anything new must either go into lib.rs's module or bring its own #[cfg(test)] block.

Three harness patterns to copy verbatim:

1. Real HTTP against a one-shot TCP mock. `mock_openai_server(content: Value) -> (String, mpsc::Receiver<String>, thread::JoinHandle<()>)` at lib.rs:1764-1788 binds 127.0.0.1:0, accepts exactly one connection, ships the raw request text back over a channel, and answers with {"choices":[{"message":{"content":<content serialized as a string>}}],"usage":{..}}; it returns the base_url as "http://{addr}/v1". `mock_openai_server_sequence(responses: Vec<(u16, String)>)` at lib.rs:10296-10319 does the same for N sequential responses and is how retry behaviour is proved. Helpers alongside: `read_mock_http_request`, `mock_profile_input(base_url)` (10321), `mock_group_input(base_url)` (10335), `mock_ok_body()` (10346), `tiny_png_bytes()` (1790), `cached_llm_inputs(root, job_id)` (1798). Exact tests to copy: `rust_llm_gateway_hits_mock_openai_compatible_chat_completion` (lib.rs:2246 - asserts "POST /v1/chat/completions ", "authorization: Bearer sk-mock-secret", "\"response_format\":{\"type\":\"json_object\"}" and evidence.source=="openai-compatible-rust"), `llm_gateway_retries_429_then_succeeds` (10363), `llm_gateway_call_record_persists_latency_and_error_class` (10394 - points at http://127.0.0.1:9/unreachable and reads jobs/<id>/llm-calls.jsonl), `rust_vision_gateway_sends_image_url_and_parses_transcription` (3187), `llm_json_parse_content_extracts_wrapped_json_and_rejects_garbage` (10419), `llm_suggestion_confidence_out_of_range_never_auto_applies` (10431), `llm_profile_save_rejects_public_http_unknown_provider_and_accepts_localhost` (10517).

2. Closure-injected gateway for pipeline behaviour - no HTTP at all. This is the pattern a new LlmTransport must keep working, and the 9 call sites that will break: `run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway(&root, &job.job_id, Some(AutoPipelineInput{ parse_mode, confidence_threshold, profile_id, execution_mode, target, allow_overwrite }), |_root, _job_id, command_name, _input, _api_key| match command_name { "extract_pdf_image_answers" => Ok(json!({..})), "generate_pdf_reading_outline" => Ok(json!({..})), _ => Err(..) })` at lib.rs:4403, 4521, 4772, 6030, 6268, 9737, 9974, 10134, plus `run_cloud_review_core_with_gateway` at lib.rs:10812. The best worked examples for the outline contract are `cloud_outline_completion_family_diff_does_not_override_local_inline_notes` (lib.rs:5983 - a full note-completion Q8-13 fixture with an answers block, proving the cloud `list` layoutHint must not override local inline_completion) and `cloud_outline_mismatch_is_persisted_as_authoring_audit_summary` (6221). `auto_pipeline_local_only_skips_all_pdf_cloud_calls` (9910) is the one that asserts executionMode:"localOnly" issues zero gateway calls - it will catch any change that makes cloud work unconditional. `cloud_review_write_back_preserves_concurrent_user_edit` (10754) guards the merge-on-write path.

3. Live-provider smoke tests, #[ignore]d and env-gated. `live_llm_profile_from_env()` (lib.rs:1811) reads EPIC8_LIVE_LLM_BASE_URL / EPIC8_LIVE_LLM_API_KEY / EPIC8_LIVE_LLM_MODEL; `live_rust_llm_gateway_provider_smoke_text_and_vision` (2315) and `live_llm_repair_contract_on_files_pdf_samples` (2401) run text + vision against a real endpoint and assert the cached inputs never contain the api key.

Shared fixtures: `temp_test_root()`, `ensure_app_dirs`, `make_job(CreateJobInput{..})`, `attach_fixture_source(&root, &mut job, "no-text.pdf"|"image-only.pdf", "MainQuestion")`, `save_job`, `ensure_job_dirs(&job_dir(&root, &job.job_id))`, `crate::llm_profiles::save_profiles(&root, &[json!({profileId,name,provider,baseUrl,model,temperature,timeoutMs,forceJson,enabled})])`, and `write_source_review_status(&root, &job_id, Some(&doc), true, None)` (needed or the pipeline stops at DocumentReview). Every test ends with `let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(root);`. Env-mutating tests must take `env_test_lock().lock()` (lib.rs:1858).

Schema round-trip tests for a new CloudRecognitionCandidateV1 belong in src-tauri/src/schema/mod.rs's own `mod tests` (schema/mod.rs:17-40), which has a generic `fn round_trip<T>(value: Value) -> T` asserting serialize/deserialize idempotence - copy `authoring_ir_v2_round_trip_preserves_shared_slot_contract` (schema/mod.rs, ~line 163).

I did not run cargo build or cargo test (instructed not to); everything above is read from source.

### 意外发现
- The concurrency problem plan 26.3-D attacks is not on the live path. The shipped UI never triggers the Mutex<FnMut> worker: src/features/import/useImportFiles.ts:149 calls runAutoPipeline with executionMode:"localOnly" (which forces cloud_diagnostics_opted_in=false at auto_pipeline.rs:1395, so profile_id is None and cloud_worker_spawned is false at 1416), then pools runCloudReview separately at 155. run_cloud_review_core_with_gateway (2217) has no Mutex, no thread and no `+ Send` bound - it runs the outline and the answer-key extraction strictly one after the other. src/features/editor/ExamWorkspacePage.tsx:115-120 does the same. So the Mutex is dead weight on the real path and the actual serializer is a plainly sequential function.
- A failed cloud call blocks V2 publish. cloud_outline_report_from_output (auto_pipeline.rs:1132-1140) writes the raw internal error string into `failure`, which lands in pipeline-report.json at quality.cloudComparison.failure (2170/2185). authoring_v2_commands.rs collect_publish_gate_markers (393-406) reads pipeline-report.json and pushes any non-empty `failure` under a path containing "cloud" into partial_failures, and a non-empty partial_failures returns Err("authoring_v2_export_blocked:partial_failures=..") (311-316). So a provider outage or an over-strict outline validator makes a perfectly good local draft unpublishable until the report is regenerated. Same mechanism catches llm.failures and blockedAutoApplyGroups.
- answerKey is mandatory in CloudReadingOutlineV1. validate_cloud_outline_output:1206 calls normalize_answer_map_value_checked(obj.get("answerKey"), ..), which returns Err("cloud_outline_answer_key_missing") when the field is absent. A question paper with no printed answer key can only pass if the model emits answerKey:{}. Worse, normalize_question_number_key (1000-1007) accepts only 1..=200 after stripping a leading q/Q, so a single key like "8-9" or "8 and 9" rejects the entire outline.
- layoutHint is never validated against its own contract. Both the prompt (llm_gateway.rs:487, "layoutHint must be inline_completion, table, or list when known") and the outputContract (llm_suggestions.rs:239) constrain it, but the validator only checks presence: `let _layout_hint = group_obj.get("layoutHint").and_then(Value::as_str).ok_or_else(..)?` (1219-1223). The unvalidated string then goes into a raw != comparison in cloud_outline_comparison (935-964), so any synonym the model invents becomes a user-visible cloud_group_layout_mismatch issue.
- catch_unwind cannot work in release. run_cloud_conversion_worker (auto_pipeline.rs:1238) wraps its body in std::panic::catch_unwind(AssertUnwindSafe(..)) and the comment promises a panic "must degrade into an Err outcome (local draft keeps working)", but Cargo.toml sets panic = "abort" under [profile.release]. The guarantee holds only in dev/test builds; in a shipped build a panic in the cloud worker takes the app down.
- The cloud outline model never sees the page manifest, or even the page list. cloud_outline_prompt (llm_gateway.rs:484-492) serializes only input.job, input.sourceFile and input.outputContract. `pages` and `extractionWarnings` are built into the request by make_cloud_paper_generation_input (llm_suggestions.rs:220-221) but are used only by append_pdf_images_to_content in the image-fallback branch (740-745). Meanwhile parser.rs already computes exactly the coverage record plan 7.12 asks for - coverageStatus/partialCoverage/renderedPageCount/coverage.missingPageIndexes (1995-2045) - and none of it is forwarded anywhere.
- Cloud outline evidence quotes are never verified against the source. The validator requires non-empty evidence.quotes with pageIndex>=1 and non-empty text (1273-1300), but nothing ever checks that the quoted text exists in the PDF. The codebase does have a quote verifier - llm_suggestion_quote_mismatches (llm_suggestions.rs:402) with whitespace and hyphen tolerance - but it is keyed by V1 blockId and is wired only into the group-suggestion apply path (llm_commands.rs:355, auto_pipeline.rs:1763). A model can fabricate every outline quote and cloud_outline_comparison will happily accept the structure.
- pageIndex base mismatch across the boundary the resolver must cross. The cloud contract and PdfImageExtractionV1 are 1-based - pageIndex==0 is an explicit error at llm_gateway.rs:1289-1292 (outline quotes) and 1219-1225 (answer evidence). DocumentIRV2 PageNodeV2.page_index (schema/document_ir_v2.rs:296) and SourceAnchorV2.page_index (schema/common.rs:69) are 0-based, as the schema/mod.rs fixtures confirm. Plan 7.7 lists this as a permitted normalization "only when the request declares it"; today nothing declares it because no cross-boundary resolution happens.
- The connectivity test is held to the full IELTS suggestion contract. test_llm_profile_core (llm_commands.rs:173) sends command_name "test_profile", which llm_gateway.rs:37-39 routes to run_openai_compatible_group_llm and therefore through validate_llm_suggestion_output. A reachable, correctly configured provider that returns valid JSON of the wrong shape reports "LLM gateway failed". It also writes into a synthetic job directory jobs/profile-test/ that no cleanup path owns.
- No max_tokens, no finish_reason. None of the four request builders sets max_tokens, and choices[0].finish_reason is never read anywhere in the gateway. A response truncated at the context limit is indistinguishable from a malformed one and surfaces as llm_json_parse_failed - which is precisely the silent-truncation failure plan 26.3-C says must be impossible.
- A whole 128 MiB PDF is base64-inlined into a single blocking JSON request body. data_url_for_pdf (llm_gateway.rs:559-577) reads the file up to MAX_LLM_PDF_BYTES = 128 MiB and embeds it as a data URL, so worst case is roughly 171 MB of base64 held in memory inside the request body, with a 16 MiB cap only on the response. There is no page-count or byte precheck, and no sharding.
- openai_post builds a brand-new reqwest::blocking::Client on every attempt (llm_gateway.rs:379-383). Nothing is pooled: three retries mean three TLS handshakes to the same host. Making the client a clonable field of the new LlmTransport fixes this and the Send+Sync requirement at the same time.
- Retry-After takes a round trip through a formatted error string. openai_post_once parses the header (401-406), then re-encodes it as a ";retry_after_ms=<ms>" suffix on the error message (421-425), which retry_after_ms_from_error (339) then re-parses. That function also handles a ";retry_after=<seconds>" form which nothing in the repo ever produces. Both parsers cap at 5_000 ms.
- The single profile timeout is both the per-request timeout and the total retry budget. openai_post computes one deadline (291) and hands the remaining budget to each attempt as its request timeout, so an attempt that consumes the full clamped timeoutMs (max 300 s) leaves nothing for attempts 2 and 3 and the call ends as llm_timeout_budget_exhausted rather than actually retrying.
- The 13 allowed question kinds are duplicated four times and can drift silently: validator.rs:41-58 (gateway validators), llm_suggestions.rs:329-346 (auto-apply gate), llm_gateway.rs:441-455 (array joined into llm_prompt), and a fourth prose copy inside cloud_outline_prompt at llm_gateway.rs:487. They happen to agree today.
- Everything in this subsystem is serde_json::Value. There is not a single struct or enum in llm_gateway.rs or llm_suggestions.rs - no LlmProvider enum (provider is a raw string compared at llm_gateway.rs:146), no CloudReadingOutlineV1 type (the name appears exactly once in the repo, as a string literal at llm_suggestions.rs:235), no typed error. Meanwhile schema/ already has fully typed, deny_unknown_fields versions of everything plan 7.3 needs, so the typed candidate contract is mostly assembly rather than new modelling.
- pdfium serializes globally regardless of any gateway change. pdf_geometry.rs:31-42 keeps a static PDFIUM_INSTANCE: OnceLock<Mutex<Result<Pdfium,String>>> and hands out a guard, so two concurrent run_cloud_review commands queue behind each other for page rendering even after the gateway mutex is gone.

---

## auto_pipeline orchestration (src-tauri/src/auto_pipeline.rs: run_auto_pipeline + run_cloud_review)
**数据流**：run_auto_pipeline (localOnly, the only in-app shape), step by step with line numbers in auto_pipeline.rs:

1. 1391-1397 parse options. parse_mode default "auto"; confidence_threshold default 0.85 clamped; local_only = execution_mode == Some("localOnly") exactly (1394); cloud_diagnostics_opted_in = !local_only && profile_id.is_some() (1395); target default "editableDraft" (1396); allow_overwrite default false (1397).
2. 1399-1406 load job.json, ensure the 15 job subdirs (util.rs:223-243), then HARD GUARD: if authoring-ir.json exists and !allow_overwrite -> Err("editable_draft_exists; pass allowOverwrite=true before regenerating draft").
3. 1411-1418 profile selection (skipped entirely when localOnly), Mutex::new(llm_gateway), cloud_worker_spawned = selected_profile_id.is_some() && main_source_is_pdf, two mpsc channels created unconditionally.
4. 1420-1435 thread::scope; the worker is spawned only if cloud_worker_spawned. In the in-app path it is not, so the channels are never used and extraction_rx.recv() is never called.
5. 1438-1479 if document-ir.json is absent: parse_source_document into cache/parser/<job>-document-ir.json, write document-ir.json, write source-review.json, update job.json to NeedsReview|Working + WorkflowStep::DocumentReview.
6. 1487-1518 physical V2 shadow: only when current_physical_shadow() is None AND main source file_type == "pdf" AND the upload exists AND document-ir.json parses -> write_pdf_facts_shadow_with_v1 into document-ir-v2.shadow.json; on error writes document-ir-v2.shadow.error.json. DOCX is silently skipped here (the manual parse_document path does handle docx, authoring_commands.rs:186-230).
7. 1539-1544 re-read document-ir.json into `doc`; physical_shadow = current_physical_shadow (validated against schemaVersion==DocumentIRV2 + jobId + source file_id + sha256, 249-266); needs_pdf_vision_transcription computed (this internally recomputes make_dynamic_split_candidates, auto_pipeline.rs:186).
8. 1553-1650 vision transcription — requires profile_id, so dead in-app. Diagnostic only: it never replaces DocumentIRV1 (applied is hardcoded false at 1509-equivalent, 1584-1600).
9. 1663-1683 source_review_status -> parser warnings + low-confidence blocks; make_dynamic_split_candidates; parse_answer_source_candidates; merge_answer_source_candidates; write split-candidates.json; job -> Working/Split.
10. 1684-1690 ir = make_dynamic_authoring_ir(job, split, doc); write authoring-ir.json; job -> DraftSaved/Authoring. This is the point after which the guard in step 2 will refuse a re-run.
11. 1698-1832 group repair loop — gated on `!local_only && !main_source_is_pdf(&job)` (1698). So it runs only for non-PDF sources with a profile, one serial extract_group LLM call per group under the gateway mutex, auto-applying at confidence >= threshold unless llm_suggestion_auto_apply_issues or quote mismatches block it.
12. 1834-1848 recompute answerKey / questionOrder / questionDisplayMap on the IR.
13. 1858-1868 join the cloud worker (skipped in-app), then persist vision-answer-output.json + vision-answer-candidates.json (1276-1360) and append the vision-answer audit issue.
14. 1922-1929 refresh_authoring_review_state; bump audit.revision, set audit.llmUsed/updatedAt.
15. 1931-2024 cloud comparison report + cloud_comparison_summary audit issue (skipped in-app).
16. 2026 write authoring-ir.json (final V1).
17. 2027-2034 write_pipeline_authoring_v2_shadow -> authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json + .compare.json, atomically. UNCONDITIONAL apart from the hardcoded-true flag.
18. 2036-2046 validate_for_runtime_gate -> report; static_runtime_passed = passed && runtime.mode == "static-rust".
19. 2048-2070 v2 quality gate: build_authoring_v2_shadow is called AGAIN, purely to read .quality out of it. On Err it reports hardFailures:["QUALITY_GATE_EVALUATION_FAILED"].
20. 2072-2110 status/step/route decision. has_review_blocks ORs low-confidence groups, blocked auto-apply, parser review, cloud confirmation, remaining authoring review, and v2_quality_requires_review. next_status: NeedsReview if blocked, else DraftSaved when target=="editableDraft".
21. 2119-2143 update_job with the summed issue counts.
22. 2145-2185 build and write pipeline-report.json.
23. 2186 minimize_process_artifacts_after_authoring — writes authoring-project.json then DELETES document-ir.json, split-candidates.json, pipeline-report.json (the file just written at 2185), validation-report.json, publish-readiness-report.json, cache/, preview/, llm-suggestions/, llm-calls.jsonl and the root parser cache entries. Gated only on config/diagnostics-settings.json keepFullProcessArtifacts, which defaults to false (diagnostics.rs:16-22).

Then the workspace: getAuthoringV2 -> get_authoring_v2_core (authoring_v2_commands.rs:54) -> build_editor_session -> load_current_authoring -> revision 0 so it reads authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json, revalidates it with validate_authoring (830-837), and returns {authoring, revision:0, source:"shadow"}. The first apply_authoring_v2_patches goes through the same loader, then append_revision writes authoring/revisions/, after which the shadow is never read again for that job.

run_cloud_review (auto_pipeline.rs:2217-2668), which the import flow fires per PDF after the local pass:
- 2227-2232 load job, ensure dirs, read authoring-ir.json or hard-fail.
- 2233 select_llm_profile with the requested id or an auto-pick.
- 2242-2322 if a profile resolved and the source is a PDF: main_pdf_vision_extraction RE-RENDERS the PDF page images (cache/vision was deleted by the pipeline's cleanup), then cloud_outline_check_for_job and vision_answer_candidate_for_job run sequentially on this one thread.
- 2338-2420 merge-on-write: re-read authoring-ir.json and replay only the cloud_comparison_summary issue onto the fresh content so a concurrent user save is not clobbered; append_authoring_audit_issue replaces same-kind issues so the replay is idempotent (1014-1049).
- 2427 write authoring-ir.json.
- 2430-2456 source_doc = read_json_opt("document-ir.json") — which the pipeline already DELETED, so this is None; split is then rebuilt from a None document; build_authoring_v2_shadow is called on that degraded input purely to compute quality.v2Gate. The result is NOT written to disk: run_cloud_review never calls write_pipeline_authoring_v2_shadow.
- 2478-2665 recompute status/step from a pipeline-report.json that no longer exists (so the fallback literal at 2501-2536 is used, meaning lowConfidenceGroups/blockedAutoApplyGroups always read as 0), update_job, then write pipeline-report.json back.

### 关键签名
- `run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway` — `pub(crate) fn run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway<F>(     root: &Path,     job_id: &str,     input: Option<AutoPipelineInput>,     mut llm_gateway: F, ) -> CommandResult<Value> where     F: FnMut(&Path, &str, &str, &Value, Option<&str>) -> CommandResult<Value> + Send,`
  - The `+ Send` bound exists only because of the scoped cloud worker thread. Returns the pipeline report Value; also the value written to pipeline-report.json at 2185 (which is then deleted at 2186).
- `run_auto_pipeline_core` — `pub(crate) fn run_auto_pipeline_core(     root: &Path,     job_id: &str,     input: Option<AutoPipelineInput>, ) -> CommandResult<Value> {     run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway(root, job_id, input, run_llm_gateway) }`
  - Production entry. Takes no AppHandle and no channel — already shaped for a queue worker to call directly.
- `run_cloud_review_core_with_gateway` — `pub(crate) fn run_cloud_review_core_with_gateway<F>(     root: &Path,     job_id: &str,     input: Option<RunCloudReviewInput>,     mut llm_gateway: F, ) -> CommandResult<Value> where     F: FnMut(&Path, &str, &str, &Value, Option<&str>) -> CommandResult<Value>,`
  - No `+ Send` — it is entirely single-threaded. Hard-fails at 2231-2232 with "authoring_ir_missing_for_cloud_review" if authoring-ir.json is absent.
- `AutoPipelineInput` — `pub struct AutoPipelineInput {     #[serde(rename = "profileId")]     pub profile_id: Option<String>,     #[serde(rename = "confidenceThreshold")]     pub confidence_threshold: Option<f64>,     #[serde(rename = "parseMode")]     pub parse_mode: Option<String>,     #[serde(rename = "executionMode")]     pub execution_mode: Option<String>,     pub target: Option<String>,     #[serde(rename = "allowOverwrite")]     pub allow_overwrite: Option<bool>, }`
  - executionMode and target are free-form Option<String>, not enums; unknown values are silently accepted.
- `RunCloudReviewInput` — `pub struct RunCloudReviewInput {     #[serde(rename = "profileId")]     pub profile_id: Option<String>, }`
  - No executionMode/target. profileId is optional and, unlike the auto pipeline, a None here still falls through to select_llm_profile's auto-pick (2233), so cloud review can fire with no explicit profile.
- `run_auto_pipeline (tauri command)` — `#[tauri::command] async fn run_auto_pipeline(     job_id: String,     input: Option<AutoPipelineInput>,     app: AppHandle, ) -> CommandResult<Value> {     let root = app_root(&app)?;     tauri::async_runtime::spawn_blocking(move || run_auto_pipeline_core(&root, &job_id, input))         .await         .map_err(|error| error.to_string())? }`
  - The entire pipeline is one blocking task. No progress reporting, no cancellation token, no event emit.
- `run_cloud_review (tauri command)` — `#[tauri::command] async fn run_cloud_review(     job_id: String,     input: Option<RunCloudReviewInput>,     app: AppHandle, ) -> CommandResult<Value> {     let root = app_root(&app)?;     tauri::async_runtime::spawn_blocking(move || run_cloud_review_core(&root, &job_id, input))         .await         .map_err(|error| error.to_string())? }`
  - 
- `write_pipeline_authoring_v2_shadow` — `fn write_pipeline_authoring_v2_shadow(     dir: &Path,     job: &ImportJob,     authoring: &Value,     split: &Value,     document: Option<&Value>,     physical_shadow: Option<&Value>, ) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - THE critical function. Returns Ok(()) even when the shadow could not be produced: the Err arm at 300-312 deletes the shadow and compare files and writes authoring-ir-v2.shadow.error.json instead.
- `write_authoring_v2_shadow` — `pub(crate) fn write_authoring_v2_shadow(     job_dir: &Path,     job: &ImportJob,     v1_authoring: &Value,     split: &Value,     v1_document: Option<&Value>,     physical_shadow: Option<&Value>,     output_path: &Path, ) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - Writes output_path and job_dir/authoring-ir-v2.shadow.compare.json, both via write_canonical_json_atomic (temp file + fsync + replace).
- `build_authoring_v2_shadow` — `pub(crate) fn build_authoring_v2_shadow(     job: &ImportJob,     v1_authoring: &Value,     split: &Value,     v1_document: Option<&Value>,     physical_shadow: Option<&Value>, ) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - Pure. Only three error paths in the whole body: the Option-`?` at 229 (not an error), serde_json::to_value of the instruction signature at 322, and `serde_json::from_value::<IeltsAuthoringIRV2>(authoring.clone()).map_err(|error| format!("authoring_ir_v2_shadow_schema_validation_failed:{error}"))?` at 377-378.
- `load_current_authoring` — `fn load_current_authoring(root: &Path, job_id: &str) -> CommandResult<(Value, u64)> {     let current = recover_current_revision(root, job_id)?;     if current.revision > 0 {         let value = read_revision(root, job_id, current.revision)?;         validate_authoring(&value)?;         return Ok((value, current.revision));     }      let path = job_dir(root, job_id).join(AUTHORING_V2_SHADOW_FILE);     let value = read_json_opt(&path)?.ok_or_else(|| {         format!(             "AUTHORING_V2_NOT_AVAILABLE:shadow_missing:{}",             path.display()         )     })?;     validate_authoring(&value)?;     Ok((value, 0)) }`
  - This is the exact seam between the pipeline and the workspace. Note revision > 0 wins permanently — the shadow becomes dead weight after the first save.
- `run_cloud_conversion_worker` — `fn run_cloud_conversion_worker<F>(     root: &Path,     job: &ImportJob,     profile_id: &str,     shared_gateway: &Mutex<F>,     extraction_tx: mpsc::Sender<Result<(Value, PathBuf), String>>,     worker_tx: mpsc::Sender<CloudWorkerOutcome>, ) where     F: FnMut(&Path, &str, &str, &Value, Option<&str>) -> CommandResult<Value>,`
  - Wraps its body in catch_unwind (1239) so a panic degrades to Err rather than hanging recv().

With a cloud profile enabled, the workspace cannot open the item until the cloud calls finish. | `F:/workspace/PDF2Test/src-tauri/src/auto_pipeline.rs:1685 vs 1858-1868 vs 2027` |

Batch fan-out and both concurrency limits live in the frontend pool (useImportFiles.ts:148,155). | `F:/workspace/PDF2Test/src-tauri/src/db.rs:143-190; job_store.rs:29-57; src/features/import/useImportFiles.ts:142-162` |

Replace the `F: FnMut` + Mutex abstraction with a Send+Sync transport trait (an &dyn LlmTransport or a cloneable client), so vision answer, outline and per-group repair can be in flight simultaneously, with a cloud semaphore rather than a mutex providing the concurrency limit. Every generic signature in this file threads that same FnMut bound, so this is a wide but mechanical change: run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway, run_cloud_review_core_with_gateway, run_cloud_conversion_worker, vision_transcription_for_job_with_gateway, vision_transcription_with_extraction, vision_answer_candidate_for_job, cloud_outline_check_for_job, cloud_outline_generate_with_gateway. | ~14 tests in lib.rs pass closures that mutate captured counters (e.g. lib.rs:9974 `gateway_calls += 1`); they must move to interior mutability (AtomicUsize / Mutex<Vec<_>>) before the FnMut bound can be dropped. |

WHERE lease expired. job_artifact_index is declared and never written by any code — decide whether it becomes the artifact registry or gets dropped. Move job state into the DB as the record of truth, or at minimum use the existing write_canonical_json_atomic (artifact_store.rs:504-540) plus an fs2 file lock like append_revision already does. | — |

The button can therefore never succeed. Either pass allowOverwrite:true (matching line 115) or point it at a new shadow-repair command. | — |

The UI has no way to distinguish "shadow never built" from other load failures except by string-matching the error. Add shadow presence + last shadow error to this payload; get_job_detail (job_commands.rs:99-114) also omits the V2 shadow entirely. | — |

### 现有测试方式
Where the tests live. auto_pipeline.rs has its own `mod tests` at 2670-2796 but it only covers four pure helpers: v2_quality_gate_off_preserves_legacy_reliability, v2_quality_gate_on_requires_ready_report_and_slots, v2_quality_review_count_never_hides_a_non_ready_state, physical_shadow_freshness_requires_schema_job_source_and_hash. Neither core function is tested from this file. Everything end-to-end lives in lib.rs's #[cfg(test)] mod (starts ~1500).

The pattern to copy (verbatim shape from lib.rs:4320-4327):
    let root = temp_test_root();                       // lib.rs:1576, env::temp_dir()/epic8-test-<uuid>
    ensure_app_dirs(&root).unwrap();
    let mut job = test_job();                          // lib.rs:1540, make_job(CreateJobInput{..})
    attach_fixture_source(&root, &mut job, "complex-reading.txt", "MainQuestion");  // lib.rs:1829
    save_job(&root, &job).unwrap();
    let report = run_auto_pipeline_core(&root, &job.job_id, None).unwrap();
Fixtures resolve through parser_fixture (lib.rs:1580) = <CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR>/../fixtures/parser/<name>; useful names in existing tests: complex-reading.txt, no-text.pdf. The 8-PDF golden corpus is reached through phase4_pdf_fixtures (lib.rs:1588) reading fixtures/golden/phase4-eight-pdf-acceptance.json and fixtures/golden/manifest.json.

To exercise the cloud path, use run_auto_pipeline_core_with_gateway with a closure as the 4th argument and pass AutoPipelineInput { execution_mode: None, profile_id: Some(id), .. } — profile_id alone is not enough only in the sense that execution_mode must not be "localOnly". You must first write an enabled profile with crate::llm_profiles::save_profiles(&root, &[json!({...,"enabled":true})]). Closest existing models: lib.rs:9909 auto_pipeline_local_only_skips_all_pdf_cloud_calls (asserts gateway_calls == 0), lib.rs:6221 cloud_outline_mismatch_is_persisted_as_authoring_audit_summary, lib.rs:10754 cloud_review_write_back_preserves_concurrent_user_edit (uses a gateway closure that writes authoring-ir.json mid-call to prove the merge-on-write at 2338-2420). Note every one of these closures mutates a captured local, which is why the F: FnMut + Mutex bound exists.

To keep intermediates for assertions, write diagnostics settings first — write_diagnostics_settings(&root, &DiagnosticsSettings { keep_full_process_artifacts: true }) (used at lib.rs:4443, 8090, 9679, 9915) — otherwise minimize_process_artifacts_after_authoring deletes document-ir.json, split-candidates.json and pipeline-report.json before your assertions run.

The gap this subsystem most needs closed: there is NO test anywhere that runs run_auto_pipeline_core and then get_authoring_v2_core (or asserts authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json exists). grep for authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json across src-tauri/src outside ielts_grammar returns only the two constant declarations and nas_package_v2/authoring_v2_commands consumers. The real-PDF pipeline test that would have caught it, lib.rs:10060 files_pdf_samples_auto_pipeline_minimizes_artifacts_and_preserves_review_gate, is #[ignore]d ("superseded by the registered Phase 4 eight-PDF corpus; requires removed legacy Files/*.pdf inputs"), and the active 8-PDF suite (ielts_grammar/real_pdf_acceptance.rs:1282 process_fixture, build at 1369) calls build_authoring_v2_shadow directly with a freshly computed physical shadow — it never goes through run_auto_pipeline, never through write_pipeline_authoring_v2_shadow, and never through load_current_authoring. Add a test that does exactly: attach a golden PDF, run_auto_pipeline_core, assert job_dir/authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json exists, then get_authoring_v2_core(&root, &job_id).unwrap() and assert source == "shadow" and revision == 0.

I did not run cargo build or cargo test (instructed not to), so every claim here is from reading source; I have not observed a live failure of the schema validation at ielts_grammar/mod.rs:377.

### 意外发现
- CRITICAL ANSWER — a fresh import does attempt the V2 artifact unconditionally, but nothing guarantees it lands, and the failure is silent. write_pipeline_authoring_v2_shadow is called at auto_pipeline.rs:2027 on every successful run with no executionMode / target / profile / source-type gate. The only flag is authoring_v2_shadow_enabled(), which is `pub(crate) fn authoring_v2_shadow_enabled() -> bool { true }` (environment.rs:223-225) — hardcoded, no env var. The single real gate is content: build_authoring_v2_shadow's final `serde_json::from_value::<IeltsAuthoringIRV2>(authoring.clone()).map_err(|error| format!("authoring_ir_v2_shadow_schema_validation_failed:{error}"))?` at ielts_grammar/mod.rs:377-378 (IeltsAuthoringIRV2 and every nested struct are #[serde(deny_unknown_fields)] with all fields required). If that fails, auto_pipeline.rs:301-302 DELETES authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json and .compare.json, writes authoring-ir-v2.shadow.error.json, and returns Ok(()) — the pipeline reports success and the workspace then fails with AUTHORING_V2_NOT_AVAILABLE:shadow_missing:<path> from authoring_v2_commands.rs:732-737. So: gated by a schema-validation outcome, not a flag; not covered by any test at that seam; and unrecoverable, because both regeneration doors (auto_pipeline.rs:1402 and authoring_commands.rs:357) refuse while authoring-ir.json exists.
- The in-app product path never makes a cloud call from run_auto_pipeline and never spawns the worker thread. cloud_diagnostics_opted_in = !local_only && options.profile_id.is_some() (1395); all four callers pass executionMode:'localOnly' and none passes profileId. The scoped thread, the Mutex<F>, both mpsc channels and the entire vision-transcription branch are dead code in production. The only caller that activates them is the CLI live-API path (lib.rs:585-596). Cloud work reaches the product exclusively through the separate, entirely single-threaded run_cloud_review.
- Note the asymmetry: run_auto_pipeline requires an explicitly passed profileId before it will even consider cloud (1395), while run_cloud_review passes options.profile_id straight into select_llm_profile (2233), which falls back to job.active_llm_profile_id and then auto-picks any enabled profile (115-168). So ExamWorkspacePage.tsx:120's runCloudReview(itemId) with no arguments can fire real LLM calls against an auto-selected profile.
- run_auto_pipeline writes pipeline-report.json at 2185 and then deletes it one line later at 2186 via minimize_process_artifacts_after_authoring (cleanup.rs:178). The Value is still returned to the caller so nothing visibly breaks, but run_cloud_review's read_json_opt of that same file at 2500 always misses, silently falling back to a literal whose lowConfidenceGroups/blockedAutoApplyGroups are empty arrays — so cloud review's has_review_blocks can never see LLM review debt the local pass recorded.
- target:'editableDraft' is effectively inert. It is read at exactly two places — 2080 (next_status becomes DraftSaved rather than ExportReady) and 2090 (next_step forced to WorkflowStep::Authoring) — never gates an artifact, is the default at 1396, and is the only value any caller sends (the TS type is literally `target?: "editableDraft"`, tauriCommands.ts:250). Similarly executionMode is matched against the bare string "localOnly" with no validation, so a casing typo would silently enable cloud.
- The V2 shadow's task groups derive from split.questionGroupCandidates, not from the LLM-repaired IR. In run_auto_pipeline the LLM group-repair loop (1698-1832) mutates `ir` but never `split`, and `split` is what is handed to write_pipeline_authoring_v2_shadow at 2030. LLM-applied kind/layout/question changes reach the shadow only indirectly through find_v1_group lookups against v1_authoring.
- Once the user saves once, the shadow is dead. load_current_authoring returns the revision whenever revision > 0 (authoring_v2_commands.rs:727-731), and auto_pipeline.rs imports nothing from artifact_store — it never touches the revision chain. So 'rerun local recognition' rewrites the shadow and the editor keeps showing the old revision, with no warning.
- The V1 and V2 write paths use different durability. The shadow goes through write_canonical_json_atomic -> write_bytes_atomic (temp file, create_new, fsync, replace; artifact_store.rs:504-540). authoring-ir.json and every other V1 artifact go through util.rs:259-265 write_json, a plain non-atomic fs::write. Since read_json_opt returns Err on a parse failure rather than None (util.rs:252-257), a torn read of authoring-ir.json propagates through the `?` at auto_pipeline.rs:2340 and fails the entire cloud review.
- run_cloud_review re-renders every PDF page image. The pipeline's cleanup deletes <job_dir>/cache (cleanup.rs:174-175) including cache/vision/pdf-images.json and its assets, and run_cloud_review's first act for a PDF is main_pdf_vision_extraction (2243) which rebuilds them. In the import flow the two commands run back to back per file, so every scanned PDF is rendered twice.
- There is no event channel at all. lib.rs:88 is `use tauri::{AppHandle, Manager};` — no Emitter import, and grep for `emit` over src-tauri/src matches only prose in comments. Plan §5.4 and §12.6's processing://item-updated does not exist in any form, so 'progress' today is whatever the frontend infers between awaited commands.
- Both quality_gate_v2_enabled() and authoring_v2_shadow_enabled() being hardcoded true has a product consequence beyond the shadow: quality_gate_requires_review returns true for any state other than "ready" (228-230), and evaluate_quality only returns "ready" when documentScore >= 0.95, no task score < 0.92, sourceCoverage >= 0.995 and issues is empty (quality.rs:186-199). So essentially every real import lands in JobStatus::NeedsReview.
- A DOCX import through run_auto_pipeline gets no physical evidence at all: the physical-shadow block filters file_type == "pdf" (1488-1490), so physical_shadow is None, coverageStatus.physicalShadow is "missing", and the quality gate can never reach "ready". The manual parse_document command does handle docx (authoring_commands.rs:186-230). The two entry points into the same artifact disagree.
- ingest_jobs and job_artifact_index exist in db.rs (143-190) but are not a queue and, for job_artifact_index, are never written by any code. The real state of record is the job.json file, dual-written to SQLite on a best-effort basis by save_job (job_store.rs:33-44) which only eprintln!s on DB failure.

## Runtime compile + NAS publish: src-tauri/src/reading_source_v2.rs (IeltsAuthoringIRV2 -> ReadingExamSourceV2 compiler + semantic validator) and src-tauri/src/nas_package_v2.rs (publish_nas_package_v2 lock/CAS/staging/probe/manifest-last/atomic-commit/journal-recovery)
**数据流**：END-TO-END, as actually wired today (src/api/publishClient.ts publishItem, L46-68):

1. `getAuthoringV2(itemId)` -> read session, take `session.revision`.
2. `exportAuthoringV2({ jobId: itemId, exportDir: destination, revision })` -> `export_authoring_v2_core` (authoring_v2_commands.rs L449). It takes a per-job flock at `<job>/export-history/phase5-export.lock`, loads the current revision (revision 0 = the `authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json` shadow, >0 = `authoring/revisions/{n}.json`), calls `refresh_quality_report` which REPLACES `authoring.quality` in memory (not persisted), rejects non-`ready` quality, computes `publish_proof = validate_authoring_v2_publish_readiness(...)`, then `compile_reading_source_v2(&authoring)`. It stages `authoring-ir-v2.json` + `reading-source-v2.json` + `manifest-v2.json` (the AuthoringV2ExportReceiptV1 carrying `authoringSha256`, `runtimeSha256`, and the embedded `publishProof`) plus every asset copied at its job-relative path, then `fs::rename(staging_dir, output_dir)` where output_dir = `<exportDir>/{safeExamId}-r{revision}-{uuid}`.
3. `exportNasPackageV2({ libraryRoot: destination, sourcePath: receipt.runtimePath, assetRoot: receipt.outputDir, examId, minimumRuntimeVersion: "0.2.0" })` -> `publish_nas_package_v2` (lib.rs L1209) -> `publish_nas_package_v2_core`.

INSIDE publish_nas_package_v2_core (nas_package_v2.rs L245-350), in order:
- `normalize_nas_library_root(absolute_path("library_root", ...))` strips a trailing `publish/` component so `<X>` and `<X>/publish` share one transaction root; `absolute_path` rejects relative paths with `nas_package_v2_library_root_must_be_absolute`.
- Read `source_path` bytes (`nas_package_v2_source_read:...`).
- `validate_v2_export_binding` (L56-206): filename must be `reading-source-v2.json`; sibling `manifest-v2.json` must deserialize as `AuthoringV2ExportReceiptV1` with exactly the 3-file `files` list, matching `jobId`/`revision` against the optional input, `examId`/`sourceDocumentId` matching the sibling authoring IR, `reviewRequired == false`, and `sha256(authoring bytes)`/`sha256(source bytes)` matching the receipt. Then it re-reads the job's persisted authoring (`<app_root>/jobs/<jobId>/authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json` for revision 0, else `authoring/revisions/{n}.json`) and compares it to the exported authoring with `quality` stripped (`authoring_binding_value`). Then it re-compiles the bound authoring and requires `exported_source == bound_source`. Finally it re-runs `validate_authoring_v2_publish_readiness` against the LIVE job dir and requires exact `Value` equality with the receipt's `publishProof`.
- Deserialize into `ReadingExamSourceV2` (`nas_package_v2_source_contract:...`), run `validate_reading_source_v2` (`nas_package_v2_source_invalid:<json array of CompilerIssueV2>`), derive `safe_exam_id` from the raw JSON and cross-check the optional `examId` input.
- `reading_root = nas_reading_exams_dir(library_root)` — which is the normalized library root ITSELF, so `manifest.js`, `{examId}.js` and `resources/{examId}/` all live directly in the destination the user picked. `make_paths` then puts the lock at `<root>/.publish-control/export.lock`, backups under `<root>/.publish-control/backups/{examId}-{exportId}`, the journal at `<root>/.publish-control/{examId}-{exportId}.journal.json`, and the report at `<root>/publish/reports/{examId}-{exportId}.json`.
- `OpenOptions::new().create(true).read(true).write(true).open(lock_path)` then `try_lock_exclusive()` (fs2 flock). Non-blocking: a concurrent publisher fails fast with `nas_package_v2_lock_busy:...`. This is also the de-facto "destination writable" probe, but `fs::create_dir_all(&reading_root)` at L287 runs FIRST and surfaces a BARE, unprefixed OS error on a read-only share.
- `write_lock_metadata` (pid + heartbeat), `recover_incomplete_transactions` (replays every stale journal in `.publish-control` before doing anything new), capture `base_manifest_sha256`, optionally CAS against `expected_manifest_sha256`, write journal status `staging`.
- `stage_and_commit`, then on Err write journal `failed` + `fs::remove_dir_all(staging_root)`; always drop lock metadata and unlock.

INSIDE stage_and_commit (L352-568):
- For each `source.assets.assets` descriptor: `validate_package_relative_path` (rejects `\`, `:`, `://`, leading `/`, empty/`.`/`..` components, and the unicode slash lookalikes `⁄ ∕ ╱ ⧸`), `safe_join_asset_path(asset_root, relative_path)` to read, case-insensitive destination-collision check, then a plain `fs::copy` into `staging_root/resources/{examId}/{relativePath}`. No hash check here — the probe does that.
- Write `resources/{examId}/asset-manifest.json` (canonical JSON of `ExamAssetManifestV2`, keyed by assetId) and `staging_root/{examId}.js` = `build_wrapper(source_value)` (a `(function registerReadingExamData(global){...})` IIFE with pretty-printed JSON inline). `runtime_bytes`/`runtime_sha256` are computed from the canonicalized re-serialized struct but the raw runtime JSON is NEVER written into the package — only the wrapper is.
- `update_lock_heartbeat`, then `run_student_loader_probe(source, &asset_manifest, staging_resource_path)`. `!probe.passed` => `nas_package_v2_probe_failed:<json StudentProbeReportV2>`.
- `verify_manifest_compare_and_swap(manifest_path, base_manifest_sha256)` (second CAS, this time against the baseline taken after the lock), `load_existing_manifest` (a brace-balanced, string-aware JS scanner that accepts both the V2 `window.__READING_EXAM_MANIFEST__ = {...}` form and the legacy closure form), `validate_minimum_runtime_version` (must parse and be <= [0,2,0]), insert/overwrite this exam's manifest entry, rewrite `_meta` to `ReadingExamManifestV2`, write `staging_root/manifest.js`.
- Journal `committing` -> `commit_package` -> report JSON -> journal `committed` -> `cleanup_transaction_artifacts`.

INSIDE commit_package (L582-855):
- Type guards on the four live paths, then `state.json` (NasBackupStateV1) recording had_exam/had_resources/had_manifest/had_report.
- Backup order matters and is deliberate: the manifest is COPY-verified (`copy_file_verified`, stays live so a student loader never sees a missing manifest), while exam.js / resources/ / report.json are RENAMED into the backup.
- Commit: `rename(staging_exam)` -> `rename(staging_resources)` -> `atomic_replace_file(staging_manifest, manifest_path)` (plain rename on Unix; remove-then-rename fallback on Windows) -> journal `manifest_committed`. Manifest is genuinely last.
- Any failure runs the `rollback(manifest_committed)` closure, which first calls `validate_backup_contents` and REFUSES to delete any visible file if a declared backup is missing (it only restores already-moved paths and returns `backup_incomplete:missing=...`, leaving the journal+backup for the next recovery pass). Errors are joined with `|` and appended as `;rollback=<...>`; a clean rollback is `;rollback=complete`.
- Post-commit metadata failures (report dir/encode/write, committed journal) never return a plain error: `recover_post_commit_metadata_failure` (L573) replays `recover_incomplete_transactions` and appends `;rollback=complete` or `;rollback=incomplete:<err>`.

RECOVERY (recover_incomplete_transactions, L1257-1369) runs at the START of every publish while holding the lock. It scans `.publish-control/*.journal.json`, skips `committed`/`recovered`, validates `examId`/`exportId` with `safe_transaction_component` (<=128 chars, ASCII alnum + `-_.`, not leading `.`), re-derives all paths from the journal, and per item calls `restore_backup_file_or_keep`/`restore_backup_dir_or_keep` (idempotent: restore if the backup exists, else keep the live file, else fail with `nas_package_v2_recovery_backup_incomplete:{label}_missing`). Then it deletes staging + backup and rewrites the journal as `recovered`.

FRONTEND BATCH TODAY: `publishItems` (publishClient.ts L71-85) is a plain `for` loop over `publishItem`, i.e. N independent export+publish transactions, N manifest rewrites, N journals, N reports. Nothing rolls back item 1 when item 3 fails; the loop just partitions into `succeeded`/`failed`. LibraryPage.tsx L94 drives it and renders "正在发布 done/total"; ExamWorkspacePage.tsx L72 calls `publishItem` for the single-item button.

### 关键签名
- `compile_reading_source_v2` — `pub(crate) fn compile_reading_source_v2(     source: &IeltsAuthoringIRV2, ) -> Result<ReadingExamSourceV2, Vec<CompilerIssueV2>>`
  - Total function, no I/O. Returns Err only from validate_reading_source_v2 on the COMPILED output, except the one early return at L80-86 (RUNTIME_PASSAGE_MISSING, target_id = source.job_id) when source.passage is None. Ordering: answer_slots sorted by (question_number, slot_id) -> question_order + question_display_map (L86-96). Drops source.modality and source.listening silently and never checks source.schema_version == "IeltsAuthoringIRV2".
- `validate_reading_source_v2` — `pub(crate) fn validate_reading_source_v2(source: &ReadingExamSourceV2) -> Vec<CompilerIssueV2>`
  - Emits 24 distinct codes. Empty Vec == valid. Called from three places: compile (L124), publish_nas_package_v2_core L270, and run_student_loader_probe L56.
- `CompilerIssueV2` — `#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)] #[serde(rename_all = "camelCase", deny_unknown_fields)] pub(crate) struct CompilerIssueV2 {     pub code: String,     pub message: String,     pub target_id: String, }`
  - This is the only typed issue the compiler produces. No severity, no fix action, no source anchor - so plan 13.4's PublishBlocker { code, target_id, user_message, action } needs a new code->action map plus English->Chinese message mapping (all 24 messages are English).
- `ReadingExamSourceV2` — `#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq)] #[serde(rename_all = "camelCase", deny_unknown_fields)] pub(crate) struct ReadingExamSourceV2 {     pub schema_version: String,     pub exam_id: String,     pub meta: RuntimeExamMetaV2,     pub assets: RuntimeAssetManifestRefV2,     pub passage: RuntimePassageV2,     pub task_groups: Vec<TaskGroupV2>,     pub answer_slots: BTreeMap<String, AnswerSlotV2>,     pub answer_key: BTreeMap<String, AnswerValueV2>,     pub question_order: Vec<String>,     pub question_display_map: BTreeMap<String, String>,     pub audit: RuntimeAuditV2, }`
  - deny_unknown_fields + a hash-pinned JSON schema with additionalProperties:false. Adding any field is a cross-repo contract break; compile_with_report() must therefore return issues ALONGSIDE the struct, never inside it.
- `score_response_group` — `pub(crate) fn score_response_group(     group: &ResponseGroupV2,     answers: &BTreeMap<String, AnswerValueV2>,     answer_key: &BTreeMap<String, AnswerValueV2>, ) -> ScoreResultV2`
  - Referenced only from the test module in this same file (L701, L732, L773). No production caller anywhere in the crate - it is a scoring oracle for the JS student runtime, not shipped logic.
- `ExamCompiler / ReadingExamCompilerV2` — `pub(crate) trait ExamCompiler {     type Output;      fn compile(&self, source: &IeltsAuthoringIRV2) -> Result<Self::Output, Vec<CompilerIssueV2>>;     fn validate(&self, compiled: &Self::Output) -> Vec<CompilerIssueV2>; }`
  - ReadingExamCompilerV2 is the only impl and has no caller besides its own #[test] at L30-33. If compile_with_report() is added, this trait must gain a third method or be deleted.
- `publish_nas_package_v2_core` — `pub(crate) fn publish_nas_package_v2_core(root: &Path, input: Value) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - Synchronous. `root` is the APP data dir (app.path().app_data_dir(), lib.rs L357-359) and is used only to locate the job's persisted authoring revision for the binding check - it is NOT the NAS root. Returns Ok(json!({schemaVersion, status:"committed", examId, manifestPath, reportPath, probe, assetCount, exportId})) at L546-554 of stage_and_commit.
- `NasPackagePublishInput` — `#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)] #[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")] pub(crate) struct NasPackagePublishInput {     #[serde(alias = "exportDir")]     pub library_root: String,     #[serde(alias = "runtimePath", alias = "sourceFile")]     pub source_path: String,     pub asset_root: Option<String>,     pub exam_id: Option<String>,     pub minimum_runtime_version: Option<String>,     pub expected_manifest_sha256: Option<String>,     pub fault: Option<String>,     pub job_id: Option<String>,     pub revision: Option<u64>, }`
  - One item per call - no batch shape exists. `fault` is a live fault-injection hook on the real Tauri command, not #[cfg(test)]-gated, and is exposed in the TS input type. Accepted values: after_assets, after_source, before_manifest, report_write, committed_journal, backup_resources, manifest_rename.
- `validate_v2_export_binding` — `fn validate_v2_export_binding(     root: &Path,     input: &NasPackagePublishInput,     source_path: &Path,     source_bytes: &[u8], ) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - Hard-requires source_path.file_name() == "reading-source-v2.json" (L62) and siblings manifest-v2.json + authoring-ir-v2.json. Re-compiles the bound authoring IR and demands exported_source == bound_source (L196-200), then re-runs validate_authoring_v2_publish_readiness and demands manifest["publishProof"] == recomputed proof by full Value equality (L202-205). This is the wall a batch publisher must get through for every item.
- `stage_and_commit` — `fn stage_and_commit(     _app_root: &Path,     input: &NasPackagePublishInput,     source: &ReadingExamSourceV2,     source_value: &Value,     source_path: &Path,     paths: &PackagePaths,     export_id: &str, ) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - Single-exam by construction: one staging_exam_path, one staging_resource_path, one manifest merge, one journal entry, one report. Order is asset copy -> asset-manifest.json -> wrapper .js -> heartbeat -> probe -> CAS -> manifest merge -> staged manifest.js -> journal "committing" -> commit_package -> report -> journal "committed" -> cleanup.
- `commit_package` — `fn commit_package(     paths: &PackagePaths,     source: &ReadingExamSourceV2,     probe: &StudentProbeReportV2,     runtime_sha256: &str,     export_id: &str,     fault: Option<&str>, ) -> CommandResult<PackageReceipt>`
  - Captures had_exam/had_resources/had_manifest/had_report as four scalar bools into backup_root/state.json (L613-622) and closes over them in the `rollback` closure (L710-796). A batch needs those four bools PER exam, so this signature and the state file schema (NasBackupStateV1) both have to change.
- `PackagePaths` — `#[derive(Debug, Clone)] struct PackagePaths {     library_root: PathBuf,     reading_root: PathBuf,     manifest_path: PathBuf,     exam_path: PathBuf,     resource_path: PathBuf,     staging_root: PathBuf,     staging_exam_path: PathBuf,     staging_resource_path: PathBuf,     staging_manifest_path: PathBuf,     backup_root: PathBuf,     journal_path: PathBuf,     report_path: PathBuf,     lock_path: PathBuf,     lock_metadata_path: PathBuf,     base_manifest_sha256: String, }`
  - lock_path/lock_metadata_path/manifest_path/library_root/reading_root are per-LIBRARY (shared, batch-friendly). exam_path/resource_path/staging_exam_path/staging_resource_path/backup_root/journal_path/report_path are per-EXAM (must become per-item collections).

### 计划断言复核

| 判定 | 计划说 | 代码实际 | 证据 |
|---|---|---|---|
| confirmed | 13.3: the publish gate keeps only business closure and drops 'requires every group manually Confirmed', 'permanently blocked because a fallback appeared in history', 'requires a complete SourceReviewV1 file', and 'scan the whole authoring/pipeline JSON for any partial/fallback string'. | All four are still live and all four are enforced INSIDE the NAS publisher, not just at export. authoring_v2_commands.rs L253-259 blocks on quality.hard_failures; L280-283 requires audit.humanVerified == true; L286-307 requires a SourceReviewV1 file that is present, non-stale, resolved and issue-free; L309-336 runs collect_publish_gate_markers over the authoring IR plus authoring-ir.json and pipeline-report.json, matching key names (aifallback/llmfallback/fallbackused/partialfailure/...), non-empty arrays (failures/blockedautoapplygroups/lowconfidencegroups), status strings (partial/needs_review/auto_apply_blocked) and even free-text substrings ('ai fallback', 'rust-local-fallback', 'partial failure') in note/audit paths. nas_package_v2.rs L202 calls this whole thing again, so relaxing it only at export changes nothing. | `F:/workspace/PDF2Test/src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs:253-336 and F:/workspace/PDF2Test/src-tauri/src/nas_package_v2.rs:202-205` |

Moving to 'already passed preflight' means deleting validate_v2_export_binding's trust chain, which is currently the ONLY thing preventing an arbitrary JSON file that happens to deserialize as ReadingExamSourceV2 from being published (the test publisher_rejects_valid_json_without_v2_export_binding pins this). | `F:/workspace/PDF2Test/src-tauri/src/nas_package_v2.rs:56-206, 245-284, 1567-1593` |

Concretely: (a) a new input carrying Vec<item> plus one library_root/overwrite policy, keeping the single-item shape as a one-element adapter so the existing command keeps working; (b) split PackagePaths into a shared part (library_root, reading_root, manifest_path, lock_path, lock_metadata_path, base_manifest_sha256, staging_root, backup_root, journal_path, report_path) and a per-item part (exam_path, resource_path, staging_exam_path, staging_resource_path) held in a Vec; (c) hoist the lock + recover_incomplete_transactions + base_manifest_sha256 capture + CAS to run ONCE for the whole batch (they are already per-library, so no path derivation changes); (d) loop asset-copy + wrapper + asset-manifest + probe per item into one staging root; (e) merge ALL items into the manifest map in a single load_existing_manifest -> insert*N -> write staging manifest.js; (f) rewrite commit_package to take Vec<PerItemCommitState> with per-item had_exam/had_resources bools and one shared had_manifest/had_report, and rewrite the rollback closure to unwind every item before restoring the manifest. | — |

If the batch publisher receives in-memory DS values instead of file paths, the equivalent guarantee has to be re-established inside the same process/transaction (e.g. compile from the persisted revision under the same lock and record the revision in the journal) rather than dropped.
