| low | `F:/workspace/PDF2Test/src-tauri/src/lib.rs::publish_nas_package_v2 (L1209-1212) and generate_handler (L1460)` | Register the batch command here and keep publish_nas_package_v2 as a thin single-item wrapper so scripts/verify-phase6-runtime.mjs:236 (which greps for 'async fn publish_nas_package_v2' and 'publish_nas_package_v2,') keeps passing. Note `root` here is app_data_dir, and the publisher needs it only for the job-artifact binding check - if 17.21 removes that check, the parameter becomes unused. | — |
| medium | `F:/workspace/PDF2Test/src-tauri/src/nas_package_v2.rs::post-publish status write (absent)` | Add the library-item status transition the V1 exporters perform. export_nas_library.rs:1511-1515 does update_job(... job.status = JobStatus::Exported; job.current_step = WorkflowStep::Export) plus cleanup_transient_job_artifacts per job; the V2 publisher does neither, so library_items.status never becomes 'published' through this path (mapping lives at library_commands.rs:160-167). Do this inside the batch commit's success path, after the manifest commit, and decide whether a partial batch leaves items unpublished. | — |

### 现有测试方式
All Rust tests are in-file #[cfg(test)] modules; there is no src-tauri/tests/ directory and no e2e spec files.

RUN COMMANDS (the CI gate uses exactly these, scripts/verify-phase6-runtime.mjs:257-258):
  cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --lib nas_package_v2 -- --nocapture
  cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --lib reading_runtime_v2 -- --nocapture
  cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --lib reading_source_v2
  npm run verify:phase6:runtime      (does npm run check + both cargo filters + JSON-schema and token assertions)
I did not run any of these - the task forbade cargo builds.

reading_source_v2.rs tests (L611-1056), 8 tests. Fixture helpers to copy: fixture() at L619 loads fixtures/golden/synthetic/ielts/early-approaches-authoring-v2.json via env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"); invalid_fixture_matrix() at L627 loads fixtures/golden/synthetic/ielts/reading-v2-invalid-fixture-matrix.json; option_answer(&["A"]) at L633 builds an unordered_set option answer.
  early_approaches_compiles_to_one_shared_runtime_response (L641) - the happy path; asserts question_order == ["q14","q15"], 5 bank options, one shared response.
  shared_response_scoring_is_order_independent (L681), shared_response_awards_one_point_for_one_correct_unique_label (L712), shared_response_rejects_duplicate_and_extra_labels_and_scores_zero_matches (L743) - score_response_group semantics.
  text_entry_response_and_text_answers_do_not_require_an_option_bank (L781).
  invalid_fixture_matrix_has_stable_runtime_issue_codes (L815) - THE pattern to extend for any new validation. It iterates the 33 cases in the JSON matrix, applies a named `mutation` to a freshly compiled runtime via a big match, and asserts the expected code appears in validate_reading_source_v2's output. Adding a check means adding a {id, mutation, expectedCode} row to the fixture AND a match arm here; the match ends in `unknown => panic!("unknown invalid fixture mutation: {unknown}")` so a fixture row without an arm fails loudly.
  invalid_fixture_matrix_unknown_enums_are_rejected_by_typed_deserialization (L1008) - the four unknown_* mutations are asserted at the serde layer, not the validator.
  writes_pr06_reading_source_v2_fixture (L1046) - #[ignore]d, writes the cross-repo fixture to $IELTS_READING_V2_FIXTURE_PATH.

nas_package_v2.rs tests (L1424-2039), 13 tests. Two helpers are the whole harness:
  temp_root() (L1431) - std::env::temp_dir()/ielts-phase6-package-{nanos}. Every test ends with `let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(root);` and there is no Drop guard, so a panicking test leaks a temp dir.
  test_export_bundle(root) (L1441) - THE fixture you must reuse for any batch test. It loads the golden authoring fixture, force-sets quality.state="ready" and quality.issues=[], compiles, writes authoring-ir-v2.json + reading-source-v2.json into <root>/export, writes the same authoring bytes to <root>/jobs/<jobId>/authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json (so the persisted-binding check passes at revision 0), calls validate_authoring_v2_publish_readiness to get a real proof, and hand-builds manifest-v2.json with the correct authoringSha256/runtimeSha256/publishProof. Returns (source_path, ReadingExamSourceV2). A batch test needs an N-item variant with distinct exam ids and job ids.
  Transaction/rollback tests, all driven by the `fault` input: package_publish_probes_before_commit_and_fault_rolls_back_old_manifest (L1604, fault=manifest_rename), partial_resource_backup_fault_restores_old_visible_package (L1641, fault=backup_resources), post_commit_report_failure_rolls_back_package_and_report (L1686, fault=report_write), v2_commit_fault_preserves_loadable_legacy_v1_manifest (L1730).
  Concurrency/CAS: package_publish_rejects_busy_lock_before_commit (L1784) - note it builds the lock path via make_paths(&library_root, &library_root, exam_id).lock_path, which is the clearest evidence that reading_root == library_root. commit_cas_rejects_manifest_changed_after_staging_baseline (L1819), manifest_cas_rejects_stale_writer (L1512).
  Recovery: recovery_restores_persistent_backup_after_manifest_commit_interruption (L1834), recovery_keeps_visible_package_when_no_backup_moves_completed (L1911), recovery_restores_partial_exam_backup_and_keeps_other_live_items (L1979). These hand-construct PackagePaths + journal + backup on disk and then call recover_incomplete_transactions directly - the pattern to copy for multi-item recovery.
  Policy units: package_path_policy_rejects_unsafe_relative_paths (L1498), runtime_version_policy_blocks_invalid_or_future_student_runtime (L1596), existing_manifest_parser_accepts_legacy_closure_wrapper (L1521), publish_normalizes_publish_child_to_one_transaction_root (L1541), publisher_rejects_valid_json_without_v2_export_binding (L1567).

reading_runtime_v2.rs tests (L344-462) cover safe_join_asset_path traversal rejection and the probe's asset verification; its temp_root() pre-creates an images/ subdir.

WHAT IS NOT COVERED (write these first for a batch change): no test publishes two different exams into one library root; no test asserts that a mid-batch failure leaves earlier items unpublished; no test covers two concurrent processes past the lock-busy check; no test covers a read-only destination; no test asserts the compiler rejects an empty prompt, an empty option, an empty answer value list, or a zero-slot exam.

### 意外发现
- nas_reading_exams_dir(library_root) returns the normalized library root ITSELF (export_nas_library.rs:174-176), so reading_root == library_root: manifest.js, {examId}.js and resources/{examId}/ all land directly in the folder the user picked, and .publish-control/ + publish/reports/ sit beside them. The sibling nas_publish_dir() which appends 'publish' is never used by this module, and normalize_nas_library_root actively STRIPS a trailing publish/ component - so passing <X>/publish writes to <X>.
- publishClient.ts passes the same directory as both exportDir and libraryRoot (L48-52), so every publish permanently leaves <NAS>/{examId}-r{rev}-{uuid}/authoring-ir-v2.json on the NAS - the full authoring IR including the answer key - next to the student-facing manifest.js. Nothing ever deletes it and each publish creates a new uuid-suffixed directory. The legacy ExportPage.tsx:236-243 does exactly the same, so this is pre-existing, not new.
- The publisher re-runs the ENTIRE authoring publish gate (nas_package_v2.rs:202 -> validate_authoring_v2_publish_readiness) and demands byte-exact Value equality with the publishProof baked into the export receipt. Relaxing the gate only in export_authoring_v2_core changes nothing, and any change to the proof's json! shape silently invalidates every export bundle already staged on a NAS. It also means an edit between export and publish surfaces as authoring_v2_export_blocked:revision_not_current:current=N:requested=M from the PUBLISH command - which publishClient.ts:31 mislabels as 'content still needs to be filled in'.
- validate_reading_source_v2 accepts an exam with ZERO answer slots: the length compare is 0==0 and every set comparison is empty==empty. The pinned JSON schema declares questionOrder minItems:1, so the Rust compiler will happily produce and the publisher will happily publish a package the contract schema rejects.
- An answer key entry of {"kind":"text","values":[]} or {"kind":"option","labels":[]} passes all three gates: the compiler's kind_matches only checks the variant (L358-368), quality.rs validate_answer_value only checks label membership and word limits (L2210-2253), and the publish gate only filters kind == "unresolved" (authoring_v2_commands.rs:211-224). Only the unordered_set branch demands one non-empty unique label per slot.
- Response kinds DiagramHotspot and Composite hit the `_ => true` arm at reading_source_v2.rs:367, so their answer-key kinds are never validated at all - including Unresolved.
- The probe's asset-closure walker matches only the JSON key "assetId" (reading_runtime_v2.rs:316), while quality.rs:2739 matches both "assetId" and "visualFallbackAssetId". A table with a visual fallback image missing from assets[] passes the publish probe but is flagged by the quality pass - two closure computations that disagree.
- The `fault` field is a live fault-injection hook on the shipped Tauri command (not #[cfg(test)]-gated) and is exposed in the TypeScript input type. Rust honours seven values (after_assets, after_source, before_manifest, report_write, committed_journal, backup_resources, manifest_rename); the TS type enumerates only four.
- The runtime JSON is never written into the NAS package. Only the {examId}.js IIFE wrapper (build_wrapper, export_artifacts.rs:33) and resources/{examId}/asset-manifest.json land there. runtime_bytes/runtime_sha256 are computed solely to populate the manifest checksum block, and scriptSha256 hashes the pretty-printed wrapper while runtimeSha256 hashes the canonicalized re-serialized struct - two different encodings of the same data.
- _meta.assetCount in the published manifest is the EXAM count, not an asset count: manifest.len() is read at L473 after _meta was removed at L470, so it counts exam entries. export_artifacts.rs:52-55 has the identical mislabel, so the V1 and V2 manifests are consistently wrong together.
- The rollback closure deliberately refuses to delete any visible file when validate_backup_contents reports a missing backup (L710-748). It restores only already-moved paths, returns backup_incomplete:missing=..., and LEAVES the journal and backup on disk for the next publish or app start to replay. This is why several error strings end in ';rollback=incomplete:...' - that suffix means the NAS is in a state that still needs a retry, and it is the one outcome a user must be told about.
- The manifest is COPY-verified into the backup (copy_file_verified, L1089) rather than renamed, specifically so a student loader never observes a missing manifest.js mid-publish - while exam.js, resources/ and report.json are renamed. That asymmetry is load-bearing and easy to break in a batch rewrite.
- load_existing_manifest uses a hand-written brace-balanced, quote-and-escape-aware JS scanner (extract_manifest_json_object, L965-1003) so it can read both the V2 assignment form and the legacy closure-wrapped V1 form. A batch publisher must keep this - the target NAS may still hold a V1 manifest.
- serde_json is pulled in without the preserve_order feature (src-tauri/Cargo.toml), so Map is a BTreeMap and to_string_pretty output is key-sorted and deterministic. The manifest CAS depends on that determinism.
- score_response_group (reading_source_v2.rs:482) has no production caller anywhere in the crate - it is referenced only from the test module in its own file. It is a scoring oracle for the JS student runtime, not shipped logic. copy_dir_recursive (nas_package_v2.rs:1186) is outright dead code, referenced only by its own recursive call.
- publish_nas_package_v2_core writes nothing to the DB or job store. The V1 exporters set job.status = JobStatus::Exported and run cleanup_transient_job_artifacts (export_nas_library.rs:1511-1526, export_pack.rs:181,278); the V2 path does neither, so library_items.status never reaches 'published' via the V2 publisher.
- NasPackageV2PublishResult.probe in TypeScript declares errors: string[] and warnings: string[], which the Rust StudentProbeReportV2 (deny_unknown_fields: schemaVersion, examId, passed, checkedAssetIds, referencedAssetIds, issues) does not have. devFallbackBackend.ts:3581 fabricates them to satisfy the type. Only ExportPage.tsx:512 reads probe.passed, so nothing breaks today.
- scripts/verify-phase6-runtime.mjs hard-requires src/pages/ExportPage.tsx and src/pages/StructuredAuthoringEditorV2.tsx to exist and to contain specific tokens including the literal 'const NAS_PACKAGE_V2_ENABLED = true'. Plan 16.9/16.10 retires both pages, so that gate breaks unless updated in the same commit.
- All 24 compiler messages are English sentences ('Response group must contain at least one slot.'), while every user-facing string in the new frontend is Chinese. A typed PublishBlocker.user_message needs a code -> Chinese-message table; the compiler messages cannot be surfaced directly.
- Two issue codes are emitted from two sites each with different target_id kinds: RUNTIME_SLOT_ASSIGNED_TWICE (slot_id at L231 vs response_group_id at L303) and RUNTIME_DISPLAY_MAP_MISMATCH (exam_id at L173 vs slot_id at L258). RUNTIME_SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED puts the version STRING in target_id (L141). All three will confuse a UI that focuses on target_id.

---

## authoring V2 editor session, patches, revisions, publish readiness (src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs + src-tauri/src/artifact_store.rs)
**数据流**：SESSION LOAD (get_authoring_v2). lib.rs:1008-1012 → get_authoring_v2_core (authoring_v2_commands.rs:54-57) → safe_job_dir (util.rs:44-47, validates job_id as a single safe path segment) → build_editor_session (711-723) → load_current_authoring (725-742). load_current_authoring is the whole story: recover_current_revision(root, job_id) reads jobs/<id>/authoring/current-revision.json; if `current.revision > 0` it reads jobs/<id>/authoring/revisions/<N>.json, validates it as IeltsAuthoringIRV2, and returns (value, N); otherwise it reads jobs/<id>/authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json (const AUTHORING_V2_SHADOW_FILE, line 34) and returns (value, 0), erroring `AUTHORING_V2_NOT_AVAILABLE:shadow_missing:<path>` if absent. build_editor_session then sets `source = if revision > 0 { "revision" } else { "shadow" }` (713) and attaches the full list_revision_records vector. There is NO fixture path in Rust: "fixture" is produced only by the browser dev fallback (src/services/devFallbackBackend.ts:2492, seeded from createPhase5Fixture for the literal jobId "phase5-editor-fixture"); the union type lives at src/types/authoring-editor-v2.ts:15. get_authoring_v2 does not create the artifact layout, but it is not strictly read-only — recover_current_revision rewrites current-revision.json when the pointer lags the newest meta record (artifact_store.rs:226-237).\n\nSAVE (apply_authoring_v2_patches). lib.rs:1024-1028 → apply_authoring_v2_patches_core (123-168). Deserializes ApplyAuthoringV2PatchesInput {jobId, baseRevision, patches}; rejects empty batches and batches >200; load_current_authoring; pre-checks `current_revision != input.base_revision` → `revision_conflict:current={}:base={}`; applies each patch in order through apply_patch's 19-op dispatch (848-875); mark_user_audit (839-846) sets audit.revision = base+1, source="user", humanVerified=false, updatedAt=now; refresh_quality_report (744-755) recomputes the whole quality report; validate_authoring (830-837) round-trips the document through IeltsAuthoringIRV2; append_revision persists. Returns the same session shape plus savedPatchCount. Note patches are applied to an in-memory clone and only the FINAL document is persisted — the .jsonl patch log is a side record, never replayed by any code.\n\nREVISION PERSISTENCE (artifact_store.rs:240-335). append_revision ensures the 12-directory layout, opens jobs/<id>/authoring/revision.lock and takes an exclusive fs2 advisory lock, then re-reads recover_current_revision UNDER the lock and re-checks `current.revision != base_revision` → `revision_conflict:current={}:base={}`. That second check is what actually makes concurrency safe; the pre-check in apply_authoring_v2_patches_core is only a fast path. Next revision = max(highest number found in revisions/ by filename scan, current.revision) + 1 — monotonic allocation quarantines a half-written N.json rather than overwriting it (test at 829-859). It then writes with create_new (fails if the file exists): revisions/<N>.json (canonical JSON), patches/<N>.jsonl (one canonical-JSON line per patch, only when patches is non-empty), revisions/<N>.meta.json (RevisionRecordV2 with parent_revision, source, artifact_sha256, patch_sha256), and finally atomically replaces authoring/current-revision.json. The lock releases on File drop, including on the early-return conflict path.\n\nREVISION_CONFLICT — three distinct emitters, same string prefix: (1) authoring_v2_commands.rs:135-140, save pre-check; (2) artifact_store.rs:261-266, authoritative check under the file lock; (3) authoring_v2_commands.rs:467-473, export when the caller passes an explicit `revision` that is not current. A fourth, differently-spelled conflict lives in the publish gate: `authoring_v2_export_blocked:revision_not_current:current=..:requested=..` (186-191). The frontend matches on the substring "revision_conflict" only (src/features/editor/useCanonicalEditor.ts:117).\n\nEXPORT (export_authoring_v2). lib.rs:1030-1034 → export_authoring_v2_core (449-614). Takes an exclusive lock on export-history/phase5-export.lock; load_current_authoring; optional requested-revision check; refresh_quality_report (mutates in memory, never persisted back to the revision file); typed deserialize; early `authoring_v2_export_blocked:quality_state=<state>` if not ready; validate_authoring_v2_publish_readiness → publish_proof; compile_reading_source_v2 → `authoring_v2_export_compile_blocked:<json issues>`; then a journalled staging commit into the caller-supplied absolute export_dir: writes .phase5-staging-<uuid>/{authoring-ir-v2.json, reading-source-v2.json, manifest-v2.json} plus hash-and-size-verified asset copies (materialize_authoring_assets, 640-691), and commits by renaming staging → <safeExamId>-r<revision>-<uuid>. A journal file export-history/phase5-v2-<uuid>.journal.json tracks staging/committed/failed, and a receipt is written to export-history/phase5-v2-<revision>-<uuid>.json via write_artifact_json. manifest-v2.json embeds the publishProof verbatim.\n\nASSET PREVIEW (resolve_authoring_asset_preview). lib.rs:1014-1022 → resolve_authoring_asset_preview_core (59-121). Canonicalizes the job dir, loads the current authoring via the same load_current_authoring, finds the AssetDescriptorV2 by asset_id, requires mime to start with "image/", resolves descriptor.relative_path through safe_asset_relative_path (616-638) and fs::canonicalize, re-checks starts_with(job_root) after canonicalization, then verifies byte_length AND sha256 against the manifest before returning a `data:<mime>;base64,<...>` URI. Fully manifest-closed: five distinct error codes for missing/unsupported/escaped/size-mismatch/hash-mismatch. Consumed by src/exam-canvas/ExamCanvas.tsx:36-43 through a module-level Map cache keyed `jobId:assetId` that is never invalidated.\n\nPUBLISH CHAIN. src/features/editor/ExamWorkspacePage.tsx:59-72 → publishItem (src/api/publishClient.ts:46-69) → getAuthoringV2, then exportAuthoringV2({jobId: itemId, exportDir: destination, revision: session.revision}), then exportNasPackageV2 → nas_package_v2::publish_nas_package_v2_core (245+) → validate_v2_export_binding (56-206), which re-reads the persisted shadow (revision 0) or revisions/<N>.json (revision >0), compares it to the exported authoring with `quality` stripped, recompiles the runtime and compares it to the exported reading-source-v2.json, then RE-RUNS validate_authoring_v2_publish_readiness and requires manifest.publishProof to equal the fresh proof exactly.

### 关键签名
- `get_authoring_v2_core` — `pub(crate) fn get_authoring_v2_core(root: &Path, job_id: &str) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - Line 54. Body is only `safe_job_dir(root, job_id)?; build_editor_session(root, job_id)`. CommandResult<T> = Result<T, String> (lib.rs:90).
- `resolve_authoring_asset_preview_core` — `pub(crate) fn resolve_authoring_asset_preview_core(     root: &Path,     job_id: &str,     asset_id: &str, ) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - Lines 59-63. Returns {assetId, mime, widthPx, heightPx, resourceUri: "data:<mime>;base64,..."}. Image-only; verifies byte_length and sha256 against the manifest and re-checks path containment after canonicalize.
- `apply_authoring_v2_patches_core` — `pub(crate) fn apply_authoring_v2_patches_core(root: &Path, input: Value) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - Line 123. Returns the session object plus `savedPatchCount`. Max 200 patches; empty batch rejected.
- `validate_authoring_v2_publish_readiness` — `pub(crate) fn validate_authoring_v2_publish_readiness(     root: &Path,     job_id: &str,     revision: u64,     authoring_value: &Value, ) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - Lines 174-179. Ok returns the AuthoringV2PublishProofV1 object {schemaVersion, jobId, revision, qualityState:"ready", humanVerified:true, sourceReview, unresolvedAnswers:[], unresolvedBlockingIssues:[], aiFallbacks:[], partialFailures:[]} (319-330). Called from export_authoring_v2_core:488 and nas_package_v2.rs:201.
- `export_authoring_v2_core` — `pub(crate) fn export_authoring_v2_core(root: &Path, input: Value) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - Line 449. Returns {receipt, history, outputDir, revision, examId}.
- `ApplyAuthoringV2PatchesInput` — `#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)] #[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")] pub(crate) struct ApplyAuthoringV2PatchesInput {     pub job_id: String,     pub base_revision: u64,     pub patches: Vec<Value>, }`
  - Lines 38-44. Patches are untyped Value and validated only by apply_patch's per-op code.
- `ExportAuthoringV2Input` — `#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)] #[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")] pub(crate) struct ExportAuthoringV2Input {     pub job_id: String,     pub export_dir: String,     pub revision: Option<u64>, }`
  - Lines 46-52. export_dir must be absolute (497-499).
- `AUTHORING_V2_SHADOW_FILE / DOCUMENT_V2_SHADOW_FILE / SESSION_SCHEMA_VERSION` — `pub(crate) const AUTHORING_V2_SHADOW_FILE: &str = "authoring-ir-v2.shadow.json"; const DOCUMENT_V2_SHADOW_FILE: &str = "document-ir-v2.shadow.json"; const SESSION_SCHEMA_VERSION: &str = "AuthoringEditorSessionV1";`
  - Lines 34-36. AUTHORING_V2_SHADOW_FILE duplicates ielts_grammar::SHADOW_ARTIFACT_FILE (mod.rs:56) — two consts, same literal.
- `build_editor_session (private)` — `fn build_editor_session(root: &Path, job_id: &str) -> CommandResult<Value>`
  - Lines 711-723. Emits {schemaVersion:"AuthoringEditorSessionV1", jobId, authoring, revision, source, revisions, v1FilesRemainReadable:true}; `source` is `if revision > 0 { "revision" } else { "shadow" }` — never "fixture".
- `load_current_authoring (private)` — `fn load_current_authoring(root: &Path, job_id: &str) -> CommandResult<(Value, u64)>`
  - Lines 725-742. The single shadow-vs-revision decision point, shared by all four commands.
- `refresh_quality_report (private)` — `fn refresh_quality_report(root: &Path, job_id: &str, authoring: &mut Value) -> CommandResult<()>`
  - Lines 744-755. Gates the physical shadow on physical_shadow_matches_authoring (757-778: schemaVersion==DocumentIRV2, jobId match, documentId==sourceDocumentId, at least one shared sourceFileId) and re-applies user resolutions via preserve_issue_resolutions (780-828).
- `collect_publish_gate_markers (private)` — `fn collect_publish_gate_markers(     value: &Value,     path: &str,     ai_fallbacks: &mut Vec<String>,     partial_failures: &mut Vec<String>, )`
  - Lines 333-437. `let lower_path = path.to_ascii_lowercase();` at 339 is the PARENT path used for all gate tests on that object's children.

### 计划断言复核

| 判定 | 计划说 | 代码实际 | 证据 |
|---|---|---|---|
| partly-wrong | §17.16 / §13.3: validate_authoring_v2_publish_readiness 递归扫描历史 JSON 中 fallback/partial 字符串 / 扫描整个 authoring/pipeline JSON 寻找任意字符串 partial/fallback | Half right, and the wrong half matters. It IS a recursive walk (collect_publish_gate_markers, 333-437) and it DOES read historical files — but exactly two named ones, `jobs/<id>/authoring-ir.json` and `jobs/<id>/pipeline-report.json` (line 295), plus the current DS labelled "authoring". It is not a directory crawl and not an arbitrary substring grep. It is key-name driven: boolean keys aifallback/llmfallback/fallbackused/partialfailure/aipartialfailure/requiresmanualquestionimport; bare `fallback` only when the parent path contains audit/llm/ai/suggestion/evidence; non-empty arrays named failures (under llm/ai paths) or blockedautoapplygroups/lowconfidencegroups (ungated); `status` in {partial, partial_failure, needs_review, auto_apply_blocked} under llm/ai/vision/cloud/quality/audit paths; `code == PARTIAL_RECOVERY_FAILURE` (ungated); `failure` non-empty under those same paths. Only the String arm does substring matching, and only for four literals ("ai fallback", "llm fallback", "rust-local-fallback", "deterministic-local-fallback") under paths containing note/audit, plus "partial failure" under audit. Note the path gates are substring tests on an accumulated dotted path, so an innocent segment can open a gate — e.g. pipeline-report.json.authoring.remainingReviewItems contains "ai" via "remaining". | `src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs:287-317 (call sites), 333-437 (the walker), 439-447 (json_value_is_nonempty)` |
| confirmed | §13.3: 因历史日志中出现过 fallback 就永久禁止发布 | Confirmed, and reachable through three ungated rules. (a) `requiresManualQuestionImport: true` is a non-optional serialized bool on the V1 QuestionDraftV1 and QuestionGroupDraftV1 written into authoring-ir.json — one manual-import group permanently yields `partial_failures=authoring-ir.json.groups[0].requiresManualQuestionImport`. (b) `blockedAutoApplyGroups` / `lowConfidenceGroups` non-empty in pipeline-report.json.llm. (c) `code == PARTIAL_RECOVERY_FAILURE` in the V1 audit issues. None of these can be cleared by editing the V2 DS. Mitigating detail the plan does not mention: minimize_process_artifacts_after_authoring deletes pipeline-report.json right after it is written (auto_pipeline.rs:2185-2186), so vector (b) only survives when developer retention is on or when run_cloud_review_core_with_gateway re-writes it at auto_pipeline.rs:2665 without a following minimize. | `src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs:295, 367-376, 393-397; src-tauri/src/authoring_pipeline.rs:197,221; src-tauri/src/authoring_review.rs:56; src-tauri/src/auto_pipeline.rs:2156,2185-2186,2665; src-tauri/src/cleanup.rs:124-139` |
| partly-wrong | §13.3: 从普通发布主线移除『要求所有题组手工逐个 Confirmed』 | There is no per-group confirmation gate in the V2 publish path — TaskGroupV2.review_state exists (schema/ielts_authoring_v2.rs:262-266) but is written once as "unreviewed" at shadow build (ielts_grammar/mod.rs:331) and is never read by the gate, never updated by any patch op. What actually exists is a single document-level `/audit/humanVerified == true` requirement that NO code path can satisfy: the shadow hardcodes false (mod.rs:369) and mark_user_audit forces false on every save (839-846). So the real defect is stricter and different from the plan's description: publish is unreachable, not merely tedious. (I could not execute cargo test to demonstrate this; the reasoning is from the write/read sites listed.) | `src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs:255-261 and 839-846; src-tauri/src/ielts_grammar/mod.rs:331,369; src-tauri/src/schema/ielts_authoring_v2.rs:262-266` |
| partly-wrong | §13.3: 从普通发布主线移除『要求保留完整 SourceReviewV1 文件』 | It does not require the file to exist. With no document-ir.json and no source-review.json, source_review_status synthesizes required=false → resolved=true and the gate passes. What it does require is that IF a saved source-review.json exists it must not say stale or unresolved, and that source_review_issues() is empty. The nasty part is order dependence: with document-ir.json present the review is recomputed from parser warnings and low-confidence blocks; once cleanup deletes document-ir.json, source_review_status returns the saved file verbatim, so a stale=true review is frozen permanently. | `src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs:263-285; src-tauri/src/source_review.rs:120-162, 206-311; src-tauri/src/cleanup.rs:124-139,184-198` |
| confirmed | §4.4: 当前 artifact_store.rs 会为每次保存建立不可变 artifact、meta 和 patch 文件；长期编辑会生成大量文件 | Exactly right, and quantifiable: 3 create_new files per debounced save (revisions/N.json, revisions/N.meta.json, patches/N.jsonl) plus an atomic rewrite of current-revision.json, at a 450ms debounce, with no pruning anywhere. The plan understates the cost though — it is not just file count. list_revision_records reads and parses every meta file, and runs twice per save and twice per session open, so save latency grows linearly with edit history. | `src-tauri/src/artifact_store.rs:268-321, 345-380; src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs:127-129,149-167,712,720; src/features/editor/useCanonicalEditor.ts:13` |
| wrong | §4.4: 数据库保存 canonical_ds_json + current_edit_version；新表 library_item_recovery_v1 / editor_journal_v1 | None of it exists. grep for library_item_recovery_v1, editor_journal_v1, canonical_ds_json, current_edit_version, edit_version across src-tauri/src returns nothing. The V2 canonical DS lives only on disk under jobs/<id>/; the DB's library_item_revisions.payload_json is documented as holding the V1 ReadingAuthoringIR / WritingJob body, and the eight tables are library_meta, exams, library_items, library_item_revisions, ingest_jobs, source_assets, publish_records, job_artifact_index. Nothing writes the V2 DS into SQLite. | `src-tauri/src/db.rs:79-190 (schema listing; library_item_revisions at 129-141); grep across src-tauri/src for the five identifiers returns no hits` |
| partly-wrong | §9.5: apply_editor_commands(itemId, baseVersion, commands) 在 db.transaction 中加载当前 DS、验证 baseVersion | No such command. The real surface is apply_authoring_v2_patches(input: {jobId, baseRevision, patches: Vec<Value>}) and there is no DB transaction — atomicity comes from an fs2 exclusive lock on jobs/<id>/authoring/revision.lock plus create_new file writes. The optimistic-concurrency semantics the plan wants are however already correct: baseRevision is re-verified under the lock, and the debounce/base-version/"已保存" frontend half is fully built (useCanonicalEditor.ts, which even cites §9.5 in its header comment) and already handles revision_conflict. | `src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs:38-44,123-168; src-tauri/src/artifact_store.rs:248-266; src/features/editor/useCanonicalEditor.ts:7-13,93-141` |

No patch-target list is threaded anywhere. It also skips refresh_group_quality, so per-task quality drifts stale after the first save. | `src-tauri/src/authoring_v2_commands.rs:744-755; src-tauri/src/ielts_grammar/quality.rs:27-56,164-180; src-tauri/src/ielts_grammar/mod.rs:377 and 2058 (refresh_group_quality is private and called only at shadow build)` |

| high | `F:\workspace\PDF2Test\src-tauri\src\authoring_v2_commands.rs::mark_user_audit (839-846) + validate_authoring_v2_publish_readiness humanVerified gate (255-261)` | HIGHEST PRIORITY — these two are mutually exclusive and make V2 publish unreachable. mark_user_audit unconditionally does `audit.insert("humanVerified".to_string(), json!(false));` on EVERY successful patch save; the publish gate requires `/audit/humanVerified == true`. No other code path in the repo writes true: the shadow builder hardcodes `"humanVerified": false` (ielts_grammar/mod.rs:369), no patch op can reach the `audit` object (find_object_mut_by_id matches only objects with an `id` field; is_safe_node_attribute (1894-1910) does not list humanVerified; find_object_by_any_id_mut matches only id/taskId/responseGroupId/slotId). So revision 0 (shadow) and every revision >0 are both blocked. Either delete the humanVerified gate (plan 13.3 says drop manual per-group confirmation) or add an explicit confirm command/patch op that sets it and that mark_user_audit does not clobber. | — |
| medium | `F:\workspace\PDF2Test\src-tauri\src\authoring_v2_commands.rs::collect_publish_gate_markers (333-437) and its call sites (287-317)` | Delete the historical-file scan. Nothing filters issues whose `details.resolution` is `resolved`/`ignored`, so the `resolveIssue` patch op can never move the document to ready, while validate_authoring_v2_publish_readiness (233-254) DOES honour those resolutions for its own blocker list. So deleting document-ir.json can freeze a stale=true review permanently. If both files are absent the synthesized review passes (required=false → resolved=true).
